# AILANG Changelog: Eval Harness, Extensions & Agent Loop (v0.18+)

> Current changelog (v0.18.0 onward). Earlier series: [v0.10–v0.17](v0.10-v0.17-bytecode-vm.md). Index: [CHANGELOG.md](../CHANGELOG.md).

## [Unreleased]

### Changed — Gemini standard-eval roster (July 2026 models) (2026-07-21/22)

Added three July-2026 Gemini models and reworked the `extended_suite` Gemini
slots. All decisions are standard-mode only; agent-mode exclusions are unchanged.
Final Gemini suite state: `benchmark_suite` = `gemini-3-1-pro`; `extended_suite`
= `gemini-3-1-pro`, `gemini-3-6-flash`, `gemini-3-flash`, `gemini-3-5-flash-lite`;
`dev_models` = `gemini-3-flash`.

- **`gemini-3-6-flash` takes the `extended_suite` Flash slot** over
  `gemini-3-5-flash`. Matched head-to-head (one command, thinking-inclusive):
  smoke 22/23 = 22/23, core 19/19 = 19/19, stretch 20/21 = 20/21 — tied on every
  tier release baselines run. Frontier at **N=3** (96 runs): 3.5 = 32/48 (66.7%),
  3.6 = 29/48 (60.4%); the 6-3 split across differing benchmarks is p≈0.25, not
  significant. 3.6 is newer, cheaper, and uses 28% fewer thinking tokens.
  ⚠️ The larger cost saving is a *frontier* number; on stretch the two are within
  3%, so for baselines this is ~cost-neutral — justified by recency + tier parity.
- **`gemini-3-5-flash` un-stranded**: it had drifted into *no suite at all*. Its
  v0.22.0 exclusion was agent-mode only (managed-agents caching → $1.79/run);
  standard mode was never affected. Now opt-in as the longitudinal comparison
  point after losing the slot to 3.6.
- **`gemini-3-5-flash-lite` added to `extended_suite` as a language-improvement
  floor tracker** (cheapest current-gen Gemini, $0.30/$2.50, zero thinking
  tokens). Baseline: smoke 21/23, core 14/19, stretch 9/21, frontier 1/16.
  Chosen for its headroom + trivial cost, not capability; it intentionally lowers
  the suite aggregate (read its score in isolation). Does not displace
  `gemini-3-flash`, a capable rung above it.
- **Fixed a dead `api_name`**: `gemini-3-1-flash-lite` pointed at
  `gemini-3.1-flash-lite-preview`, which now 404s (GA dropped the `-preview`
  suffix). Corrected after auditing every `provider: google` api_name.

⚠️ **Methodology note carried in `models.yml`**: frontier N=1 variance is ±2
(measured: `gemini-3-1-pro` 10/16 banked vs 12/16 fresh). Two earlier N=1 claims
made this session — "3.6 is 3 benchmarks worse" and "3.5 beats the flagship" —
were retracted after N=3 / matched-run checks. Do not rank models on frontier
gaps ≤2.

### Added — `@nomcp` annotation (hide a handler from the MCP tool surface only)

`serve-api` gains a parameterless `@nomcp` annotation for handlers that should be
reachable over HTTP — and present in the OpenAPI spec and the A2A agent card —
but NOT advertised as an agent-callable MCP tool. It closes the
docparse `getKeyUsage`/`requestHistory` MCP leak with a one-line annotation and
no HTTP behavior change.

- A `@route @nomcp` handler answers HTTP 200 and appears in `/api/_meta/openapi.json`,
  but is **absent** from MCP `tools/list`, and `tools/call` on its name errors as
  unregistered.
- Unlike `@noexpose` (which hides from HTTP + MCP and is reset by `@route`),
  `@nomcp` is an MCP-only exclusion carried on a new independent
  `ExportInfo.IsNoMCP` flag that is set **unconditionally** — so it survives the
  `@route` override in `routes.go`. `@noexpose` and `@nomcp` do not interact.
- Parser: `@nomcp` joins the `@route`/`@raw`/`@nowrap`/`@noexpose`/`@mcp_name`
  allowlist; unknown annotations still hard-error with `PAR_UNKNOWN_ATTRIBUTE`.
- Docs, both devtools prompt copies (+ compact), and
  `examples/runnable/serve_api_webhook.ail` (new `@nomcp` demo route) updated.

### Fixed — agent mode records reasoning tokens + finish_reason (cost was understated)

Agent-mode eval rows banked `reason_tokens` as 0 and `finish_reason` as empty
100% of the time (all 280 rows of the v0.30.0 agent baseline), so hidden
thinking — billed upstream at the output rate — was invisible in token totals,
and a run truncated mid-thought was indistinguishable from a capability gap.
This is the agent-side counterpart to `43333e7a8`, which fixed the same class of
loss on the standard path *after* the v0.30.0 standard baseline was banked.

The providers were never at fault. `internal/ai/gemini/generate.go` has mapped
`usageMetadata.thoughtsTokenCount` since v0.18.x, and opencode has parsed
`tokens.reasoning` and the `step_finish` `reason` for just as long — the fields
were simply never copied across the mapping boundary into the banked result.

- **`executor.Result` gains `ReasonTokens`**, contractually DISJOINT from
  `OutputTokens` so totals cannot double-count.
- **opencode**: sums `tokens.reasoning` across `step_finish` deltas and records
  the *last* step's finish reason (intermediate steps report `tool-calls`;
  `length` is the truncation signal that matters).
- **claude**: the headless result `subtype` now maps to `FinishReason`. It was
  already parsed for the success boolean and the failure reason discarded, so
  `error_max_turns` runs landed in the generic bucket — `max_turns` is already
  aliased to `step_exhausted` by `CategorizeAgentError`.
- **managed_agents**: thought tokens are no longer folded into `OutputTokens`.
  Cost is unchanged (still billed at the output rate over `output + reason`).
- **Banked totals**: `total_tokens` now includes reasoning. `cost_usd` is
  deliberately NOT recomputed in agent mode — agent CLIs report their own billed
  cost, which already includes thinking; deriving it would double-count.
- **Guards**: the executor→banked mapping is now a named
  `tokenUsageFromResult` function covered by a test, plus a reflection-based
  invariant asserting every `*Tokens` field on `executor.Result` has a
  `TokenUsage` counterpart — so the *next* token field cannot be silently
  dropped the way this one was.

Verified live end-to-end on both paths: standard (`gemini-3-flash`:
`reason_tokens: 673`, `finish_reason: "stop"`, cost matching
`input×rate + (output+reason)×rate` exactly) and agent (`opencode-or-glm-5-2`:
`reason_tokens: 106`, `finish_reason: "tool_calls"`, totals balancing).

Still uninstrumented: `codex` and `pi` never set `FinishReason` (pi parses
`stopReason` but its struct omits the field), and no CLI executor except
opencode/managed_agents reports a reasoning count. Cache tokens parsed by
opencode/codex remain dropped — deliberately left out of this change to avoid
shifting input-token accounting mid-experiment.

The affected v0.30.0 baseline is annotated in
`eval_results/baselines/v0.30.0/CAVEATS.md` and flagged machine-readably via
`cost_data_valid: false` in its `baseline.json`; its cost figures must not be
used for model ranking.

### Added — fmt-hook A/B toggle for weak-model eval (M-EVAL-FMT-WEAKMODEL-AB, M1+M2a)

Engineering + preregistration for the "does the `ailang fmt` PostToolUse hook
help a weak model author correct AILANG?" experiment. No language-surface
change; no GPU (the scored haiku runs are a later, rig-gated milestone).

- **New `-fmt-hook on|off` flag** on `eval-suite` (agent mode; default `off` =
  byte-identical to today's path). `on` writes a workspace `.claude/settings.json`
  registering the LANDED `scripts/hooks/format_ail.sh` as a `PostToolUse`
  Edit|Write hook so `ailang fmt --write` canonically formats every edited
  `.ail` file. Modeled on the `-microrag` A/B precedent: a `FmtHookMode` type
  (`ParseFmtHookMode` / `ResolvedState` / `Apply`) in
  `internal/eval_harness/fmt_hook_mode.go`, one `AgentBenchmarkConfig.FmtHook`
  field, wired through the active executor path (`RunAgentBenchmarkWithExecutor`)
  and both legacy runners. A missing LANDED hook in `on` mode fails loudly
  (no silent degrade to an untreated arm).
- **Resolved arm banked** as `fmt_hook_state` in the per-run result, so the
  required ON-vs-OFF config diff is reviewable from the banked JSON.
- **Hook-reality capture**: per-turn `format_ail.sh` status
  (`formatted`/`error`) captured from the executor tool-result stream into
  `fmt_hook_events`, for the treatment-delivery metric (was the ON arm actually
  treated, vs the ~8% fail-closed refusal baseline).
- **Preregistration** (`design_docs/planned/v0_31_0/m-eval-fmt-weakmodel-ab-prereg.md`)
  freezing the 6-benchmark matched set, N=5/arm/bench, `claude-haiku-4-5`,
  budgets/timeouts/tools, metric definitions (pass-rate delta with Wilson CIs,
  convergence, hook-reality), and the refutation threshold — before any scored run.

### Added — eval harness memory watchdog: generated code can no longer OOM the host (M-EVAL-MEM-GUARD)

The 2026-07-20 rig kernel panic: the eval harness executed model-generated
benchmark programs with NO memory limit, and three generated Python processes
reached ~80–120GB each (the local-model rotation runs with `--timeout 1500s`,
so a fast allocator had 25 minutes to exhaust RAM → watchdogd timeout panic).
macOS does not reliably enforce `RLIMIT_AS`, so the fix is a polling watchdog:

- **`AILANG_EVAL_MAX_RSS`** (default **8G**, `off` disables): every
  generated-code execution lane now samples its child's **process-group** RSS
  every 2s and SIGKILLs the whole group on breach — group-level, so wrapper
  children die too (`uv run` → python, `go run` → compiled binary). An
  unparseable value fails the run loudly (no silent fallback).
- **New `error_category: "resource_limit"`** + `RES_LIMIT` repair hint: a
  memory-killed run banks as a model failure (unbounded allocation) in eval
  reports instead of crashing the host or masquerading as `runtime_error`.
  Wired through standard mode (`populateMetrics`), agent mode
  (`CategorizeRunError` promotion off the `[resource_limit]` stderr marker),
  and the repair loop.
- **Systemic refactor**: the six copy-pasted start/wait/timeout blocks
  (Python, AILANG, JS/Go, MoonBit, Aver, `averCheckDiagnostics`) now share one
  guarded helper (`runGuarded`/`waitWithGuards` in
  `internal/eval_harness/memlimit.go`), so the guard set (process group,
  output limits, timeout, memory cap) cannot drift per-lane again.
- **Closed two fully unguarded agent-mode lanes** found in the audit:
  `runPythonSolution` validated model Python via `CombinedOutput()` with no
  timeout/process-group/cap at all (now guarded, 30s, graded on stdout only —
  matching the standard lane), and `gradeInWorkspace`'s probe run had no
  process group or memory cap. `RunAICheck` (type-check + Z3 over model
  source) is also guarded now. Out of scope by design: opencode/codex/claude
  CLI executors manage their own children.

Tests: deliberate ~300MB allocator killed in ~2s under a 64M cap, including a
grandchild-allocator case (the uv shape) asserting the whole tree dies.

### Added — `ailang fmt`: stable multi-line let-chain comment boundaries (M-AILANG-FMT-INLINE-INTERIOR)

`ailang fmt` Phase 2 previously fails-closed (exit 2, no output) whenever a comment
landed strictly inside a multi-line `let … in` chain, because the printer collapsed
the chain onto one inline line (`let x = v in let y = w in tail`) so a between-binding
comment had no stable boundary. That was the largest coherent refusal class:
**28 of 59 corpus refusals**.

This sprint adds a **printer-local conditional multi-line let-chain emitter** (design
Decision 1, option (a); parser/AST untouched):

- `internal/format/letchain.go` — a shared maximal-chain flattener (`flattenLetChain`)
  used by both attachment and emission, treating any `*ast.Let` with a non-nil `Body`
  as a chain root (including length-2 `let x = v in tail`).
- `internal/format/attach.go` — registers one child list owned by the root `*ast.Let`
  with explicit non-overlapping child spans (binding end = `subtreeEnd(binding.Value)`;
  the `in` has no AST position so it rides the separator). Only maximal chains register
  (no overlapping suffixes). A braced block whose sole expression is a let-chain no
  longer registers a competing one-child list.
- `internal/format/expr.go` + `decl.go` + `format.go` — `letIn` emits multi-line ONLY
  when the root let owns a comment attachment (`hasAnyAttachment`), interleaving the
  comments at their boundaries and writing `in` before a trailing comment so a `--`
  cannot swallow it. Comment-free and unattached input keep the exact old inline bytes.

Invariants preserved: `fmt(fmt(x)) == fmt(x)` (layout chosen from reconstructed
attachment ownership, never source line count), never-lossy (marker count preserved),
comment-free byte identity, and the strict-interior fail-closed guard for every
unsupported interior (unchanged — Decision 2).

**Result** (corpus gate, `go test ./internal/format/ -run TestCorpusCommentGate`):
comment-unattached refusals **59 → 32** (15.28% → 8.29%), the **let-chain-interior
refusal class is eliminated (0)**, with `marker-fail=0`, `PHASE2-rt-regression=0`, and
`preexisting-Phase1-rt-bug=0`. 27 of the 28 let-chain files now format losslessly; the
28th (`records.ail`) carries an additional deferred footer comment and stays fail-closed
on that comment (no-enclosing-list class, out of scope). The corpus refusal counter is
split into truthful construct classes (let-chain-interior / non-let-equation-body /
inline-tests-list / no-enclosing-list / other-interior).

### Fixed — motoko executor: stderr-grounded error attribution on crash-shaped failures (M-RIG-RELIABILITY follow-up)

The 2026-07-16→20 incident: motoko died at STARTUP with an AILANG compile error
(v0.30.0's `std/ai.Message` gained an `images` field; motoko core literals were
4-field), but the executor reported only the downstream symptoms — "SYSTEM
PROMPT NOT DELIVERED … recurring delivery regression" and "terminated without
emitting run_summary (likely crash)" — while the actual compile error sat fully
captured in the per-task stderr log (`$TMPDIR/motoko-stderr-<session>.log`,
previously surfaced only under `DEBUG_AGENT`). Triage chased the historical
env-forward delivery-regression class for days.

Systemic fix (`internal/executor/motoko/motoko.go`): a single `attachStderrTail`
helper appends the last ~1KB of captured subprocess stderr + the on-disk log
path to `Result.Error` on **every** no-authoritative-run_summary failure path —
session JSONL missing (startup crash before the logger initializes), JSONL
unparseable, and parsed-but-crashed (no run_summary, mid-run crash, terminal
error event) including when no system prompt is configured so the delivery
guard never runs. The guard's startup-crash verdict (which already embeds the
tail, landed in 5afd79cda) is deduplicated against the central attachment.
Covered by four new tests including the incident shape: mock motoko exits
nonzero before writing JSONL → `Error` carries the stderr tail.

### Fixed — OS eval publishing: mixed-version attribution + version-bank destruction

Found while computing the first matched v0.29.2→v0.30.0 comparison: the published
"per-release" local-rig numbers were not per-release at all.

- **Mixed-version pooling**: `eval-publish` loads `summary.json` files RECURSIVELY
  under `--rotation`, so publishing from the rolling ROOT pooled every version's
  summaries (plus a stale June root summary) into rows attributed to one release —
  the v0.30.0 history entry and the "Current data" table were multi-version data.
  All publish paths (release snapshot, rotation filler, nightly lang-eval) now
  publish from the per-version bank dir only, with `--summarize` (new flag:
  regenerate the bank dir's summary from raw results — eval-suite only finalizes
  one when a suite completes) and `--ailang-version` (new flag: pin attribution
  when the checkout has moved past the published version). The old silent
  root-accumulator fallback is removed: the snapshot now fails loudly instead.
- **Version banks are now permanent**: `os-release-snapshot.sh --reset` deleted
  active-model files from the ENTIRE rolling tree including the per-version bank
  dirs (this destroyed v0.29.2's raw per-run files on release day); the reset now
  prunes `v*/` dirs and clears only the root accumulator working set.
- **Purged 6 poisoned v0.30.0 motoko results** (harness startup crashes from the
  std/ai.Message regression, zero model attempts) so the rotation re-measures
  them instead of freezing crashes into the release record via `--skip-existing`.

### Fixed — `ailang fmt` contract-clause round-trip + silent contract-deletion data loss (m-fmt-properties-printer-roundtrip)

Makes the entire Z3-verified contract corpus formatter-eligible and closes a
latent data-loss bug where `fmt --write` could **silently delete verified
`requires`/`ensures` clauses**.

- **Contract-clause emission (`internal/format/decl.go`)** — the Phase-1 printer now
  treats `FuncDecl.Properties` as a mixed-kind slice: `RequiresKind`/`EnsuresKind`
  entries are emitted as merged `requires { … }` / `ensures { … }` clauses in
  signature position (after the effect row, before tests/properties and the body),
  and only `PropertyKind` (forall) entries route through the `properties [...]`
  block. Previously every `Properties` entry was printed as a bare `properties`
  predicate, which the parser rejects (`expected forall in property`), so every file
  using `requires`/`ensures` failed `fmt --check` with exit 2 (fail-closed). 30
  contract examples (`examples/runnable/contracts/*.ail` + `discount_calculator.ail`)
  are now formattable and have been reformatted.
- **Silent contract-deletion fix (`internal/parser/parser_func.go`)** — the
  properties-block parse used assignment (`fn.Properties = …`), clobbering the
  already-parsed `requires`/`ensures` entries whenever a function carried BOTH
  contracts and a `properties [...]` block. `fmt --write` would then exit 0 while
  **destroying the verified contract** (the loss happened at parse time, before the
  fail-closed round-trip check could see it). Now appends, preserving contracts.
- **Two adjacent Phase-1 printer round-trip bugs** the contract corpus exposed, fixed
  so the corpus gate reaches zero: `startsWithStatementStarter` ignored
  precedence-driven parenthesisation (a statement rendered `(a + b) * c` got no `;`
  separator and re-parsed as a call); and `@verify(depth: N)` dropped the `depth:`
  key at parse, so the printer emitted `@verify(N)` which failed to re-parse.
- **Regression guard** — new acceptance-gated `TestCombinedContractsAndPropertiesPipeline`
  locks the combined contracts+properties case end-to-end (contracts reach
  `core.DeclMeta.Contracts` and Z3 verification, the forall reaches only the property
  pipeline, exact-count no-duplication assertions, fmt round-trip preserves the
  `requires` clause). `TestCorpusCommentGate` is hardened: `preexisting-Phase1-rt-bug`
  dropped 28 → 0 and any future non-zero count now FAILS the test.

### Fixed — motoko system-prompt guard no longer misfires on startup crashes

The END-TO-END system-prompt delivery guard (M-RIG-RELIABILITY) warned "SYSTEM
PROMPT NOT DELIVERED … recurring delivery regression" whenever
`ProviderData["system_md"] != "set"` — but that signal comes from motoko's
step-0 `runtime_config_resolved` broadcast, so ANY crash before step 0 (e.g.
the 2026-07-16 v0.30.0 `std/ai` Message `images` schema break that crash-looped
the rig for 4 days) produced the same warning and misdirected diagnosis toward
the env-forward delivery class.

- **`guardSystemPromptDelivery`** (extracted to
  `internal/executor/motoko/system_prompt_guard.go`) now distinguishes the two:
  a session JSONL with no step-0 broadcast, no `run_summary`, and no executed
  steps reports "motoko crashed at startup before step 0" with the stderr log
  path (`$TMPDIR/motoko-stderr-<session>.log`) and the stderr tail folded into
  `Result.Error` + `ProviderData["motoko_startup_crash"]`. Sessions that
  actually ran steps with `system_md != "set"` keep the loud
  delivery-regression warning.
- Parser now exposes `ProviderData["motoko_run_summary_present"]` so the guard
  (and other consumers) can tell crash-before-step-0 from ran-without-teaching.
- Unit tests cover all four verdicts: delivered, startup crash, delivery
  regression, and mid-run crash (steps ran, no broadcast → still the delivery
  class).
### Added — ELO-driven benchmark prioritization for the local-rig rotation (M-EVAL-ELO-PRIORITY-ROTATION)

The os-rotation filler now orders each per-version lap by discriminative signal
instead of `ls` order: non-saturated (and unrated) benchmarks bank FIRST at full
trials; saturated benchmarks (ELO band "Trivial" in the published ratings) run as
a cheap once-per-version re-confirmation tail at `OS_FILLER_SAT_TRIALS` (default 1)
— reordered, never skipped, so a regression can still un-saturate them at the next
fit. Design: `design_docs/planned/m-eval-elo-priority-rotation.md`.

- **`tools/eval-signal-set.sh`** (new) — canonical reader of the saturated set from
  the unified dashboard JSON (`ratings.<mode>[.byLang.<lang>].benchmarks[]` in
  `docs/static/benchmarks/latest.json`, which already blends cloud + local trials
  via `eval-report --merge`). Fail-open contract: any data problem emits an empty
  set + stderr note and exits 0, so schedulers degrade to today's behavior.
- **`tools/launchd/os-rotation-filler.sh`** — signal-first/saturated-tail partition
  for both the AILANG-first pass (prefers the `byLang.ailang` fit) and the
  cross-language pass; boundary chunks split into per-group `eval-suite` calls so
  trial count follows the benchmark. Kill switch: `OS_FILLER_ELO_PRIORITY=0`.
  Cursor, lap-marker, `--skip-existing`, and per-version banking semantics unchanged.

### Added — OS leaderboard release evolution + automatic release pickup

Answers "does each AILANG release move the needle for local models?" directly on
the website, and removes the last manual step between cutting a release and the
local-rig trend reflecting it.

- **`OSReleaseTrend` component** (docs site) — "Evolution across releases" section on
  the OS/Local-model leaderboard page, charting per-tier AILANG pass rate and the
  AILANG−Python gap (the language-improvement control metric) per release from
  `os/history.json`, with per-release coverage caveats (N trials, tiers not run) so
  denominator changes aren't mistaken for performance swings. Saturated (smoke) and
  mixed-coverage (overall) views stay selectable but are labeled as non-signal.
- **OSLocalLeaderboard header** now leads with the AILANG release the runs executed
  against (`AILANG v0.30.0 · rotation rolling-YYYYMMDD`) instead of only the
  rotation snapshot tag.
- **os-rotation-filler release pickup (step 3b)** — the rig detects a `std/VERSION`
  bump on origin/dev within one cycle, pulls, reinstalls `ailang`, finalizes the
  outgoing release's history entry (`os-release-snapshot.sh <old> --reset`), and
  starts banking fresh under the new version — previously a manual post-release
  skill step (now documented as fallback only).

## [v0.30.0] - 2026-07-19

### Added — Architecture boundary enforcement (M-ARCH-BOUNDARIES Phases 1–3)

Formalizes the logical core/dashboard separation with a mechanical CI gate, over
the current `internal/` layout (no physical restructure — Phase 4 `git mv` is
deferred to the v1.0→v1.1 boundary). Lets the v1.0 stability promise scope to the
compiler core.

- **`scripts/check_boundaries.sh`** — self-testing import-boundary gate. Rule 1: no
  core package imports a dashboard package. Rule 2: no dashboard package imports the
  compiler surface (`parser`/`types`/`core`/`elaborate`/`pipeline`) directly — it must
  go through the sanctioned `internal/embed` bridge. `eval` is excluded from Rule 2's
  deny-list because `embed`'s public API forces callers to name `eval.Value` (documented
  exception, not a behavioral dependency). Anchored on quoted Go import paths with a
  MODULE-vs-`go.mod` drift guard (exit 2) so a wrong anchor can't silently false-pass.
- **`make check-boundaries`** target + a CI gate step (after `check-file-sizes`).
- **`ARCHITECTURE.md`** boundary section + **`CLAUDE.md`** agent-boundary guidance +
  **`.github/CODEOWNERS`** path-based ownership on the real `internal/` layout.

### Added — `ailang fmt`: lossless comment preservation (M-AILANG-FMT-PHASE2)

Phase 2 makes `ailang fmt` preserve comments losslessly, unblocking the formatter
on the ~94.7% of the example corpus Phase 1 refused. Every input comment appears
in the output exactly once, deterministically placed; the AST grammar is
unchanged. The Phase-1 blanket comment-refusal is **removed**.

- **Lossless comment collector + literal-region map** (`internal/lexer/comment_scan.go`)
  — `ScanForComment` (boolean) is joined by `CollectComments(source) -> ([]Comment
  with byte-exact spans + kinds, []LiteralRegion)`. `skipString` is now
  interpolation-nesting-aware: `${...}` hole interiors are scanned at code level, so
  a comment inside a hole is reported as a real comment outside every literal region
  (the fail-closed carve-out signal). The parser-visible token stream is byte-for-byte
  unchanged; `ScanForComment` delegates to the collector so they cannot drift.
- **Token-anchored envelope** (`internal/format/envelope.go`) — line-start table +
  rune-walk `(line,column)->byte` conversion (column = 1-based rune index into
  NFC-normalized source), literal-region clamping, byte-level bracket matching,
  `MinAnchor` (leftmost subtree token, recovering `x` from an operator-positioned
  `x + 42`), and `WidenLeft` with a **hard left wall** (widening stops at the nearest
  enclosing-list open delimiter; absorbs `export`/`pure`/`extern`). AST `Span` fields
  are never used (measured unusable). A permanent premise-sweep test enforces
  conversion exactness corpus-wide (393 files, 81,224 tokens).
- **Deterministic attachment** (`internal/format/attach.go`) — rules 1–5
  (trailing / leading / floating / file-boundary / grouping) over the printer's
  multi-line ordered child lists (file top-level, blocks, match arms). Totality:
  every comment attaches to exactly one boundary or the file **fails closed** — never
  a silent drop or relocation.
- **Fail-closed carve-outs** — a comment inside a `${...}` interpolation hole, or
  interior to an expression the printer emits inline (dominantly collapsing
  `let ... in` chains), is refused fail-closed (exit 2, byte-identical). On the
  corpus: interpolation-comment rate 0.00%; inline-interior rate 15.28%; the
  remaining ~85% format losslessly and idempotently.
- **Exit-code split** (`cmd/ailang/fmt.go`) — **3 = input parse error** (mid-edit,
  not-yet-parseable; tooling defers), **2 = operational error** (envelope/attachment
  fail-closed, round-trip defect, usage, read, print, write). `fmt --help` and
  `docs/docs/reference/formatter.md` updated.
- **Gates** — marker property test (unique markers, exactly-once, idempotence),
  386-parse-valid corpus gate (0 Phase-2 defects, 0 comment loss), structural
  round-trip. Note: 28 corpus round-trip failures are pre-existing Phase-1
  `properties[...]` printer bugs (fail comment-free too), caught fail-closed.

Design doc: `design_docs/planned/v0_30_0/m-ailang-fmt-phase2.md`.

### Added — `ailang fmt` adoption: discoverability + opt-in harness hooks (M-AILANG-FMT-ADOPTION)

Now that Phase 2 makes `ailang fmt` lossless, this closes the adoption gap so AI
authors and the harness actually reach for it. No parser/type/codegen/runtime change
— prompts, docs, build-glue and opt-in hooks only.

- **Teaching prompt v0.16.3** (`prompts/v0.16.3.md`, registered append-only in
  `prompts/versions.json` and flipped `active`) — a byte-identical copy of v0.16.2 plus
  a short Formatting section (command usage only, no code snippet) so the prompt now
  tells the model `ailang fmt` exists. Old versions still serve byte-identical
  (`ailang prompt --version v0.16.2` is unchanged).
- **Adoption docs** — `docs/docs/reference/formatter.md` gains an Adoption section: the
  exit-code contract table (0=ok · 1=`--check` drift · 2=operational · 3=parse), the
  five contract clauses (idempotence, meaning-preservation, atomicity,
  non-blocking+non-silent, never-format-non-parsing), the `make fmt-check-ail`
  description, a copy-paste Claude Code PostToolUse hook config, and the Motoko
  per-edit cross-repo contract (text only). Cross-linked from the development-workflow
  guide.
- **`make fmt-check-ail`** (`make/code-health.mk`) — standalone AILANG drift check over
  `examples/**` + `stdlib/**`; informational, **not** wired into `make ci` (which is
  byte-identical before/after). Named `-ail` to avoid the pre-existing Go `fmt-check`
  gofmt gate. Baseline at introduction: 393 `.ail` files → 31 canonical, 268 drift,
  87 comment-refusals (exit 2), 7 parse errors (no mass reformat performed).
- **Opt-in PostToolUse hook** (`scripts/hooks/format_ail.sh`, registered opt-in in
  `.claude/settings.json`) — non-blocking, non-silent, bounded auto-format on
  Edit/Write of `.ail` files. Carries a SIGTERM→grace→SIGKILL escalation so a
  SIGTERM-ignoring `ailang` cannot wedge the turn; never suppresses `ailang fmt`
  stderr; silent-defer pinned to exit 3 (non-parsing input).

Design doc: `design_docs/implemented/v0_30_0/m-ailang-fmt-adoption.md`.

### Fixed — `expected_fail` example audit closed as ghosts; examples promoted to runnable (M-DX-EXPECTED-FAIL-FIXES)

A Gate-2 live-repro found the four "parser/runtime bugs" catalogued in the
`examples/expected_fail/` audit were ghosts — 0 of 4 required a language fix
(non-canonical `\x ->` lambda arrows, mergeable `requires` blocks, a missing
statement separator + deprecated `++`, and a `--caps`-after-filename repro error).
The affected examples were corrected to canonical syntax and **promoted to
`examples/runnable/`** (including `runnable/contracts/`), `examples/manifest.json`
was de-drifted (two mispathed `contracts/` entries), and the `expected_fail/README`
corrected. The one real residue — the block-RHS `let` statement-separator
inconsistency — is split out to `m-parser-block-let-separator` (planned/v0_30_0).
Zero Go changes. Design doc: `design_docs/implemented/v0_30_0/m-dx-expected-fail-fixes.md`.

### Added — `ailang fmt`: canonical AILANG source formatter (M-AILANG-FMT, Phase 1)

A new `ailang fmt [--write] [--check] <files...>` subcommand collapses AILANG's
equivalent statement-sequence spellings (equation `;`, braced `;`, braced newline)
into one stable canonical form. It is a presentation-only convenience, not a
correctness gate: `Parse(fmt(x))` is structurally identical to `Parse(x)` for every
accepted file.

- **New `internal/format` package** — an exhaustive AST-to-source printer over every
  concrete declaration, expression, type, pattern, effect, test, and property node.
  Public API: `Source(program, Options) ([]byte, error)` and `HasComments(source)
  (bool, error)`. Unknown / nil-required / `ast.Error` nodes and malformed literals
  fail loudly — there is **no** debug `String()` fallback. Parentheses are
  reconstructed from parent precedence (the AST has no `ParenExpr` node); redundant
  source parentheses are dropped. Cons `::` is re-emitted infix (it has no
  prefix/call re-parse). A single `;` is inserted only where a following block
  statement begins with a non-starter token (e.g. a bare `()` or tuple) that the
  parser would otherwise glue onto the previous statement — keeping the common case
  semicolon-free while preserving round-trip.
- **Canonical form** — braced newline-per-statement blocks with a bare final
  expression, single-expression equation bodies as `= expr`, explicit `let ... in`
  preserved, two-space indent, LF, one final newline, one blank line between
  top-level declarations, and original AST order (no reordering of imports, symbols,
  cases, fields, or declarations).
- **CLI** (`cmd/ailang/fmt.go`) — stdout (exactly one file), `--write` (1+ files),
  `--check` (1+ files), `--write`⊕`--check` mutual exclusion. `--write` validates
  every input in memory (comment preflight + parse + print + round-trip) before
  touching any file, then replaces each changed file atomically (same-directory temp
  + `os.Rename`, preserving mode). Fail-closed exit codes: 0 success / all-canonical,
  1 `--check` drift, 2 usage/read/comment/parse/print/round-trip/write error.
- **Comment safety (Phase 1)** — files containing comments are refused (exit 2) and
  left byte-identical; the formatter never deletes a comment. Detection is a lossless
  lexical scan (opt-in `internal/lexer` comment scan that leaves `NextToken()`
  unchanged) that distinguishes real `--`/`//` introducers from comment-like text in
  strings, char literals, regex literals, and quasiquote templates. Lossless comment
  preservation is a separately-scheduled Phase 2.
- **Release-gate tests** — idempotence (`fmt(fmt(x)) == fmt(x)`) and structural
  round-trip (`Parse(fmt(x)) == Parse(x)`, go-cmp ignoring pos/span) over every
  comment-free `examples/**/*.ail` (21 files) plus generated cases; the three
  separator spellings format identically; explicit `let ... in` stays explicit.
  `internal/ast/print.go` and its JSON goldens are untouched. Docs:
  `docs/docs/reference/formatter.md`.

### Added — Module-placement diagnostics: `MOD002` wired + `PAR_MODULE_PLACEMENT` (M-PROMPT-FOOTGUNS Part A)

Multiple/misplaced `module` declarations in one file (a top-2 fleet-tier compile footgun — models
simulating Python/JS/Go multi-file layouts inside the single allowed file) used to produce the
opaque `PAR_NO_PREFIX_PARSE: unexpected token in expression: module` + `PAR_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`
cascade that never stated the one-module-per-file rule. A decl-level `module` token now routes to a
coded, directional, fix-carrying parse diagnostic (mirroring `PAR_IMPORT_PLACEMENT` / #325):

- **`MOD002` (duplicate) wired** (`internal/parser/parser_file.go`, `parser_decl.go`). The `MOD002`
  code was defined, registered, and published in `dist/error_codes.json` since forever but had
  **zero emission sites** — it is now live. A second `module` after a valid leading module emits
  `MOD002` naming both module paths + the first module's position, plus two teaching suggestions
  (one-module-per-file, split into separate .ail files).
- **`PAR_MODULE_PLACEMENT` (misplaced) added.** A module-less file with a late `module`
  declaration (module isn't first) emits `PAR_MODULE_PLACEMENT` — distinct from a duplicate, so the
  registry stays truthful.
- **Error-recovery state-isolation (gemini's fix).** Parser fields
  `seenModule`/`firstModulePos`/`firstModulePath` are set **only** by `ParseFile`'s valid
  leading-module branch, never by the recovery path. So a module-less file with two late modules
  emits `PAR_MODULE_PLACEMENT` for **both** (the second is never a false `MOD002` referencing a
  non-existent "first module").
- **Cascade killed.** No `PAR_NO_PREFIX_PARSE`/`PAR_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` for the module token; exactly
  one diagnostic per offending declaration.
- **Stale comment fixed.** `MOD011`'s "multiple module declarations per file" comment/summary was
  stale — corrected to "cross-file module-name collision" (its actual, untouched behavior).
- **Tests.** `internal/parser/module_placement_test.go` (duplicate→`MOD002`+no-cascade;
  misplaced→`PAR_MODULE_PLACEMENT`; 3 modules→2 errors; two-late-modules state-isolation;
  leading-only clean; malformed-duplicate→one truncated error) + footgun contract rows
  `duplicate_module`/`misplaced_module` in `internal/diag/footgun_fixtures_test.go` and the
  `footguns.md` coverage table.

### Added — `split_map_join.ail` runnable regression guard (M-PROMPT-FOOTGUNS Part B, ghost-close)

`m-prompt-split-list-operations` was a **ghost**: the embedded prompt (v0.16.2) already teaches
`split(s, delim) -> [string]` and the split→map→join pipeline (lines ~704/715/1071-1085). Closed as
a ghost with a durable, CI-gated guard so a future prompt-diet pass can't silently delete that
teaching before a replacement diagnostic exists.

- **`examples/runnable/split_map_join.ail`** exercises both the explicit `split` → `map` → `join`
  pipeline and the fused `mapSlicesJoin` form; green under `make verify-examples` (the CI gate).
- Manifest entry + statistics block updated (total 187→188). No prompt change (prompt stays 2535
  lines). **Prompt-deletion candidates recorded** for the gated diet pass (m-diagnostic-coverage /
  R3.1 sequencing): embedded prompt lines ~704 (`split -> [string]`), ~715 (`splitAny`), and
  ~1071-1085 (`foldSlices`/`mapSlicesJoin` table + worked block) — **not** deleted here.

**Severed to backlog:** Part C / Phase 3 (primitive-field-access `TC_PRIMITIVE_FIELD_ACCESS_001`
unifier diagnostic) was **dropped** from this sprint per Mark's 2026-07-18 ratification and severed
to an extension-lane backlog doc (`design_docs/planned/v0_30_0/m-diag-primitive-field-suggestions.md`).
No `internal/types` change this sprint.

### Added — Gemini clone-over-egress: in-sandbox verification via opt-in network egress (M-GEMINI-REPO-MOUNT Phase 2)

The `managed_agents` (Vertex Managed Agents) executor gains an **opt-in egress-enabled sandbox**
so the hosted agent can `git clone` the public AILANG repo at a target revision, run
review / `ailang check` in-sandbox, and return a structured verdict — upgrading Gemini from
reasoning-only review (prompt-packed diff) to in-sandbox verification. This is the LIVE-VERIFIED
"clone-over-egress" path (probes Q/R). No mount, no GCS, no inline, no encoder.

- **Typed capability gate (`internal/executor/executor.go`).** New `Task.RequiresEgress bool`
  field (zero value = today's behavior), `CapNetworkEgress` capability constant, and a shared
  `ValidateTaskCapabilities(task, exec)` pre-dispatch helper — ONE grep-able enforcement point.
  Setting `RequiresEgress` on an executor that does not advertise `CapNetworkEgress` is a **loud
  error before any network I/O** (no silent fallback), covering programmatic and CLI callers alike.
- **Egress env wiring (`internal/executor/managed_agents/managed_agents.go`).** `buildEnvironment`
  replaces the `envRaw` hardcode: `RequiresEgress == false` → byte-identical `{"type":"remote"}`;
  `RequiresEgress == true` → `{"type":"remote","network":{"allowlist":[{"domain":"*"}]}}` (the exact
  wildcard-egress shape Vertex accepts, probes Q/R). managed_agents now advertises `CapNetworkEgress`.
- **CLI flags (`cmd/ailang/exec.go`).** `ailang exec --clone-repo <url> [--clone-sha <sha>]` sets
  `RequiresEgress`, prepends the canonical clone-review preamble, and runs the shared gate. Clone
  flags on a non-`managed_agents` resolution or with `--api-only`, or `--clone-sha` without
  `--clone-repo`, exit non-zero with a clear error (never ignored).
- **Eval-harness clone-review (`internal/eval_harness/gemini_evaluator_bridge.go`).** `EvalOptions`
  gains `CloneRepoURL` + `CloneSHA`; when set, `RunGeminiEvaluator` builds the clone-review directive
  (HEAD: `git clone --depth 1`; arbitrary SHA: shallow fetch-by-SHA) instead of packing the diff
  (unset → `BuildDiffBundle` path unchanged). Conditional evidence check on the echoed
  `git rev-parse HEAD` (pinned ⇒ must equal `CloneSHA`; HEAD ⇒ valid non-empty 40-hex, recorded as
  the reviewed revision); missing/invalid evidence or a deadline-exceeded runner ⇒
  `VerificationDegraded` with reason — never a silent retry, never a clean pass.

### Fixed — `make fuzz-parser` CI flake: spurious `context deadline exceeded` on the fuzztime boundary (mission iter 50)

Dev CI went red on a **data-only** commit (`3556e9377`) with `FuzzParseExpr: context deadline
exceeded` — no parser code had changed in 7 days and the previous 11 runs were green. Root cause:
when `-fuzztime=2s` expires while a slow, deeply-nested input is still executing on a loaded CI
runner (4 workers vs 16 locally), Go's fuzzing coordinator cancels the worker context and reports
the cancellation as a test FAILURE even though no crasher was found. `make fuzz-parser` now
discriminates a real crasher (`Failing input written to testdata/fuzz/…` → fail immediately) from
the transient boundary timeout (`context deadline exceeded` with no crasher → retry once). A
genuine slow-parse regression that *always* times out still fails on the second attempt, so
crash-detection and perf-regression coverage are preserved.

### Fixed — `design-quorum` reasoning-model reviews truncated silently at the 4096-token cap (mission iter 43)

A reasoning reviewer (Gemini 3.x — "2× reasoning") whose thinking tokens count against
`maxOutputTokens` could exhaust the quorum's `reviewMaxTokens = 4096` cap mid-verdict, returning
a valid-but-cut-off JSON body. `ParseReviewResult` rejected it as "malformed JSON" and the
reviewer was silently dropped, degrading the two-provider quorum to N−1 (observed live mission
iter 42: `gemini-3-1-pro` `finishReason=MAX_TOKENS` on a substantive objection). Intermittent —
it fired cleanly whenever the review happened to be terse.

- **Root-cause fix:** `reviewMaxTokens` raised 4096 → 16384, leaving genuine thinking headroom
  above the ~1–2K structured verdict. Output is billed per actual token (a short verdict stays in
  cents) and the pre-flight budget cap uses a fixed `expectedOutputTokens` estimate, so the
  ceiling change does not alter budget gating (`internal/mission/quorum/call.go`).
- **Fail loudly (Principle 2):** a residual `finish_reason == "length"` truncation now surfaces as
  an explicit `"reviewer output truncated at N tokens … raise reviewMaxTokens"` error instead of an
  opaque parse failure (`internal/mission/quorum/run.go`).
- **Provider signal wired:** the Gemini `Generate` path was discarding `finishReason`; it now
  populates the normalized `ai.Response.FinishReason` (`MAX_TOKENS → length`, `STOP → stop`, safety
  reasons → `content_filter`) so all Gemini callers can see truncation
  (`internal/ai/gemini/generate.go`). Regression tests: `TestNormalizeGeminiFinishReason`,
  `TestRunReviewerWith_TruncatedOutputFailsLoudly`.

### Fixed — `design-quorum` cross-provider schema: OpenAI reviewers were silently dropped (mission iter 41)

The mission pick-time `design-quorum` gate ran with only ONE reviewer present on every
invocation, silently degrading a two-provider quorum to a single-provider veto. Root cause:
`internal/mission/quorum/reviewer.go`'s `reviewSchema` listed `proposed_fix` in `properties`
but deliberately omitted it from `required` (to keep it "optional"). OpenAI's strict
`json_schema` response-format mode **requires every property to appear in `required`** and
400s otherwise (`'required' ... must include every key in properties`), so every OpenAI
reviewer (e.g. `gpt5-6-sol`) was recorded as `unreachable` while only the laxer Gemini
reviewer ran.

- **Fix:** `proposed_fix` is now listed in `required` as a plain `string`. It stays optional
  by CONVENTION — a reviewer with no fix returns `""`, which `ValidateReviewResult` never
  inspects, so it validates byte-identically to the old omitted case; the parser also tolerates
  a missing/`null` value defensively.
- **Cross-provider constraint discovered + guarded:** a nullable `["string","null"]` union
  satisfies OpenAI strict mode but is rejected by Vertex/Gemini (`Proto field is not repeating,
  cannot start list`). A single plain `string` in `required` is the one form BOTH providers
  accept. Live-verified: both `gpt5-6-sol` and `gemini-3-1-pro` now `present`.
- **Regression guards:** `TestReviewSchema_OpenAIStrictInvariant` (every property must be in
  `required`; no union types) + `TestParseReviewResult_NullProposedFixPreservesContract`. The
  stale `TestReviewSchema_ProposedFixNotRequired` (which encoded the broken contract) was removed.

### Added — Inject a sprint diff bundle into a sandboxed gemini evaluator (M-GEMINI-EVALUATOR-DIFF-BRIDGE, M1–M3)

A policy-layer bridge (`internal/eval_harness/gemini_evaluator_bridge.go`) so a sandboxed
gemini sprint EVALUATOR (Vertex Managed Agents, `executor.CapRemoteSandbox`) can review a
sprint's UNCOMMITTED worktree changes. This is the INVERSE of the extract-out
`managed_agents_bridge.go`: the managed_agents executor sends only `Directive` +
`SystemInstruction` with no repo upload, so this INJECTS a git-diff bundle INTO the
directive. Reasoning-only (no local test re-runs). The executor stays policy-free (zero
changes to `internal/executor/managed_agents/`); the frozen `quorum.ReviewResult`
design-review contract is untouched (`GeminiVerdict` is a SEPARATE type in the sprint-eval
domain).

- **M1 — `BuildDiffBundle`.** Enumerates the changed-file set via
  `git status --porcelain -z` (modified + staged + **UNTRACKED** — new files are
  load-bearing: a sprint frequently CREATES files and `git diff` alone misses them),
  emits the tracked unified diff (`git diff` + `git diff --cached`, ALWAYS included /
  never dropped) plus `+++ NEW FILE:` views of untracked files, then appends full
  changed-file contents under a configurable **256 KiB** ceiling. Drop order:
  binary (NUL in first 8 KiB) → generated/vendored (`*.pb.go`, `vendor/`, `dist/`,
  `*.min.js`, `*_generated.go`, `go.sum`) → largest-remaining-whole-file. Every drop
  emits a LOUD `=== BUNDLE TRUNCATED: … — NOT shown to evaluator ===` marker (no silent
  drop) and a dropped new file keeps its `+++ NEW FILE` header. Deterministic (sorted
  drop order).
- **M2 — reasoning-only directive + structured verdict.** `BuildEvaluatorDirective`
  composes a compile-time reasoning-only instruction (NO repo/test access, emit a fenced
  `json` verdict) + the design doc / plan / criteria + the bundle; a truncated bundle
  appends a "note unseen files in blockers" line. `GeminiVerdict{Score, Pass, Blockers,
  VerificationDegraded, DegradedReason}` is a COMPACT ADAPTATION of the sprint-evaluator
  skill's `evaluation_report_schema.md` (documented as such; distinct from
  `quorum.ReviewResult`). `ParseGeminiVerdict`/`ValidateGeminiVerdict` hard-error on a
  malformed verdict (missing fence, non-JSON, score out of `[0,100]`, `pass==true` with
  blockers, degraded without a reason) — never a lenient/coerced pass. Fence parsing is
  shared via a new exported thin wrapper `LastFencedBlock` DELEGATING to the existing
  unexported `lastFencedBlock` (no rename of the extract-out call site).
- **M3 — `RunGeminiEvaluator` caller seam.** Takes an INJECTABLE runner func (tests pass
  a stub — NO live Vertex call in the suite; the default `DefaultGeminiRunner` shells
  `ailang exec gemini --json`). The caller ENFORCES degradation: on bundle truncation OR a
  backend error it stamps `VerificationDegraded=true` with a reason, and a model/stub
  `pass:true` under degradation can NEVER stand as a real pass (CLAUDE.md "no silent
  fallbacks"). A backend error yields a degraded NON-PASS verdict carrying the error text,
  never a fabricated pass.

Ships the CAPABILITY only — routing gemini as the evaluator lives in
`m-mission-agentic-provider-routing.md` (out of scope) and the mission default stays
sonnet-5. Tests: `gemini_evaluator_bridge_test.go` (12 new, each references a symbol
absent on the base binary → non-vacuous; the 5 extract-out `TestExtractCode_*` and the
new `TestLastFencedBlock_UnchangedForExtractOut` guard the shared fence helper).

### Fixed — Thread GCP project/location env into `ailang exec` Task (M-GEMINI-EXEC-PROJECT-PLUMBING)

`ailang exec gemini "<directive>"` failed with `managed_agents: GCP project not set`
even when `AILANG_CLOUD_PROJECT`/`GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT` were exported — the CLI's
`executor.Task` construction (`cmd/ailang/exec.go`) never populated `GCPProject`/
`GCPLocation`, unlike the eval harness (which sets them per-model from `models.yml`).
The agentic gemini provider (Vertex Managed Agents) was therefore unreachable via the
CLI outside the eval harness. Fix: a new `resolveGCPProjectEnv()` helper reads
`AILANG_CLOUD_PROJECT` → `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT` (the coordinator's existing precedence,
`daemon_tasks_init.go`), and `executeCLI` now sets `GCPProject`/`GCPLocation` on the
shared Task. Empty location defers to the executor's own `defaultLocation = "global"`
(no CLI-side default that could drift); unset project stays empty so the existing loud
error still fires (no silent default project). Non-gemini providers are unaffected — the
fields are `""` when env is unset (today's value), and codex/claude already thread
`task.GCPProject` identically. Unblocks the fleet live gemini reviewer/evaluator lane
(fleet step c0). Regression guard: `TestResolveGCPProjectEnv` +
`TestExecTaskGCPFieldsFromEnv` (`cmd/ailang/exec_test.go`, env via `t.Setenv`, no live
GCP call).

### Added — Agentic quorum reviewers + two-tier escalation (M-QUORUM-AGENTIC-VERIFY, M1–M3)

The mission design-doc quorum (`internal/mission/quorum/`) gains a second reviewer
backend that VERIFIES against the repo instead of only reasoning, plus a cost-smart
two-tier escalation. The shipped verdict contract is FROZEN throughout: `ReviewResult`'s
three required fields (`verdict`, `strongest_objection`, `catch`), `ValidateReviewResult`
(reject-by-default), `reviewSchema.required`, the N-1 degradation, and artifact recording
are byte-for-byte unchanged.

- **M1 — agentic caller behind the existing `JSONCaller` seam.** A new `agenticCaller`
  (`agentic_caller.go`) satisfies the EXISTING `JSONCaller` interface (`call.go:35`, no
  interface change) by running a read-only tool-using agent instead of a single text call,
  returning the same `{verdict, strongest_objection, catch}` JSON through the same
  `ParseReviewResult`/`ValidateReviewResult` path. `RunAgenticReviewer` carries the same
  N-1 degradation as the text tier: over-cap → `budget` absence, executor error/kill →
  `unreachable`, malformed output → `invalid` — never a silent pass. The per-review cost cap
  is enforced against the OBSERVED cost (post-flight, mirroring `run.go`); runs are bounded
  by a context deadline. `NewCoordinatorAgenticRunner` (`agentic_provider.go`) REUSES the
  shipped executor layer (`provider_executor.go`: `Kind=="question"` read-only tools,
  `opts.Timeout`/`IdleTimeout`, `Execute(ctx)` cancellation, `result.Cost`, `Workspace`
  worktree) — no new executor plumbing.
- **M2 — escalation trigger + additive-optional `proposed_fix`.** `ShouldEscalate`
  (`escalate.go`) is a pure function that escalates to Tier-2 iff a Tier-1 objection is
  premise-class (a disputed factual codebase claim), the doc self-declares high-stakes
  (Conflict Surface / shared infra), or Tier-1 is split (pass/reject disagreement) — a clean
  unanimous non-high-stakes pass never escalates. `proposed_fix` is added to `ReviewResult`
  and `reviewSchema.properties` as ADDITIVE + OPTIONAL (absent from `required`, NOT validated;
  Mark decision option (a)): a fix-less reject validates byte-identically and is recorded as
  reviewer friction. Reviewers are prompted to include a concrete fix on every reject.
- **M3 — Tier-2 escalated verify (codex + claude, read-only worktrees).** `RunTier2`
  (`tier2.go`) runs the agentic reviewers in parallel over their OWN read-only worktrees,
  focused on the contested premise, when (and only when) `ShouldEscalate` fires.
  `RunQuorumWithEscalation` (`quorum.go`) ties Tier-1 → decision → Tier-2 together; an
  escalated reject blocks the synthesis exactly like a Tier-1 reject (the controller still
  synthesizes — reviewers have no independent merge authority). Tier-2 outcomes are recorded
  distinctly (a `tier` label + additive `Tier2` field on `QuorumResult`, both `omitempty` so
  the shipped Tier-1 artifact shape is preserved for Phase E) and rendered as a labeled
  Tier-2 markdown section.

Deferred: M0 (managed-sandbox outbound-network probe) and M4 (gemini clone-in-sandbox path)
are parked pending the `Task.GCPProject` plumbing gap in the managed_agents lane; M5's
single bounded live-fire is deferred. All M1–M3 acceptance is code + unit tests + build,
provider-independent (no live billed reviewer calls).

### Fixed — `ailang exec gemini` agentic lane reachable (M1c gemini fleet lane)

`ailang exec gemini "..."` (agentic, i.e. without `--api-only`) now routes to the
`managed_agents` executor (Vertex AI Managed Agents API via ADC) instead of failing
with `unknown executor: gemini`. The Gemini CLI was retired in v0.22.0 and its
successor registers in the executor factory under `managed_agents`; a small
`resolveAgenticExecutorName` mapping in the agentic path (`gemini` → `managed_agents`,
identity otherwise) bridges the CLI-facing provider name to the registry name. The
`--api-only` single-shot gemini `generateContent` path (via `internal/ai/gemini`) is
untouched. The stale `exec` help text describing the retired "Gemini CLI" (and its
`--model gemini-2.5-pro` example) was corrected to describe the server-side sandbox —
file edits are bridged via the agent's text output, not written locally — and `--model`
now selects the Vertex agent name (default `antigravity-preview-05-2026`).

### Added — `ailang run --caps auto` capability inference (auto_caps M1, mission iter 32)

`--caps auto` infers the run entrypoint's declared effect row and grants exactly those
capabilities (nothing more), printing `auto-granted capabilities: <sorted,comma-joined>`
(or `none` for a pure entrypoint) to stderr. Removes the #1 friction of having to
remember/spell the `--caps` list for a program you just wrote. Reuses the existing
`iface`/`TFunc2`/`EffectRow` machinery (the same path `DetermineDeclaredAIMode` walks) —
no new analysis pass. Secure-by-default is unchanged: with no `--caps` flag a program that
needs a capability still fails loudly. First slice of the auto_caps design doc; the
standalone `--auto-caps` flag, `AILANG_AUTO_CAPS` env, always-on preflight, and
benchmark-harness integration remain follow-ups.

### Added — Image (vision) input on std/ai (M-STD-AI-VISION-INPUT, stx-bench)

`std/ai` can now feed images INTO a vision-capable model. The `Message` record gains a
required `images: [ImagePart]` field (`ImagePart = {source, mime}`), so **every**
`step`/`stepWithCache`/`stepWithStream` caller — including motoko and any AILANG app — gets
vision with no new API. `source` is base64 or a data-URI; `mime` e.g. `"image/png"`. This is
distinct from `callImage`/`callImageBase64`, which *generate* images.

- Each provider maps images to its native block: Anthropic image content block, OpenAI/OpenRouter
  `image_url`, Gemini `inline_data`, Ollama `images[]`. Empty `images: []` is byte-for-byte
  identical to the pre-vision wire request (no regression).
- Non-vision providers (configdriven) return a typed `AIError{code:"ModelNoVision"}` rather than
  silently dropping the image.
- Live-verified: a 2×2 red PNG through `step()` → Gemini returns "Red".
  Example: [`examples/runnable/ai_vision_input.ail`](examples/runnable/ai_vision_input.ail).

**BREAKING**: `images` is a required field (AILANG records are closed). Existing `Message`
literals must add `images: []`. In-repo callers (std/ai.ail + 4 examples) are migrated. The
missing-field type error now gives an actionable hint (see below). Full static typing on `images`
was chosen over source back-compat — see the design doc's Design Freeze.

One-call structured-output-over-vision (`callJsonResultVision`) was intentionally **not** added;
it is subsumed by the composable [M-AI-STRUCTURED-STEP](design_docs/planned/v0_31_0/m-ai-structured-step.md)
follow-up (schema as an orthogonal option on `step`).

### Improved — Actionable error for a record missing a required field

The record-unification error now, when a literal is **missing** a required field, names the
field and says to add it (`add the field(s) to the literal, e.g. images: <value>`) instead of
only suggesting open-record syntax (which fixes *extra* fields, not missing ones). Systemic —
helps every record type that gains a required field, not just `Message`.

### Fixed — Per-call `step(model)` now resolves models.yml friendly names (DX, cph-uni-stx-bench)

The per-call `step`/`stepWithCache`/`stepWithStream` model parameter now resolves
models.yml **friendly** names (e.g. `gemini-2-5-flash`, with dashes) to their `api_name`
(`gemini-2.5-flash`) the same way the `--ai` flag does, instead of forcing callers to
hand-write api_names. Previously `step("gemini-2-5-flash")` returned `ModelNotFound` even
though `--ai gemini-2-5-flash` accepted the identical friendly form — surprising and
undocumented.

- **Friendly-name resolution** is injected as an `effects.ModelResolver` into the
  `AIContext` at handler setup ([`cmd/ailang/ai_handlers.go`](cmd/ailang/ai_handlers.go),
  [`internal/effects/ai.go`](internal/effects/ai.go)). The resolver lives in `cmd/ailang`
  (which owns models.yml) — the `internal/ai`/`internal/effects` provider layers stay free
  of the eval-harness dependency.
- **api_names still work unchanged.** A string unknown to models.yml passes through to the
  provider untouched (the provider stays the final authority), preserving the pre-fix path
  where `step("gemini-2.5-flash")` already worked.
- **Cross-vendor per-call now gives a clear, typed error.** `step("claude-haiku-4-5")` while
  bound to a Vertex/Google handler returns `AIError{code:"ModelNotAllowed"}` with a hint
  (`resolves to provider "anthropic", but the bound --ai handler is "google" … use --ai
  claude-haiku-4-5 to switch providers`) instead of a bare `ModelNotFound`.

Verified live (`--ai gemini-2-5-flash`): `step("")`→OK, `step("gemini-2.5-flash")`→OK,
`step("gemini-2-5-flash")`→OK (was ModelNotFound), `step("claude-haiku-4-5")`→ModelNotAllowed
with hint. Unit tests in [`internal/effects/ai_test.go`](internal/effects/ai_test.go).

### Added / Fixed — One-shot stdlib discovery for AI agents (M-DX-AI-DISCOVERY, mission iter 30)

Three self-contained CLI/error-path surfaces so an agent (or human) can discover the
whole stdlib without writing throwaway probe files. All rendered from live mechanisms
— no hand-maintained tables that can drift.

**`ailang docs --all-functions [filter]` (M1)** — one deterministic, grep-able line per
stdlib export (`std/clock.now: (()) -> int ! {Clock} -- ...`), plus the prelude
pseudo-module. Signatures are rendered from the AST (`ast.Type.String()` /
`ast.FormatEffects`, the same renderers the compiler and error messages use) instead
of the old brittle regex. Optional trailing positional filters case-insensitively over
the full line. An unparseable stdlib file fails loudly (stderr + non-zero exit), never
a silent partial render.

**V16 effect-row truncation fixed** — the per-module `ailang docs std/X` view shared the
same regex signature path, which stopped at `{` and dropped effect rows
(`now() -> int ! {Clock}` printed as `now() -> int ! `). `showModuleDocs` now renders
from the AST too, so effect rows print in full in both views.

**Unknown-stdlib-module recovery (M3)** — a mistyped module (`import std/time`) now gets
`did you mean: std/clock?` plus the sorted available-module list appended to the
"stdlib module not found" error. A curated alias table (`time→clock`, `date→datetime`,
`http→net`, ...) is tried first (it catches edit-distance-5 slips the distance pass
can't), then Levenshtein ≤2 over the live module list — reusing the existing
`internal/importhint` engine (no parallel implementation). If the module list is
unavailable it says so explicitly (no silent skip).

**`ailang docs prelude` (M4)** — renders the entry-module prelude (previously
`module 'std/prelude' not found`): `println` (enumerated from `pipeline.InjectPrelude`'s
surface), the `show` builtin, and the implicit `std/option` / `std/result` imports
(from the loader's `EntryPreludeModules` / new `EntryPreludeSymbols` accessor), with the
entry-only / lowest-precedence / silent-shadowing scope notes. A bidirectional drift
test fails if a prelude binding is added or removed without the page tracking it.
`ailang docs --list` gains a footer line pointing at it.

**Guarded (M5)** — `ailang docs search` remains the design-doc SimHash search,
byte-identical (regression-guarded so the new `--all-functions` positional filter can
never intercept the `search` subcommand). Guide: `docs/docs/guides/ai-stdlib-discovery.md`.

### Fixed — `--routing-max-price` now enforces the cost cap instead of silently dropping it

The `--routing-max-price` flag (and `AIRoutingPolicy.MaxPricePerMTok`) was parsed but
**never forwarded** — a stale `TODO(v0.17.0)` left it "reserved for a future version"
through v0.29. Callers who set a per-million-token price cap got a request with **no cost
guard at all**, which is worse than having no flag: it reads as enforced when it isn't.
The code comment also misattributed the OpenRouter surface (claimed `transforms`; the cap
actually lives under `provider.max_price`).

**Now:**
- **OpenRouter forwards it.** `translatePolicy` emits `provider.max_price.{prompt,completion}`
  (USD per million tokens; the single cap applies to both legs), so providers priced above
  the cap are excluded upstream. Verified against
  [OpenRouter provider-routing docs](https://openrouter.ai/docs/features/provider-routing).
- **Fail loud on malformed input.** A non-numeric or negative cap is rejected at the CLI
  boundary (`buildRoutingPolicy`) and again defensively in the provider translation, rather
  than silently shipping an unguarded request. `translatePolicy` now returns an error.
- **Other providers reject it, not ignore it.** Anthropic / OpenAI / Gemini cannot enforce a
  per-token price cap. Previously a *price-cap-only* policy slipped past their routing guard
  (`HasRouting()` only checks `Order`/`AllowFallback`) and was silently dropped. A new
  `PriceCapSet()` check makes all three return `ErrRoutingNotSupported` for any policy
  carrying a cap. **The max-price budget is OpenRouter-only, and now says so loudly.**

Files: [internal/ai/openrouter/routing.go](../internal/ai/openrouter/routing.go),
[internal/ai/routing.go](../internal/ai/routing.go),
[internal/ai/openrouter/{chat,step,streamstep}.go](../internal/ai/openrouter/),
[internal/ai/{anthropic,openai,gemini}/](../internal/ai/),
[cmd/ailang/routing_flags.go](../cmd/ailang/routing_flags.go).

### Fixed — `ailang verify` no longer crashes Z3 on cross-function callees over parametric ADTs (M-SMT-CALLEE-SORT-GATE)

A contracted function whose cross-function callee returns (or takes) a parametric ADT
over a primitive — e.g. `convertTo(...) -> Option[float]` — made `ailang verify` emit a
hard Z3 error (`unknown sort 'Option'`, `unknown constant convertTo (Real String)`,
exit status 1) instead of the promised graceful skip. Root cause: the fragment gate only
inspected `$builtin`/stdlib call *names*, never the signature *types* of a user callee, so
the callee leaked an undeclared sort into the SMT script. Reported via feedback (grader
`gradeNumeric` path).

**Primary fix (M1)** — a caller-side gate (`firstUnencodableCalleeType` in
[cmd/ailang/verify.go](../cmd/ailang/verify.go)) walks the cross-function call graph and,
before encoding, rejects any caller whose callee signature uses an unencodable *AST* type:
a parametric ADT application (`Option[float]`, `Result[e,a]`), a bare type variable, or an
undeclared bare name. The check is AST-based on purpose — the flattened SMT sort string
`"Option"` loses the `[float]` argument, and an imported parametric ADT is registered in
`adtTypes` yet can't be monomorphized, so a sort-string check wrongly passes it. Monomorphic
enums, records, lists, and primitives still verify unchanged. The function now reports
`SKIPPED` with a structured `UNENCODABLE_TYPE` reason naming the callee and offending type.

**Defense-in-depth (M2)** — `validateDeclarations` in
[internal/smt/codegen.go](../internal/smt/codegen.go) now also scans callee `(define-fun …)`
*signatures* (params + return, never the body) for undeclared sorts, returning
`ErrUnresolvableTypes` (→ graceful skip) so any leak that slips the caller gate still can't
reach Z3.

**Leak-site guard (M3, systemic)** — a follow-up report showed two more shapes still
hard-ERRORed with `unknown constant`: a callee returning a **record** (`canon(...) -> Rec`)
and a user function called inside a **contract predicate** (`ensures { legal(result) }`). Both
share one root cause: *any* user function referenced (in the body OR a requires/ensures
predicate) that `ResolveCallees` doesn't turn into a define-fun/contract-substitution falls
through `encodeApp` to `encodeConstructorApp` and emits a raw uninterpreted symbol. The fix
detects this at the leak site: `encodeApp` now knows the set of user function names
(`activeUserFunctions`) and, on an unresolved call to one, returns `ErrUnresolvableTypes` (→
graceful skip) instead of leaking. This single guard covers every drop path and both body +
contract positions; real ADT constructor applications are unaffected. Verified: `useCanon`
(record callee) and `pick` (`legal` in `ensures`) now SKIP; `useDbl` (int callee) and
`classify` (enum-in-ensures) still VERIFY.

Note this closes the *crash* for all known shapes — no contracted function hard-errors on an
unencodable cross-function callee anymore. It does not *extend* the fragment: actually
verifying record/enum-returning callees, user functions in contract predicates, or
`Option`/`Result`-over-primitive callees is future work (parametric-ADT monomorphization +
contract-callee inlining). New example
[`examples/runnable/contracts/unencodable_callee_skip.ail`](../examples/runnable/contracts/unencodable_callee_skip.ail),
unit + e2e tests (record-callee & contract-predicate-callee skip-not-error, leak-guard unit),
design doc `design_docs/planned/v0_30_0/m-smt-callee-sort-gate.md`.

### Added — `std/yaml`: YAML ingestion via a pure JSON bridge (M-STD-YAML)

New `std/yaml` stdlib module for reading YAML into AILANG's existing `Json` ADT,
unblocking single-language pipelines that previously had to shell out to Python for
YAML→JSON (requested by `cph-uni-stx-bench` for the STX benchmark harness). One
Go-backed pure builtin `_yaml_to_json` (`gopkg.in/yaml.v3` → `encoding/json`, already
vendored) serves both public functions:

- `yamlToJson(s: string) -> Result[string, string]` — the minimal bridge (YAML string → JSON string).
- `decode(s: string) -> Result[Json, string]` — `yamlToJson` composed with `std/json.decode` in
  pure AILANG, reusing the `std/json` `Json` ADT so YAML and JSON share one accessor surface. Zero extra Go.

**Pure and WASM-portable** — `yaml.v3` is pure Go (verified `GOOS=js GOARCH=wasm` buildable),
so `std/yaml` runs in the browser, unlike a Python pre-step. **No silent coercion** (A5/A11):
YAML with no JSON representation (non-string map keys, `NaN`/`±Inf`) returns `Err`, never a guessed
value. Single-document only in v1 (`decodeAll` deferred); anchors/aliases resolve but aren't preserved.
New reference page [`std/yaml`](../docs/docs/reference/std-yaml.md), example
`examples/runnable/yaml_config.ail`, and `tests/yaml_bridge_test.ail`.

### Added / Fixed — Examples coverage, a REAL CI gate, and a working `ailang docs --examples` (M-DX-EXAMPLES-COVERAGE, mission iter 29)

Closed four verified gaps in the examples surface (issue #341 absorbed as Phase 1).

**Coverage (M2)** — 6 new runnable examples for the previously zero-importer std
modules, each ~30–60 LOC showing the module's headline pattern with deterministic
output and a manifest entry: `stdlib_embedding.ail` (cosine-similarity ranking),
`stdlib_game.ail` (fixed-timestep movement + Clock `frame_count()`),
`stdlib_gzip.ail` (lossless compress→decompress round-trip), `stdlib_sharedindex.ail`
(namespaced SimHash index + deterministic query, needs `--caps SharedIndex`),
`stdlib_simhash.ail` (SimHash + Hamming near-duplicate classify), `stdlib_smoke.ail`
(package boot-probe pattern). `std/embedding`, `std/game`, `std/gzip`,
`std/sharedindex`, `std/simhash`, `std/smoke` now each have ≥1 example importer.

**A real CI gate (M3)** — `make verify-examples` now actually gates: it captures
the verifier's exit code and re-raises it AFTER writing both `examples_report.json`
and `examples_status.md` unconditionally (preserving all 5+ report consumers).
Removed all three silent-pass layers (`|| true` in `make/examples.mk`, the
unmatchable `Failed: 0` grep and the unused `status` output in `ci.yml`) — this is
the NO-SILENT-FALLBACKS principle applied to CI (A5). Added `if: always()` to the
following trace step so its signal still prints on a red gate. New
`make verify-examples-gate-selftest` guards the call-site: a deliberately-broken
fixture must turn the verifier non-zero AND still write both artifacts.

**Manifest `modules` + drift-lint (M3/M4)** — `examples/manifest.json` entries gain
an additive `modules` field, backfilled for all resolvable entries by a one-time
parser-backed script (`make backfill-manifest-modules`) using the real compiler
parser (`internal/parser` → `ast.File.Imports`), NOT regex. `validate_manifest.go`
was previously unwired (it couldn't even load the real manifest); it's rewritten to
do schema + `modules`-vs-actual-imports drift validation via the SAME shared
extractor, wired into the `verify-examples` gate (`--ci`), with a self-test
(`make validate-manifest-selftest`) proving a drifted entry turns it red.

**`ailang docs --examples <module>` works (M4)** — the flag was inert (byte-identical
with/without it, A11 tooling-honesty lie). It now prints a "Try it" section of
registered runnable examples that import the module, with `ailang run` commands,
resolved via the existing installed-binary path (`AILANG_EXAMPLES` /
`~/.ailang/examples`). No matches says so explicitly; resolution failure prints the
explicit error as a note — never silently identical to flagless output.
`ailang docs <module>` (no flag) is byte-identical to before.

**Triage of the 5 red examples (M1)** — `effectful_list.ail`,
`effectful_list_t7_chain_combinators.ail`, `mcp_tools.ail`, `stream_multi_source.ail`,
`stream_process_source.ail` were red on dev with CI green. Bisect from v0.13.0 was
inconclusive (corrupted by stale-binary caching → false-positive on a docs-only
commit); in-file bisection root-caused a **genuine effect-row inference regression**
(`show()` in an effectful-lambda string interpolation collapses the combinator
effect row to closed-empty, conflicting with an explicit `{IO}`) plus a separate
`getString`-returns-`Option[string]` regression in mcp_tools. Per the sprint's
Conflict Surface (no `internal/types`/`internal/effects` changes), the 5 files are
**quarantined** (skipped with reason in `verify_examples.go` + `status: broken` in
the manifest) and owned by follow-up issue **#386** (bisect result + owner + the
5-file quarantine list as its closing checklist). `make verify-examples` → 0 failed.



Two new CLI subcommands let a design doc get an independent, off-quota second
opinion from non-Anthropic frontier models before an Opus sprint is spent on it:

- `ailang design-review <doc> --reviewer <model> [--json] [--max-cost-usd 0.10]` —
  one **reject-by-default** reviewer verdict (`verdict` ∈ {pass, reject} +
  required non-empty `strongest_objection` + `catch`). Exits non-zero if the
  reviewer can't produce a verdict — never a silent pass.
- `ailang design-quorum <doc> [--reviewers gpt5-6-sol,gemini-3-1-pro]
  [--controller-verdict pass|reject --controller-note ...] [--mission-log <path>]` —
  composes N reviewers **in parallel** plus the Claude controller's IN-SESSION
  review (not an API call) into one verdict. Synthesis: any present reviewer or
  the controller rejects → **blocked** (exit 3), the objection returns to the
  author; all present pass → **proceed** (exit 0). Writes a machine artifact
  under `.ailang/state/mission-quorum/<slug>-<iso>.json` (seeds Phase E's
  assignment table) and, with `--mission-log`, a human markdown block.

**Graceful N−1 degrade (no silent fallbacks):** an unreachable / over-budget /
mis-authed reviewer is recorded by NAME with its reason (`unreachable`/`budget`/
`auth`/`invalid`) and the quorum proceeds with the remaining reviewers; a missing
reviewer is never treated as a pass, and all-absent = blocked (refuses to proceed
on zero signal). Per-reviewer **budget cap** (`--max-cost-usd`, default $0.10)
refuses before any spend when the estimate (scaled to the actual doc size)
exceeds the cap.

Rides the shipped `internal/ai/{openai,gemini}` handlers + the `models.yml`
resolver (no `ailang exec` unification rebuild — the binding redundancy audit).
Gemini reviewers route via **Vertex ADC** (not `GEMINI_API_KEY`). Invoked via a
documented optional hook in the design-doc-creator skill — **no inner-loop
skill-contract change**. New package `internal/mission/quorum`.

### Added — Option/Result are prelude in entry modules, no import needed (M-PRELUDE-OPTION-RESULT)

`Option`/`Some`/`None` and `Result`/`Ok`/`Err` — the types AND their data
constructors — now resolve **without** `import std/option` / `import std/result`
in **entry modules** (a module with an exported `main`), at both `ailang check`
and `ailang run`. You can construct and pattern-match them directly:

```ailang
module m
export func main() -> () ! {IO} {
  match Some(42) { Some(x) => println(show(x)), None => println("none") }  -- no import
}
```

Previously this failed with `undefined variable: Some` — ~6% of recent
AI-authored compile failures were exactly this missing import. Models write
idiomatically-correct AILANG (Option/Result are in the standard prelude of
Haskell, Rust, OCaml, F#, Swift) and reasonably omit the import; this removes the
friction structurally rather than re-teaching every model.

**How:** entry modules implicitly import `std/option` + `std/result` at the
**loader** — the single call-site both the compile pipeline (`mod.File.Imports`)
and the runtime (`LoadedModule.Imports`) consume — so compile and runtime stay in
lockstep with **zero** changes to the type checker, elaborator, or runtime
resolver core. The injection reuses the existing constructor-import machinery
(selective import → `M-CTOR-AUTO` → `resolveConstructorImport` →
`findConstructorMatches`).

**Shadowing / precedence (lowest):** implicit imports are prepended and deduped.
A user's explicit `import std/option (…)` is left untouched; a local
`type Option[a] = Some(a) | None` or `type Result = Pending | Done` (different
constructors, same name) — or any local constructor whose name collides —
**shadows** the prelude cleanly (the colliding module is simply not injected), so
there is no ambiguous-constructor error at compile or runtime.

**Entry-only:** library modules (no exported `main`) are untouched and still
require explicit imports, keeping library code self-documenting. Helper functions
(`map`, `getOrElse`, …) still need an explicit import (only the type +
constructors are prelude). No `cacheKeyVersion` bump — no persisted `Iface`
struct changed; entry-module cache keys shift naturally because their dependency
set now includes the two implicit deps. Example: `examples/prelude_option_result.ail`.

### Added — parameterized type aliases substitute their arguments (M-XMOD-ALIAS-POLY)

A type alias may now take type parameters and be **used at a concrete
instantiation**: `type Box[a] = { items: [a] }` used as `Box[int]` type-checks
as `{ items: [int] }`. Previously the applied form reached unification as an
un-expanded `TApp{TCon("Box"), [int]}` and failed with
`cannot unify old record type with *types.TApp`. Now working, single-module and
cross-module, for every alias body shape:

- **record** — `type Box[a] = { items: [a] }` → `Box[int]`
- **bare param** — `type Ident[a] = a` → `Ident[int]`
- **tuple** — `type Pair[a, b] = (a, b)` → `Pair[int, string]`
- **function** — `type Fn[a, b] = (a) -> b` → `Fn[int, int]`
- **nested** — `Box[Box[int]]`

**How:** aliases now carry their parameter names (`AliasParams`) alongside the
body at every layer (elaborator, `iface.Iface`, type-checker, unifier), threaded
cross-module through the interface (JSON cache round-trip) and the import
resolver. `expandAlias` gained a `*TApp` branch that instantiates an applied
alias by substituting `param→arg` through the body (via the existing
`Type.Substitute` method, which recurses through record/tuple/function bodies)
and continues the fixpoint loop, so alias chains still resolve.

**Non-regression (critical):** expansion is keyed **strictly on alias-env
membership**, so genuine parameterized ADTs (`Option[a]`, `Result[a, b]`, a user
`type Tree[a] = …`) — whose head constructor is registered as a constructor, not
an alias — are **never** instantiated and stay nominal. Locked by tests.

**Arity mismatch** (`Box[int, string]` on a 1-param alias, or a nullary alias
applied with args) now fails with a coded, directional diagnostic
`TC_ALIAS_ARITY_001` (styled on `TC_ARITY_001`) instead of a generic unification
error. Adding a parameterized alias is **digest-neutral** (no dependent-package
cascade), locked by the digest test. `cacheKeyVersion` bumped `v2 → v3`
(on-disk `Iface` gained `AliasParams`). Example: `examples/type_alias_poly.ail`.

### Verified — two "footgun" claims closed as ghosts, with regression guards (M-DX-MATCH-HOF, M-POLY-ARITH-LAMBDA)

Mission iteration 25 live-probed two long-queued footgun rows at HEAD; both were already fixed:

- **`match` inside block-body lambdas in HOF arguments** — the original failure used the retired
  `match … with` syntax; brace-form `match` works in every probed position (block-body, direct
  body, mid-block, nested HOFs, curried `foldl` lambdas). The `\x ->` wrong-arrow confusion it was
  conflated with already has a first-line teaching diagnostic. Guard:
  `examples/match_hof_lambda.ail`.
- **Polymorphic arithmetic in lambdas** — fixed in v0.7.0; verified including a single let-bound
  lambda used at BOTH `int` and `float`, and arithmetic+comparison mixed in one lambda. Guard:
  `examples/poly_arith_lambda.ail`.

Both guards run in `verify-examples`, so neither can silently regress. (PR #379)

### Fixed — lambda `{field, ...}` open-record patterns now propagate row polymorphism (M-LAMBDA-OPEN-RECORD-PATTERN)

`\obj. match obj { {name, ...} => name }` previously inferred a **closed** parameter
`{name: τ}` instead of the **open** `{name: τ | r}` the user asked for with `...`, so a
caller passing a record with extra fields was rejected — even though top-level
`let … in match` already worked, and the error's own Hint told the user to write
`{name, ...}`, which they already had. Fixed across three coordinated sites:

- **`core.RecordPattern` gains a `Rest bool`** (was erased at the AST→Core boundary);
  the elaborator now propagates `p.Rest`.
- **`checkPattern` honors `Rest`** for an *unresolved* scrutinee (a lambda parameter):
  an open pattern builds a `{fields | freshRow}` constraint; a closed `{name}` builds a
  `Row: nil` (closed) constraint so it STILL rejects extra fields. A *resolved* record
  scrutinee keeps subset-matching semantics unchanged.
- **Record unification does row-polymorphic subsumption** (`unifyOpenRecords`): the old
  `TRecord ~ TRecord` path rejected any field-count difference *before* consulting row
  variables, so an open `{name | r}` param vs a `{name, id}` caller failed a length check
  and never reached row unification. Now, when either side is genuinely open (row tail
  unbound — a helper disambiguates the overloaded `TRecord.Row`, since even closed literals
  carry a `*Row{Tail:nil}`), common fields unify and each side's extra fields flow into the
  other side's extension row. The strict length check is kept ONLY for closed~closed.

Soundness preserved (regression matrix, 7 tests in
`internal/pipeline/lambda_open_record_test.go`): open+extra PASS, open+matching PASS,
closed+matching PASS, **closed+extra FAIL**, IIFE+extra PASS, **open+missing-field FAIL**,
and the misleading hint no longer fires on the fixed reproducer. `examples/runnable/record_patterns.ail`
restored to use `{name, ...}` in a lambda with an extra-field caller.
