### Fixed — external user feedback silently lost (M-PUBLIC-FEEDBACK-DELIVERY-AUDIT)

Two independent defects caused external users' public feedback to never reach
Discord (or any watched inbox). Both are fixed; the live prod round-trip is a
one-time human daemon reload (see the daemon docs).

- **Defect A — package feedback was Discord-invisible by construction.** The
  notify daemon tagged `EventType: "public-feedback"` (the only inbox class the
  Discord allow-list accepts) **only** for the literal `public-feedback` inbox,
  but the feedback publisher routes package feedback to `pkg:*` inboxes, which
  got `EventType: "message"` and were dropped. `messageNotification`
  (`internal/daemon/handlers.go`) now treats an inbox as external-feedback-worthy
  when it is `public-feedback` **or** any `pkg:*` inbox (new named, unit-tested
  `isExternalFeedbackInbox` helper), giving both the 🌐 shape and the
  `public-feedback` EventType with the inbox name in the body. Internal inboxes
  (`user`/`controlplane`/agents) keep `EventType: "message"` and stay
  Discord-dropped — `DefaultDiscordEventTypes` is deliberately **not** widened
  (that would leak internal chatter to Discord).
- **Defect B — daemon watched dev only; the public MCP writes prod.** The public
  MCP (`mcp.ailang.sunholo.com`) writes to **prod** Firestore/PubSub
  (`ailang-multivac`), but the rig notify daemon subscribed to **dev**
  (`ailang-multivac-dev`) only, so nothing listened to prod. The daemon can now
  **dual-subscribe**: it holds N inbox-message sources (dev + prod) fanning out
  to the same notifier/webhook, with a shared dedup window (message IDs are
  globally unique) and task events kept dev-only. Opt-in and **off by default**
  (existing single-project behavior is byte-identical) via
  `extra_message_envs: [prod]` in `daemon.yaml` or `--also-subscribe prod` on
  `ailang daemon run`. Each extra source's Firestore fetcher is scoped to its own
  project via the new `firestore.NewClientForProject(ctx, projectID)` — **without**
  mutating the shared `AILANG_CLOUD_PROJECT` env (which would collide the dev/prod
  fetchers). No prod resource is created or modified; the daemon only reads the
  already-existing `ailang-messages-laptop` subscription.
- **Runbook:** triaging public feedback requires
  `AILANG_CLOUD_PROJECT=ailang-multivac` (**prod**) — every dev-only check has
  been blind to real users. Documented in
  [agent-messaging](docs/docs/guides/agent-messaging.md) and
  [cloud-messaging-integration](docs/docs/guides/cloud-messaging-integration.md).

### Fixed / Changed — module-level `let`/`letrec` resolves module funcs (M-MODULE-LET-FUNC-RESOLUTION, #366)

A module-level `let`/`letrec` value now resolves module-scope names **exactly as a
func body does**, closing the 4th member of the "#323/#327 resolution diverges by
syntactic position" family — this one at the DECL-CLASS level (module-let vs func)
rather than the expression-position level that #327 fixed.

- **Widening (previously a FALSE error, now compiles):** a top-level `let` whose
  value calls a module `func` — in either declaration order, including an immediate
  call `let four = double(2)` and a combinator `let quad = \y. double(double(y))`.
  These used to fail with a misleading `undefined variable` whose "workaround"
  ("bind it with let first") was a no-op for the shape. This broke the nightly
  `higher_order_functions` benchmark on 2026-07-13; the cleaned top-level-let
  solution now type-checks and runs to `Result: 14`.
- **Mechanism:** module lets are now first-class nodes of the existing SCC
  call-graph (`BuildCallGraph` in `internal/elaborate/scc.go`), emitted as
  `core.Let`/`core.LetRec` in topological order interleaved with funcs. The
  wrapping special case (`wrapInLets`) and its two duplicate re-elaboration loops
  in `internal/elaborate/file.go` are **deleted** — one unified declaration
  ordering, no per-decl re-evaluation of let values.
- **`letrec` at module level now supported:** a recursive-lambda `letrec` compiles
  and runs; a non-lambda self-reference (`letrec loop = loop + 1`) gives an HONEST
  runtime `RT_REC_001` cycle error, never the old false `undefined variable`.
- **Narrowing (new compile error) — `MOD007`:** a module-scope name declared as
  BOTH a `let` and a `func`, or as two `let`s, was previously accepted **silently**
  with undefined shadowing semantics. It is now a loud compile error naming **both**
  declaration positions with a rename fix. A col-0 scan of `examples/**` +
  `stdlib/**` found zero programs relying on the old silent shadowing.
- **Diagnostic truth pass:** the residual "not resolvable in this position" hint
  (`internal/types/import_hint.go`) no longer cites the **closed** #327 or the no-op
  "bind it with let first" workaround; it now cites the live #366 and the VERIFIED
  workaround "declare it as a `func`".
- New behavior-matrix test `internal/elaborate/module_let_resolution_test.go`
  (v1–v10 shapes), new runnable example `examples/runnable/module_let_helpers.ail`,
  MOD007 footgun fixture, and `internal/diag/footguns.md` + errors-reference updates.

### Security — tarball extraction hardened (Tar Slip / `gosecurity:S6096`)

`pkg.ExtractTarball` (the `ailang install` / registry download-and-extract path) used
a weaker `filepath.Clean` + reject-leading-`..` guard than the sibling extractor in
`internal/builtins/tar.go`. It's now brought to parity: a shared traversal check
(`isTarPathTraversal`: rejects `..`, absolute, and non-`IsLocal` names), a
`filepath.Rel` containment check that the entry stays under `destDir`, and **explicit
rejection of symlink/hardlink entries** (previously they fell through to a regular-file
write by luck rather than by design). Regression tests `TestExtractTarball_RejectsTraversal`
and `TestExtractTarball_RejectsMaliciousEntries` cover parent/nested traversal, absolute
paths, and symlink/hardlink escapes. Clears the three BLOCKER `gosecurity:S6096` findings
on `internal/pkg/tarball.go`.

### Added — coded, directional arity diagnostic `TC_ARITY_001` (M-ARITY-STYLE)

- Calling a function with the wrong number of arguments now emits a **coded,
  directional, style-aware** diagnostic instead of the bare
  `function arity mismatch: 2 vs 1`. The message carries the greppable code
  `TC_ARITY_001`, states how many arguments the function expects vs how many were
  provided, and adds a `Suggestion:` line naming the fix:
  ```
  TC_ARITY_001: function expects 2 argument(s), but 1 provided
    Suggestion: AILANG has no partial application — call with all 2 arguments, or wrap in a lambda `\a b. f(a, b)`.
  ```
  - **Under-supply / partial application** (`add(1)` or `let inc = add(1)` on an
    arity-2 `add`) names AILANG's no-partial-application rule.
  - **Over-supply** (`add(1, 2, 3)`) tells you to remove the extra argument(s).
- **Diagnostic-only** — no arity semantics change. Strict arity and
  no-partial-application are preserved; no program that compiled before stops
  compiling, and curried↔tupled unification still works. The code + hint are
  inline in the message (mirroring the `TC_REC_00X` convention) because the
  unification error is plain-`%w`-wrapped and never recovered via `errors.As`.
- Documented in [docs/docs/reference/errors/index.md](../docs/docs/reference/errors/index.md).
  Golden tests: `internal/types/arity_diagnostic_test.go`.

### Fixed — record patterns in cons & nested-record-list extraction verified + regression-guarded (M-DX-RECORD-CONS, M-DX-TAPP-TRECORD)

- Two long-standing DX bugs from the `docparse-demo` feedback (Feb 2026) were **verified fixed at
  HEAD and closed as ghosts** (mission iteration 18), then locked in with CI-enforced regression
  guards so they cannot silently regress:
  - **M-DX-RECORD-CONS** — a record literal as the head of a `::` cons pattern
    (`{text: s, bold: b} :: rest`) once failed with `PAT_INVALID_CONS`. It now type-checks in both
    the infix and canonical `::({…}, rest)` forms. Guards:
    `internal/parser/list_cons_pattern_test.go::TestListConsPatternWithRecord` and the runnable
    example `examples/record_cons_pattern.ail`.
  - **M-DX-TAPP-TRECORD** — a helper taking a nested list of a record-type alias
    (`makeTable(rows: [[TableCell]]) -> Block`) once failed inference with `cannot unify type
    application with *types.TRecord` when the same body type-checked inline. It now type-checks in
    both the `func` and `let … = \rows. …` forms. Guard: the runnable example
    `examples/record_list_extraction.ail`.
- No language/runtime change — the underlying fixes shipped earlier with record-pattern / `::`-sugar
  and type-unification work; this iteration is verification + regression coverage. Both design docs
  moved to `design_docs/implemented/v0_30_0/` with resolution notes.

### Added — compile-time warning for reversed `split` arguments (M-DX-SPLIT-ARG)

- The compiler now emits a **non-blocking** warning when it detects the reversed
  `split` footgun: `split("/", name)` where the first arg is a short (1–3 rune)
  string literal and the second arg is not a literal. `split(s, delimiter)` is
  **data-first**, so `split("/", name)` silently splits the literal `"/"` by the
  contents of `name` and returns the 1-element list `["/"]` instead of the parts.
- The warning names the exact call site and suggests the fix:
  ```
  warning: split(s, delimiter) takes the string first, delimiter second.
    --> app.ail:8:20
    = hint: did you mean split(name, "/")?
    = note: split("/", name) splits "/" by name, which likely returns ["/"]
  ```
- Both `ailang run` and `ailang check` surface it (and `check` now renders pipeline
  warnings, which it previously dropped). The program **still compiles and runs** —
  this turns a silent wrong result into a visible diagnostic (A11 Structured Failure).
- **Why models get this wrong**: `join(delimiter, xs)` is separator-first while
  `split(s, delimiter)` is data-first — inverse operations with **opposite** arg
  orders — and both args are `string`, so the type system can't catch a swap.
  Observed twice in AI-generated AILANG code.
- **Extensible**: the detector is a `swapTraps` table keyed by imported symbol
  (`internal/pipeline/warn_split_args.go`); only `std/string.split` is armed today.
  A user-defined local `split` never triggers (module-guarded on the resolved
  `std/string.split` reference).
- New runnable example **`examples/runnable/split_argument_order.ail`** demonstrates
  the wrong-vs-right call end-to-end (the reversed call yields 1 part, the correct
  call yields 3) with the join-vs-split ordering teaching in its header.
- Implements the split heuristic without a runtime or type-system change; see
  `design_docs/planned/v0_29_0/m-dx-split-argument-warning.md`.

### Added — `std/json.asBoolLoose`: boundary-tolerant boolean decode (M-DX-JSON-BOOL)

- New helper **`asBoolLoose(j: Json) -> Option[bool]`** for the *system-boundary*
  case where an external API may hand a boolean back as either a JSON boolean or a
  stringified `"true"`/`"false"`. It accepts `JBool(b)` **or** `JString "true"/"false"`;
  anything else is `None` — a **structured failure, never a silent default** (CP2).
- **Why**: booleans must be encoded with `jb(b)` (→ JSON `true`), *not* `js("true")`
  (→ JSON `"true"`). The two are different JSON values, and `asBool` only accepts the
  real boolean, so a value written the wrong way — or normalised by an API like
  Firestore's `booleanValue` — reads back as `None` and is silently treated as false.
  `asBoolLoose` is the liberal-in-what-you-accept decoder for exactly that boundary;
  keep using `asBool` for values you control.
- New runnable example **`examples/runnable/json_bool_encoding.ail`** demonstrates the
  `jb` vs `js` footgun and the `asBool`/`asBoolLoose` distinction end-to-end.
- Regression test `internal/repl/json_asboolloose_test.go` reproduces the Firestore
  `{"field":{"booleanValue": …}}` round-trip for both boolean and stringified shapes.
- **Scope note**: this landed the in-repo half of M-DX-JSON-BOOL. The original
  package-level encoder bug (`sunholo/firestore/fields` using `js("true")`) lives in
  the separate `ailang-packages` repo and is parked for a coordinator task there.

### Fixed — foreign-constructor error now lists transitively-known constructors (M-MATCH-XCHECK-ERROR-QUALITY)

- When a `match` arm uses the wrong ADT's constructor, the error already named
  both ADTs and the offending constructor. But the `<scrutinee ADT>'s constructors
  are:` suggestion line was **empty** whenever the scrutinee's ADT was known only
  *transitively* — e.g. `std/json.asNumber` returns `Option[float]`, but a module
  that imports only `std/result` (not `std/option`) matched it with `Ok`/`Err`.
  The typechecker knew the scrutinee was `Option` yet couldn't enumerate `Some`/
  `None` because they weren't in the direct-import constructor map.
- Fix (design doc Option A): a **diagnostic-only** constructor→ADT registry sourced
  from every transitively-loaded module interface, consulted **only** for the error
  message when the direct/local map has no entry for that ADT. The offending case
  now reads `Option's constructors are: None, Some` with a matching `did you mean
  one of: None, Some?` hint. The registry never brings constructors into scope — a
  module that hasn't imported `std/option` still cannot call `Some(x)`; the message
  is purely a suggestion. Cosmetic/UX only; correctness was already sound.

### Added — `std/regex`: linear-time (RE2) regular expressions (M-STDLIB-REGEX)

- New stdlib module **`std/regex`** backed by Go's `regexp` package (the RE2
  engine): matching is **guaranteed linear-time with no backtracking**. The
  classic catastrophic pattern `(a+)+$` against a long non-matching input — which
  makes PCRE/Python/JS blow up exponentially — returns in microseconds. This
  closes the regex half of the v1.0 orchestration bar (clause 4).
- Two-stage API (like `std/json`'s `decode -> Result`): `compile(pattern) ->
  Result[Regex, string]` is the **only** fallible entry point; once you hold a
  `Regex`, the match functions are total — `isMatch`, `findFirst`
  (`Option[RegexMatch]`), `findAll` (`[RegexMatch]`), `replaceAll` (Go `$1`/
  `${name}` replacement syntax), `split`. All `pure` (`! {}`), usable in contracts.
- **RE2 subset is the documented contract**: no backreferences, no lookaround —
  these are what RE2 drops to guarantee linearity. `compile` returns `Err(message)`
  for them (and for any invalid pattern), **never a panic** (CP2). See LIMITATIONS.
- Capture groups: `RegexMatch` exposes the whole-match span + per-group spans +
  text; `groups[0]` is the whole match, `groups[i]` is capture group `i`;
  non-participating optional groups report `start = -1`. **Match spans are RUNE
  indices**, consistent with `std/string` (Go's byte offsets are converted).
- Compile is memoized Go-side (mutex-guarded cache keyed by pattern string) — pure
  memoization, invisible to AILANG semantics, deterministic. Axiom net +8
  (A1/A5/A9: linear-time removes the ReDoS footgun and keeps cost analyzable).
- Examples: `examples/regex_basics.ail`, `examples/regex_capture.ail`,
  `examples/regex_log_orchestration.ail` (the orchestration use case — parse +
  route structured log/LLM lines).

### Added — `std/net`: RFC-3986 URL parsing (`parseUrl` / `parseQuery`) (M-STDLIB-URL-PARSE)

- `std/net` gains two **pure** URL-parsing functions backed by Go's `net/url`
  (the reference RFC-3986 parser) — the *inverse complement* of the existing
  `urlEncode`/`urlEncodeForm`. This closes the **URL-parse half** of the v1.0
  orchestration bar (clause 4); the regex half shipped alongside (above).
- `parseUrl(s) -> Result[Url, string]` splits a URL into a flat record
  `Url = {scheme, host, port, path, query, fragment : string}`. `port` is `""`
  when absent (Go `url.Port()` semantics — no `-1`/`0` sentinel); `host` is the
  hostname only (no port, **no brackets for IPv6**: `http://[::1]:80/` →
  `host="::1"`); `path`/`fragment` are percent-decoded; `query` is the raw
  substring after `?` (still encoded — feed to `parseQuery`). Malformed input
  (invalid `%`-escape, control char, bad port) returns `Err(message)`, **never a
  panic** (CP2) — one fallible boundary, the record fields are then total.
- `parseQuery(s) -> [{name, value}]` decodes a query string into
  order-preserving `{name,value}` pairs (values percent-**DECODED**) — the inverse
  of `urlEncodeForm`. Deliberately **not** Go's `url.ParseQuery` (a sorted map):
  it parses the raw string to **preserve source order and duplicate keys**
  (`a=1&a=2` → two entries). A bare key (`flag`) → `{name:"flag", value:""}`;
  the empty string → `[]`. Total — never fails.
- Both `pure` (`! {}`, `IsPure: true`) — **no `Net` capability** despite living in
  `std/net` (parsing reaches no network); usable in contracts and pure code.
  `parseQuery(urlEncodeForm(pairs))` round-trips (modulo `urlEncodeForm`'s
  alphabetical key sort). Axiom net **+8**.
- Examples: `examples/url_parse_basics.ail` (parse + all six fields),
  `examples/url_route_dispatch.ail` (the orchestration use case — route on host,
  dispatch on path, branch on a query param).
- Design: [`m-stdlib-url-parse.md`](../design_docs/planned/v0_30_0/m-stdlib-url-parse.md).

### Added — Forgiving statement separators: newline-as-soft-separator in blocks (M-SYNTAX-AI-FORGIVING R2)

- A **newline** before a statement-starter (`let` / `letrec` / `if` / `match` /
  identifier) now separates statements inside a `{ }` block, not only `;` — the
  form small models naturally write. This relaxes the **PAR020** "missing ;"
  failure class to the genuinely ambiguous same-line case *without changing the
  model*.
- Additive and **backward-compatible**: a newline-separated block parses to the
  same `ast.Block` the `;`-separated block does. The rule is line-aware
  (`peek.Line > cur.Line`, the M-GAP2 precedent) AND requires a statement-starter,
  so multi-line *expressions* (`{ 1\n+ 2 }`, operator continuations) are never
  split. Record literals / record-updates use commas and are detected up front, so
  the newline rule never reaches a record body.
- **Systemic fix**: patched all block statement-loops — `parseFunctionBody`,
  `parseBlockOrExpression`, and both loops in `parseRecordLiteral` (the LBRACE-
  prefix path that `if/then` blocks and `\`-lambda bodies take). **PAR020** is
  preserved for the genuine same-line no-`;` case at both guard sites.
- Enforced by the same corpus AST-diff fuzz gate (`AILANG_CORPUS_FUZZ=1`): every
  currently-valid `benchmarks/**/*.ail` + `examples/**/*.ail` file (389 of 400)
  produces a byte-identical AST under R2. Follow-up: a canonical formatter is
  deferred to [`m-ailang-fmt.md`](../design_docs/planned/v0_29_0/m-ailang-fmt.md).
- Design: [`m-syntax-ai-forgiving.md`](../design_docs/implemented/v0_30_0/m-syntax-ai-forgiving.md).

### Added — Forgiving statement separators: `;`-sequences in `=` bodies (M-SYNTAX-AI-FORGIVING R1)

- The parser now accepts a `;`-separated statement sequence directly in an
  equation-form (`=`) function body — `func f() = s1; s2; e` — the form small
  models naturally write. This eliminates the **PAR017** parse-failure class
  (~32% of small-model failures, directional) *without changing the model*.
- Additive and **backward-compatible**: a multi-statement `=`-body parses to the
  same `ast.Block` a braced `{ ... }` body produces, so elaboration and typing are
  unchanged; a single-expression `=`-body is unchanged. The sequence stops at a
  top-level declaration boundary (`export | type | import | extern | @ | pure |
  func IDENT | EOF`), while an anonymous-function expression `func ( ... )` in the
  body is never cut (M-TAINT guard).
- Implementation: `parseEquationBody()` + `peekIsDeclBoundary()` in
  `internal/parser/parser_func.go`. Backward-compatibility is enforced by a corpus
  AST-diff fuzz gate (`internal/parser/corpus_astdiff_test.go`, run with
  `AILANG_CORPUS_FUZZ=1`): every currently-valid `benchmarks/**/*.ail` +
  `examples/**/*.ail` file (389 valid of 399) produces a byte-identical AST under
  the new parser. Example: `examples/runnable/syntax_ai_forgiving.ail`.
- Design: [`m-syntax-ai-forgiving.md`](../design_docs/implemented/v0_30_0/m-syntax-ai-forgiving.md).

### Fixed — Windows CI: riglock dead-holder steal used Unix-only PID probe

- `internal/riglock` split its holder-liveness probe into platform files:
  Unix keeps signal-0 (`pid_unix.go`); Windows now uses
  `OpenProcess`/`GetExitCodeProcess` semantics (`pid_windows.go`). The Unix-style
  code misread Windows' `os.FindProcess` error for any dead PID as "assume
  alive", so dead-holder locks were never stolen there —
  `TestAcquire_StealsDeadHolderLock` turned `test-windows` (CI + Build-and-Release)
  red on dev from d4cbcb5f7 through v0.29.2. Steal semantics stay conservative on
  both platforms: only positively-dead holders (`ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER`, or an
  openable handle whose exit code is not `STILL_ACTIVE`) are stolen;
  access-denied/inconclusive results count as alive.

### Fixed — module-less file silent-success footgun (`MOD014`) (M-MODLESS-FAIL-LOUD)

- A `.ail` file with top-level function declarations (`export func main …`) but
  **no `module` declaration** used to type-check clean, print `✓ Running`, and
  **exit 0 with no output** — the entry never ran, because a module-less file
  exports nothing and the runner fell through to a non-module print of unit. The
  worst possible signal for an AI author, and it silently sank any model-generated
  solution that omitted the module line (empty-stdout failures with no cause).
  This is exactly the "NO SILENT FALLBACKS — FAIL LOUDLY" anti-pattern.
- `validateModulePath` (the shared check/run/eval/test chokepoint) now emits
  **`MOD014`** when a module-less file has top-level `Funcs`:
  `Error MOD014: no 'module' declaration — this file has top-level declarations
  but no module, so nothing is exported and the entry never runs. Fix: add
  'module <canonical/path>' as the first line of the file`. Both `ailang run` and
  `ailang check` fail loudly (exit ≠ 0).
- Gated on top-level `Funcs` **only**: a bare-expression file (`1 + 1` → `2`) has
  no `Funcs` and still evaluates. (`MOD011` was already the module-path-collision
  code, so this uses the next fresh `MOD014`.)
- Remediated the one affected corpus file (`examples/runnable/block_demo.ail`,
  now `module examples/runnable/block_demo`). Added a footgun-coverage fixture
  (`internal/diag/footguns.md` + `footgun_fixtures_test.go`).

### Benchmark Results (M-EVAL, v0.30.0 baseline)

**Overall**: 80.9% success rate (1,810 standard runs), tier `core,stretch,frontier` (56 benchmarks).

**Standard eval** (0-shot + self-repair, 17 models):

| Metric | v0.30.0 |
|--------|---------|
| AILANG 0-shot | 73.2% (871/1189) |
| AILANG final (with repair) | 78.2% (930/1189) — repair worth +5.0pp |
| Python final | 83.6% (754/901) |
| AILANG↔Python gap | 5.4pp |

Top AILANG models: gemini-3-1-pro 89%, or-kimi-k3 88% (debut), gpt5-6-sol 86%.

**Agent eval** (multi-turn, AILANG-only, 5 non-Anthropic models): **78.5% (220/280)**.
opencode-or-deepseek-v4-flash 96% (vs 50% standard — the agent loop is worth +46pp to it),
opencode-or-deepseek-v4-pro 80%, opencode-or-glm-5-2 77%, opencode-or-glm-5-1 73%,
gpt5-6-luna 66%.

**Key findings:**

- **GLM-5.2's June "regression" was our harness truncating its thinking.** The always-thinking
  model runs 28–32K reasoning tokens; the old `max_output_tokens: 32768` guillotined it
  (6 `finish=length` failures, 3 with zero content). The harness previously recorded neither
  `reason_tokens` nor `finish_reason`, so this was invisible — both are now banked on every
  standard result, totals/cost include reasoning, and 5.2 got 64K headroom (OpenRouter's
  Baidu/StreamLake upstreams do not enforce `reasoning.max_tokens`; probed). **Re-verdict:
  5.2 beats 5.1 on standard AILANG (80% vs 77%), standard Python (81% vs 79%), and agent mode
  (77% vs 73%) at ~+43% price.** Residual: `gauntlet_10`/`quine` overthink to any ceiling.
- **`gpt5-6-luna` was the only agent-suite model without an explicit cost budget** — the
  default formula gave it $0.256 vs the tier's $0.30; parity recovered 10 agent passes
  (27→37/56). 18 runs still exceed $0.30 legitimately.
- **Claude data is partial** (Anthropic API quota exhausted mid-run; resets 2026-08-01):
  393/436 standard runs banked, no Claude agent runs. Clean-portion AILANG rates:
  sonnet-5 73%, fable-5 73%, opus-4-8 66%, sonnet-4-6 57% (interpret with care — the 43
  missing runs are quota holes, not failures). Notable: **claude-fable-5 refused 17/53
  Python benchmarks** (`stop_reason=refusal`) under the codegen system prompt while
  answering all AILANG variants — a Mythos-class safety-layer behavior worth tracking.
- Cost accounting fixed release-wide: reasoning tokens and repair-attempt tokens are now
  billed into `cost_usd` (both were silently dropped before).
- **Curation (next-sprint input)**: demote candidates saturated ≥95% on every measured
  dimension — `contract_bst_validate`, `contract_leap_year`, `effect_tracking_io_fs`,
  `float_eq`, `merge_sort`.

## [v0.29.2] - 2026-07-11

### Added — GPT-5.6 family in the eval suite (new-release tracking)

- `gpt5-6-terra` (OpenAI mid tier) and `gpt5-6-luna` (OpenAI fast tier) added to
  `internal/eval_harness/models.yml` and the extended suite. `models.yml` is embedded in
  the binary, so this cuts a clean tagged base that includes the two models for the
  upcoming eval run.

## [v0.29.1] - 2026-07-11

### Fixed — container deploys unblocked (MCP / dashboard / agents)

- All `docker/` Go builder base images bumped `golang:1.25-bookworm` → `golang:1.26-bookworm`
  to match `go.mod` (`go 1.26.5`). The MCP, dashboard, agent, and registry-validator Cloud
  Build deploys were failing at `go mod download` (`go >= 1.26.5` under `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`);
  CLI binary builds were unaffected (GitHub Actions uses `GOTOOLCHAIN=auto`). Restores the
  prod-MCP release deploy, which never rolled off v0.28.0 after v0.29.0. (dev-health #341)

### Changed — closed effect-mode set is now enforced (M-EFFECT-MODE-VALIDATION)

> **Pre-1.0 breaking narrowing (Experimental surface).** Parameterised effects are
> an Experimental v1.0 surface; this tightens which forms type-check. Programs using
> the legal v0.15.0 forms are unaffected.

Phase 1 (v0.15.0) ratified a **closed** mode set per effect, but the typechecker never
enforced it — `Rand[mode=banana]`, `Rand[flavor=hot]`, and `Clock[mode=pinned]` all
passed `ailang check` clean. Effect parameters are now validated against a frozen
per-effect **schema** at effect-row elaboration, producing three structured,
fix-carrying diagnostics:

- **`EFF_UNKNOWN_MODE`** — a param *value* outside the effect's allowed set
  (e.g. `Rand[mode=banana]`); the error lists the legal values.
- **`EFF_UNKNOWN_PARAM_KEY`** — a param *key* the effect doesn't define
  (e.g. `Rand[flavor=hot]`); the error lists the legal keys.
- **`EFF_PARAMS_NOT_SUPPORTED`** — an explicit param on a schema-less effect
  (e.g. `Clock[mode=pinned]`); names the tracking doc `m-effect-clock-net-fs-modes`
  so a future port unlocks the syntax.

Legal surface (frozen): `Rand[mode=os|seeded|crypto]`,
`AI[mode=fixed|routeable|replay-only]`, `AI[scope=byok]`. Every other effect accepts
its bare form only. Schema + `validateEffectParams` live in `internal/types/effects.go`;
the three diagnostics are CI-fixtured in `internal/diag/footgun_fixtures_test.go`; the
teaching prompt (`prompts/v0.16.2.md`) and the parameterised-effects guide now describe
the closed set as shipped fact.
### Added — GPT-5.6 family (Sol/Terra/Luna) gated into the eval suite; Sol promoted to flagship slot

OpenAI's GPT-5.6 family (released 2026-07-09) added to models.yml as `gpt5-6-sol` ($5/$30 per 1M),
`gpt5-6-terra` ($2.50/$15), `gpt5-6-luna` ($1/$6). All three cleared the standard smoke gate at
21/23 — an exact tie with the `claude-sonnet-4-6` anchor, whose 2 failures (`binary_tree_sum`,
`dense_operator_program`) mark those as benchmark-side issues, not model gaps. Core-tier
head-to-head vs incumbents: **Sol 26/26 (N=1) → tie with `gpt5-5` at 25/26 after N=3 de-flake of
`explicit_dataflow_ssa`** (both fail it 3/3) at identical price → Sol replaces `gpt5-5` in
`benchmark_suite`/`extended_suite` as a same-cost generational refresh (`gpt5-5` stays opt-in +
in `harness_suite`). Terra 24/26 at $1.82 ties the sonnet-4-6 anchor at ~70% of its cost. Luna
23/26 at $0.73 LOST to `gpt5-4-mini` (25/26, $0.55) — `dev_models` keeps mini. Terra and Luna
join `extended_suite` as well (new-release longitudinal tracking), so the full 5.6 family appears
in release baselines from the next run onward. (Note: gating ran pre-v0.29.0 re-tiering; the
26-benchmark core set referenced here is the pre-demotion set.)

### Fixed — rig lock no longer gates cloud-only eval runs; dead-holder locks are stolen

`ailang eval-suite` acquired the single-GPU rig lock unconditionally, so a cloud-only run
(OpenAI/Anthropic/Google/OpenRouter APIs) could be blocked by — or block — a local Ollama job it
shares nothing with. The lock is now acquired only when the resolved model list actually touches
the local GPU (new `ModelsConfig.UsesLocalGPU`: provider `ollama` or an `ollama/` agent model).
Separately, `riglock.Acquire` only stole locks older than the 6h staleness window; a holder that
exited via `os.Exit` (skipping its deferred release — observed 2026-07-11 after a completed
eval-suite) left the rig blocked for the remainder of the window. Acquire now also steals a lock
whose recorded holder PID is positively dead (signal-0 liveness probe; conservative on
EPERM/unreadable holder files). Both Go and shell (`rig-lock.sh`) holders write `PID timestamp`,
so the probe is valid for either.

## [v0.29.0] - 2026-07-11

### Added — `frontier` benchmark tier + curation (M-EVAL-FRONTIER-TIER)

Added a **`frontier` tier** to the eval suite to keep it discriminating at the top end
(the best model passes 36/37 AILANG `core`+`stretch` benchmarks — the suite had stopped
separating frontier models). A `frontier` benchmark's defining property: **at least one
frontier model fails it in standard mode**; if every frontier model passes, it demotes back
to `stretch`.

- **Tier machinery**: `frontier` added to `ValidTiers` (`internal/eval_harness/spec.go`),
  ordered after `stretch`. Tier filtering is centralized, so `ailang eval-suite --tier frontier`
  works automatically. `--tier` help, `benchmarks/CURATION.md` §1 tier table + §5 demotion rule,
  and `docs/docs/guides/evaluation/README.mdx` tier table all gain the `frontier` row.
- **8 benchmarks re-tiered** stretch→frontier per the design doc's 2026-07-08 authoring update:
  `ssa_constant_fold`, `bytecode_vm_trace`, `lfu_cache_trace`, `parse_prec_climb`,
  `effect_txn_rollback`, `glob_match_spec`, `dep_resolver_backtrack`, `stream_lcg_topk`.
  Each YAML carries a comment noting that **frontier-FAILURE validation is PARKED** (API-billed,
  out of scope for the headless mission loop). `spec_test.go` tier-distribution re-centered
  (stretch 30→22, frontier 0→8). `benchmarks/events.yml` gains a v0.29.0 taxonomy event.

### Changed — saturation demotion audit: 7 core benchmarks → stretch (M-EVAL-FRONTIER-TIER)

Ran a **4-dimension saturation demotion audit** (standard/agent × AILANG/Python)
over the 12 saturated core + 3 saturated stretch benchmarks, using **only banked
re-graded `eval_results/baselines/v0.25.0` data** (no new eval runs). Codified the
4-dimension rule into `benchmarks/CURATION.md` §5 as the source of truth; full
per-benchmark table in `design_docs/implemented/v0_29_0/m-eval-frontier-tier-demotion-audit.md`.

- **Demoted 7 core → stretch** (100% on all four dims): `audit_chain_replay`,
  `effect_composition`, `float_eq`, `graph_bfs`, `merge_sort`,
  `pattern_matching_complex`, `shadowing_heavy_contract`.
- **Kept 5** of the design doc's proposed 12 — the conservative rule protected real
  signal: `cli_args` is a strong agent-mode discriminator (agent-Python 0%);
  `api_call_json`/`error_handling`/`higher_order_functions`/`tree_transformation_pipeline`
  are not Python-saturated or show standard/agent disagreement.
- `symbolic_diff` (stretch) saturated on all four but is kept-for-coverage (low-ELO,
  comment added) per the doc — not retired.
- Tier counts: core 26→19, stretch 22→29 (`spec_test.go` re-centered).
  `benchmarks/events.yml` gains a v0.29.0 demotion event.

### Fixed — retire the free-text exact-match grading anti-pattern (M-EVAL-FRONTIER-TIER)

`decision_block_capture` graded a free-text justification sentence by **exact string
match** — it measured verbatim copying of the prompt's example, not capability. Added a
`prefix_line` **structural grading mode**: fixed output lines match exactly, but a
`PREFIX: ` placeholder line requires only that the actual output contain a
`PREFIX: <non-empty>` line (the rationale content is not matched).

- New centralized `GradeStdout(spec, actual, submittedSource)` router
  (`internal/eval_harness/runner.go`) — the grading policy (default / `quine` /
  `prefix_line`) now lives in ONE place. Threaded through all 6 grading call sites
  (repair.go + 5 in agent_runner*.go) that previously called `CompareOutput` /
  special-cased `quine` inline.
- `spec.go` grading enum accepts `prefix_line`; unit tests cover pass (any non-empty
  CHOICE), fail (missing/empty CHOICE, wrong number), and mode routing.
- Anti-pattern audit of all stretch + frontier benchmarks: only `decision_block_capture`
  genuinely graded prose by exact match; the other flagged benchmarks
  (`contract_sorted_merge`, `docx_reimplement`, `legal_obligation_engine`, `lfu_cache_trace`)
  are false positives — their expected output is fixed deterministic data.

### Added / Docs — The 1.x Stability Promise + LIMITATIONS accuracy pass (M-V1-STABILITY-PROMISE)

Published the **1.x stability promise** (`docs/docs/reference/stability.md`): three tiers
(**Stable** / **Experimental** / **Internal**), the 1.x compatibility semantics (no breaking
changes to the Stable surface within 1.x minors; Experimental may change with a CHANGELOG notice;
deprecations live ≥1 minor before removal), and full tier tables enumerating all 42 `std/*.ail`
modules + `std/ai/streaming` and every top-level CLI command/family. Stable syntax is stated by
reference to the canonical embedded prompt (v0.16.2). The page carries a
`RATIFICATION: pending (Mark, at release)` stamp; ambiguous tier rows are marked `⚠ proposed`.
Linked from `README.md`, `docs/docs/intro.mdx`, and the docs sidebar.

**LIMITATIONS accuracy pass** — every entry re-verified live at HEAD
(`v0.28.0-141-g379990ad5`, 2026-07-10) with `ailang check`/`run` transcripts. The published
`docs/docs/reference/limitations.md` is now the canonical page (each open entry carries a
`Verified at` date; fixed items moved to a re-verified "Resolved" list); the root

pointer + summary. Corrected entries that documented **fixed** behavior as broken: polymorphic
arithmetic lambdas (`add(3.14)(2.71)` → `5.85`), `match` in block-body lambdas passed to HOFs
(`[zero, ok, ok]`), and multi-statement block expressions. Deleted 3 stale in-file past-version

**Stale website version-promises fixed** — the four inventoried "planned for v0.x" statements

### Added — Footgun coverage: 4 fix-carrying diagnostics promoted to `covered` with CI fixtures (M-DIAG-FIXTURE-PROMOTION)

Promoted four footgun rows from `inventoried` to `covered` in the coverage table
(`internal/diag/footguns.md`) by adding CI fixtures in
`internal/diag/footgun_fixtures_test.go` that assert each diagnostic's code **and**
its fix-carrying substring: reserved-keyword-as-name (`PAR_RESERVED_KEYWORD`),
hyphen-in-module-path (`PAR_HYPHEN_IN_MODULE`), `;`-in-expression-body-func
(`PAR017`), and stdlib-func-used-without-import (the `ImportSuggester` "exported by
std/…; add the matching import" hint). The stdlib-import fixture wires
`AILANG_STDLIB_PATH` + `types.ImportSuggester` via a `TestMain` so it exercises the
same hint an agent's `ailang check` sees. The `%`/Fractional row is held at
`inventoried`: its table-claimed diagnostic is not reachable via `ailang check` on
an `.ail` snippet (only via the internal `InstanceEnv.Lookup` unit test), so it is
not fixturable without new diagnostic work. Part of m-diagnostic-coverage (R1.1).

### Fixed — contract codegen emitted uncompilable Go on Windows (path escapes in panic literals)

`internal/gen/golang/contracts.go` interpolated `Contract.Message`/`Contract.Location`
raw into the generated `panic("…")` string literal. `Location` carries an absolute
source path, so on Windows the backslashes became invalid Go escape sequences and the
generated file failed to compile — `TestContractViolation_Integration` broke the
`test-windows` CI job as soon as the stale-binary guard + anti-silent-skip CI gate let
it actually run there. A `Message` containing `"` or `\` broke codegen identically on
every OS. Both panic sites (requires/ensures) now `strconv.Quote` the fully-formatted
message (fixed on dev in 9d2e32ac1; this entry documents it). Output is byte-identical
for clean messages. Regression tests cover the requires path with a Windows-style
location (`TestGenerateContractRequiresChecks_WindowsPathLocation`) and — added here —
the ensures emission path plus quote-containing messages, validated via `go/format`
(`TestGenerateContractChecks_EscapesMessageAndLocation`).

### Changed — Bump `github.com/ollama/ollama` 0.21.2 → 0.31.1; project toolchain → go 1.26 (Dependabot #320)

Took the Dependabot ollama bump. The blocker was never an API break — all four call-sites
(`internal/ai/ollama/{client,step}.go`, `internal/builtins/ollama_embed.go`,
`internal/messaging/embedder.go`) compile and test green unchanged. ollama ≥ 0.23.1 raised its
own `go` directive to `go 1.26.0`, which propagated into ours; CI pinned go 1.25.12 with
`GOTOOLCHAIN=local` failed at `make deps` (`go.mod requires go >= 1.26.0`). Resolved by moving the
whole project toolchain to **go 1.26.5** — the `go` directive plus all 14 workflow `go-version`
pins (`build.yml`, `ci.yml`, `release.yml`, `docusaurus-deploy.yml`, `eval-weekly.yml`,
`test-game-codegen.yml`). `go mod tidy` also pruned the heavy ML transitive deps (gorgonia,
pdevine/tensor, google/flatbuffers) that only ollama's non-`api` packages pull — we import only
`ollama/api`. No security advisory motivated the ollama bump itself (no ollama GHSA, no repo
Dependabot alert). The specific patch **1.26.5** matters: the `crypto/tls` fix for GO-2026-5856
(CVE-2026-42505, "Encrypted Client Hello privacy leak") landed in go 1.25.12 and 1.26.5 — pinning
1.26.4 would regress the stdlib below the fix and trip the `govulncheck` gate, so the toolchain is
pinned to 1.26.5.


### Added — Feedback gate cloud adapter: Firestore stores + live classifier wired (M-FEEDBACK-GATE-CLOUD-ADAPTER)

Operationally closes the P0 shipped by M-FEEDBACK-TRIAGE-GATE: the merged gate's
cooldown, classifier, and budget stages were injected dependencies that nothing
constructed in production, so cloud protection was rules-only. This wires them
live. New `internal/storage/firestore` adapters — `FeedbackGateCooldownStore`
(collection `feedback_gate_cooldown`, per-contact sliding window of dispatch-
attempt timestamps, hashed doc IDs, transactional trim→append→write, saturation-
capped at 64 to bound doc size under flood) and `FeedbackGateBudgetStore`
(collection `feedback_gate_budget`, per-UTC-day classifier call counter). All
window math lives in the pure, table-tested `trimAndCount` (half-open 24h window:
an attempt exactly at now−24h is kept), so no Firestore emulator is needed.
The coordinator CLI (`ailang coordinator start`, cloud mode) now constructs these
stores plus the real Anthropic classifier and hands them to the daemon via
`Daemon.SetFeedbackGateDeps`; `initTaskProcessing` attaches them onto the gate
config only when `coordinator.feedback_gate.enabled` is set. **Fail-closed** when
`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is absent: the classifier is built with a nil provider (a
LOUD startup warning), so heuristic-flagged submissions are filed for human
triage and never dispatched. The startup log now names all three live stages
(`cooldown=firestore`, `classifier=anthropic|fail-closed|none`,
`budget=firestore`). Nil deps (local mode) leave every stage a no-op — behavior
identical to the pre-adapter, rules-only gate. Rollout is DRY-RUN-first (see the
Feedback Cost & Abuse Gate runbook in the coordinator guide); the Firestore TTL
policy on `expires_at` and the `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` secret are a documented
terraform handoff (sibling `ailang-multivac` repo), not part of this change.

### Added — CI: z3 on the runner + anti-silent-skip gate for binary-shelling integration tests

An audit of the last green dev CI log (run 29092834899's predecessor) confirmed the
ailang-binary-gated integration tests (internal/pkg smoke runner, cmd/ailang prompt/pipe,
internal/gen/golang contract violations, eval_harness runner, …) already **run and PASS** in
CI — `make install` (Linux) / `go install` (Windows) precede `go test ./...` and the runner's
PATH includes GOPATH/bin. What actually never ran: every **Z3-dependent** test
(internal/smt `TestSolve_*_Z3`/`TestZ3Version`, internal/bestof `TestZ3VerifyEndToEnd`/
`TestZ3CatchesRuntimePassingButUnprovable`) skipped with "Z3 not installed" in every run —
the contract-verification tier (M-CONTRACT-GUIDED-EVAL) had zero CI signal.

- ci.yml (Linux test job): install `z3` via apt before the test step, so the SMT/contract
  integration tests execute for real.
- ci.yml (both jobs): new "Assert binary-gated integration tests ran" step — re-runs one fast
  representative test per external-binary gate (`TestRunSmokeInTempDir_Pass`,
  `TestPromptCommand_Piping`, `TestZ3VerifyEndToEnd`; Windows minus z3) verbosely and fails
  unless each shows `--- PASS`. `go test ./...` is non-verbose, so without this a skip is
  invisible and CI stays green while integration coverage silently evaporates (e.g. if the
  build/install steps are removed or reordered). Also arms the staleness guard landing in
  `internal/testutil` (ailangbin.go): if it ever misfires in CI, this step turns the silent
  skip into a red build.
### Fixed — `prepare-embed` is now content-aware; classifier prompt mirror committed

`make prepare-embed` (make/build.mk) only re-copied `prompts/` into
`cmd/ailang/prompts/` when `prompts/versions.json` had a **newer mtime** than
the mirror's copy — so any prompt file added or edited without touching
`versions.json` left the committed mirror stale, and a fresh checkout embedded
the stale mirror into the binary. Concretely, `prompts/feedback_gate_classifier.md`
(M-FEEDBACK-TRIAGE-GATE M3) never made it into the committed mirror and showed
up as untracked noise after every `make build`. The sync check is now
content-based (`diff -rq` for the prompts tree, `cmp -s` for the axiom
scorecard), which also catches deletions, and the missing mirror copy is
committed. Note the feedback-gate classifier itself was never at risk: it
reads its own committed embed (`internal/feedbackgate/feedback_gate_classifier.md`),
not the `cmd/ailang` prompts FS, and nothing serves the classifier prompt
through that FS (`prompts/versions.json` has no entry for it).

### Fixed — stale-binary guard for integration tests (internal/testutil)

A stale `bin/ailang` (a v0.26.0 June build while HEAD was v0.28.x) made
`TestRunSmokeInTempDir_Pass/_Isolation` fail with phantom stdlib errors
("undefined variable: _io_flush at std/io.ail:29:36") that looked like a merge
regression and cost real debugging time (2026-07-10). Root cause was a class,
not one site: `internal/pkg`'s `findAilangBinary` preferred `bin/ailang` with
no freshness check, `internal/gen/golang` used `bin/ailang` directly (even
self-building an unversioned one into the repo), and ~12 more test files
trusted a bare `exec.LookPath("ailang")` — a stale PATH install produces the
same phantom failures or false greens. Systemic fix:

- New `internal/testutil` package owns binary discovery for all integration
  tests: `FindAilangBinary` (prefers `bin/ailang`, falls back to PATH) for
  tests that exec the returned path, and `RequireAilangOnPath` for tests whose
  code under test hardcodes the bare `"ailang"`. Both **skip loudly** — naming
  the stale binary, the newest source file, and the rebuild command — when the
  binary is older than the newest `*.go` under `cmd/`+`internal/` (or
  `go.mod`/`go.sum`). Mtime is used instead of `--version` vs `git describe`
  because version stamps can't see uncommitted edits and exact-describe
  matching false-positives in dirty worktrees.
- All 14 test files in the class migrated to the shared helpers; the
  incident site keeps a regression note.
- `make test` now depends on `make build`, so the blessed path always tests a
  freshly built `bin/ailang` (raw `go test` still works — tests skip with the
  rebuild hint instead of running a stale binary).

### Added — Feedback cost & abuse gate on the cloud dispatch path (M-FEEDBACK-TRIAGE-GATE)

A deterministic-first cost & abuse gate between the coordinator's cloud Pub/Sub pickup
(`pollAndProcessTasksCloud`) and `CreateTask`, so a flood of anonymous public
`submit_feedback` submissions can't fan out to Sonnet-driven agents. Defense in depth on top
of the Cloud Armor per-IP edge throttle and the `task_max_cost` backstop.

New package `internal/feedbackgate`: `Decide(ctx, Input, FeedbackGateConfig) (Verdict, error)`
composes three stages — (1) pure deterministic rules (auto: prefix → file; body >8KB → reject;
spam >5 URLs / ≥1KB base64 → reject; untrusted sender → reject; unknown inbox → reject; unknown
category → file), (2) a per-contact **sliding cooldown** (3/hour, 10/day) behind a fake-able
`CooldownStore`, and (3) a last-resort **Haiku JSON classifier** via an injected `ai.Provider`
(native JSON mode + schema) that is skipped for `agent-*` senders and **fails closed** (any
parse/schema/provider error → file, never dispatch). A daily classifier **budget cap** (default
$5/day, `coordinator.feedback_gate.daily_budget_usd`) short-circuits the classifier to file when
exceeded. Wired into the coordinator behind `coordinator.feedback_gate.enabled` (**off by
default → zero behavior change**); a filed/rejected verdict suppresses dispatch, emits a
structured `feedback-gate-audit` inbox record (no silent drops), and — for reject — acks the
source message non-destructively (TTL cleanup). Operator controls: env
`AILANG_FEEDBACK_GATE_MODE=off|file-only|full` and `AILANG_FEEDBACK_GATE_DRY_RUN=1` (run the gate,
audit the would-be verdict, always dispatch). Ships an offline flood-drill
(`scripts/security/feedback_flood_drill.sh` + `RunFloodDrill`) that simulates N submissions and
prints a verdict histogram + simulated spend — no cloud creds, no live API, no Ollama.

**Naming disambiguation (important):** this is DISTINCT from the shipped
**M-MSG-TRIAGE-ROUTER** (`internal/coordinator/triage_router.go`, config key `coordinator.triage`,
type `coordinator.Decision` Hold/Promote/Drop, `TriageConfig`, `classify()`), which does local
intake-inbox → design-doc-creator promotion. To avoid entanglement, this feature deliberately uses
`internal/feedbackgate`, `Verdict` (dispatch/file/reject), `FeedbackGateConfig`,
`coordinator.feedback_gate`, `AILANG_FEEDBACK_GATE_*`, and the `feedback-gate-audit` inbox — none of
the M-MSG-TRIAGE-ROUTER names are reused or extended.

Out-of-scope follow-ups (not in this change): the terraform 80%-budget alert lives in the sibling
`ailang-multivac` repo; a dashboard feedback-gate panel + `ailang messages list --show-triage` flag
(the audit data already lands in `feedback-gate-audit`); the real cloud flood drill with live spend
measurement; and the Firestore-backed cooldown/classifier store adapters (the in-repo default gate
runs rules-only until the cloud adapter attaches them).

### Added — V1 mission: scheduled outer loop that works the backlog toward v1.0.0 (M-V1-MISSION)

A second long-running mission (peer of `motoko-mission.md`): a nightly, rig-scheduled outer loop
that works `design_docs/planned/` toward a v1.0.0 release through the existing honed inner-loop
skills. New artifacts: `design_docs/v1-mission.md` (charter: v1.0-bar draft, model routing
policy, two-tier GPU rule, guardrails, ordered queue), `design_docs/v1-mission-log.md`
(append-only per-iteration log with routing-evidence + ruled-out ledger; entry 0 records the
backlog census — 66 docs v0_29_0 / 27 v1_0_0, no written v1.0 criteria existed, 6 open P0s),
`.claude/skills/mission-control/SKILL.md` (one gated iteration: preflight → observe → pick with
reality-check → route/execute → record → retro), and launchd wiring
(`tools/launchd/mission-control.sh` + `dev.ailang.mission-control.plist`, 22:00 nightly, 6h bash
watchdog since the rig has no GNU timeout, kill switch at
`~/.ailang/state/mission-control.disabled`) — written but NOT armed; installation is a
deliberate human step. Key policies: cloud-only iterations never take `rig.lock` (it is a GPU
mutex only; GPU-touching steps lock per-step), model routing is evidence-updated (Fable
coordinates/judges, Opus plans/executes, Sonnet only for verified-mechanical work), and Gate-5
retro closes the loop that never existed: sprint retros/evaluator reports now route into skill
edits (max one per iteration, ≥2 frictions each), process fixes, or backlog items.

### Fixed — dev-cycle agent no longer pins sonnet for the whole dev workflow

`.claude/agents/dev-cycle.md` pinned `model: sonnet` — the only model directive in the entire
skill pipeline — so orchestrated design→plan→execute cycles ran compiler-grade work on sonnet
(observed as the "corrections needed" false economy; coordinator-helper's own guidance says
opus for execution). Now `model: opus`, consistent with the V1-mission routing policy.

### Fixed — M-TYPEENV-SUB type-safety hole already closed; locked in with regression guards (P0)

The P0 hole "a function returning an imported ADT (Result/Option/Json) has its return type
erased to a type variable, so downstream call sites silently type-check invalid programs"
(design doc `m-typeenv-sub-fix.md`, triggered by `device_auth.ail` accepting `decode(jsonValue)`)
is **no longer reproducible** in the current tree. Empirically: `decode(jo([]))`,
`needsString(jo([]))`, and a local `wrap() -> Result[int,string]` used as `string` all now
produce the correct compile-time type error, deterministically (`-count=20`), including the full
interface serialise→deserialise roundtrip (an EXPORTED `Result`-returning function misused as
`string` in a separate downstream module). The hole was closed as a side effect of the
**M-TYPE-LIST-SOUND series (by round 3, `66aceed79`)** — evaluator-verified by running the
un-skipped tests across history: they FAIL at `ebd23a67c` (2026-03-27, where the bug was
documented live) and PASS at round 3's tree (generalization-withholding stops the escaping
metavar being over-generalized into the exported scheme). `e67e24cb1` (M-TVAR-COLLISION-FIX)
predates the live-bug documentation, so it was at most a precondition, not the closing change.
The design doc's stated root cause (`InferWithConstraints`
never applying the solve substitution to the returned `TypeEnv`) technically still holds, but the
generalization site no longer emits the offending `∀a. … -> a` scheme, so no observable soundness
loss remains. Accordingly the planned high-risk repair (alpha-rename every scheme on env insertion
+ post-solve current-binding repair in `internal/types`) was **not implemented** — it would modify
shared compilation infrastructure to fix a nonexistent bug. Instead the three parked regression
tests in `internal/pipeline/type_safety_test.go` were un-skipped (they pass) and a new
`TestTypeSafety_CrossModule_ExportedADTRoundtrip` was added to guard the interface-builder path the
originals miss. If a future generalization-site change re-opens the hole, these guards trip loudly.

### Added — WASM bridge accepts structured arguments (arrays/records) via the shared argdecode path

`ailangCall` previously converted only scalar JS arguments ("unsupported argument type: object"),
forcing JSON-string workarounds for any structured export (discovered integrating the
`sunholo/deontic` engine client-side). Structured JS values now round-trip through
`JSON.stringify` into a new natively-tested `argdecode.DecodeJSONUntyped` — the SAME package
serve-api uses, so both hosts share one decoder. Objects → records (structural, unify at
application), arrays → lists, integral numbers → ints (matching the existing scalar heuristic;
tuples remain unsupported). Measured on the deontic PPA demo under Node: structured
`analyzeContract` call 8.4ms, 8.9ms per what-if recompute, output byte-identical to serve-api
(20ms/request) and the CLI. NOTE: the shipped `docs/static/wasm/ailang.wasm` artifact is from
May and predates this — refresh at next release (post-release flow).

### Fixed — SCC call-graph exhaustiveness: false "undefined variable" for locals in record-update fields, match guards, array elements (#327, M-DOGFOOD-FIXES M2)

Root cause of #327 (found from the preserved deontic artifact after clean-room repros failed):
`findReferences` (`internal/elaborate/scc.go`) built the intra-module dependency graph with a
non-exhaustive AST traversal — no cases for `RecordUpdate` (base + field values), `Array`
elements, or match `Guard` clauses. A local function referenced ONLY from such a position got no
call-graph edge, Tarjan's SCC ordering emitted its declaration after its caller's, and the
strictly-in-order core type checker reported a false "undefined variable" (order-independent at
the source level, which is why small repros passed — their orderings happened to be benign).
Fixed by making the traversal exhaustive over every expression variant, with the default branch
panicking under DEBUG_STRICT instead of silently dropping edges. The audit-first fixture matrix
mandated by the design doc (4 callee kinds × 10 positions, `internal/pipeline/
record_update_positions_test.go`) caught the two extra latent positions before the fix shipped.
The deontic package's hoist-to-let workaround is now unnecessary (un-hoisted engine verified
green); it will be removed from ailang-packages after this ships in a release.

### Added — error-time teaching, first shipment: PAR_IMPORT_PLACEMENT + #327 truth-telling + footgun coverage table (M-DIAGNOSTIC-COVERAGE / R1.1, M-DOGFOOD-FIXES M3)

- **PAR_IMPORT_PLACEMENT (#325):** an `import` after any declaration now produces ONE
  fix-carrying diagnostic ("imports must appear immediately after the module declaration →
  move this import above the first type/func declaration") instead of a cascade of
  PAR_NO_PREFIX_PARSE errors. The eval harness gained a matching repair rule ordered BEFORE
  the generic PAR_001 so self-repair receives the placement fix, not a semicolon hint.
  Causal evidence for prioritization: this footgun cost haiku-4-5 an entire benchmark cell
  and hit the deontic package's own author three times in one sprint.
- **#327 interim honesty:** "undefined variable: X" now appends a truth-telling hint (defined
  in this module, not resolvable in this position, let-binding workaround) — gated strictly on
  X actually being a module-level function. With the M2 root fix landing simultaneously this
  path should no longer fire; it remains as a tripwire for any future ordering regression.
- **Footgun coverage table:** `internal/diag/footguns.md` — 14 entries inventoried from the
  teaching prompt's Common Mistakes + LIMITATIONS.md, every current diagnostic live-verified
  verbatim; `footgun_fixtures_test.go` CI-asserts diagnostic code + fix substring per covered
  entry. Prompt-line deletion remains gated on the rig A/B per the design doc.

### Fixed — named `test "name" { }` blocks execute; skipped suites can no longer report success (M-NAMED-TEST-BLOCKS, M-DOGFOOD-FIXES M1)

Named test blocks parsed but were never executed (`runner.go`: "For now, skip these"), and the
reporter printed "All tests passed!" over all-skipped suites — a deliberately failing test
reported success, and every shipped package test file (duckdb, deontic) had been skipped since
creation. Now: named blocks evaluate (pass = body is `true`; `false`/runtime error = FAIL with
detail), skip reasons are visible in human output, "All tests passed!" requires something to have
run, all-skipped suites print "NO TESTS RAN" and exit non-zero (escape hatch: `--allow-skips`).
Integration hardening after the first merge attempt panicked on a real package: the test-body
source printer is exhaustive over AST variants and per-test execution is recover()-guarded, so a
bad body fails that test instead of crashing the runner. Acceptance: the deontic package's five
ground-truth tests pass for real via `ailang test --package .`.


### Added — sunholo/deontic 0.1.0: the contracts-as-code engine ships as an extension package (M-DEONTIC-PKG)

Executed the extension-routing decision on m-contracts-as-code-vertical (user-prioritized for
Aitana): the wave-5 deontic engine is now a reusable package in ailang-packages
(`packages/deontic`) — pure `runEvents` event fold over Event/Term ADTs with Policy/State record
aliases; settlement arithmetic 6/6 Z3-VERIFIED (`ailang verify`); ground-truth demo byte-identical
to the cross-validated `legal_obligation_engine` benchmark output; effect ceiling demonstrates A4
(shrinking `[effects].max` rejects even the demo's println at the manifest). Dogfooding found two
things: **#327** (new: local function in a record-update field resolves as "undefined variable"
under cross-module package conditions; hoist-to-let workaround applied) and a pre-existing
`ailang test` limitation (package-relative test modules are silently SKIPPED — duckdb's tests
skip identically; the runnable demo diff is the enforced gate). Also: the author hit the #325
import-placement footgun three separate times while writing this package — R1 error-time teaching
keeps earning its priority.


### Added — wave-5 follow-up measurements + contracts-as-code flagship proposal (M-EVAL-FRONTIER-TIER close-out)

Three follow-ups from the frontier-benchmark campaign, with results recorded for the next baseline:
**(1) Mid-tier language delta** (`eval_results/delta_probe_midtier/`, haiku-4-5 / gpt5-mini /
gpt5-1-instant on `legal_obligation_engine`): standard-mode hypothesis REJECTED — Python 2/3,
AILANG 1/3. But the failure MODES confirm the mechanism: haiku's Python computed every value
correctly and failed silently on an extra header line (unrepairable logic_error), while its
AILANG failed loudly on the #325 import-placement footgun and burned its one repair on an
unexplained diagnostic. Refined falsifiable prediction: the AILANG advantage appears in AGENT
mode, where the compile loop iterates — to be measured in rotation. #325 upgraded with this
evidence (one diagnostic plausibly flips a whole benchmark cell for the mid-tier class).
**(2) gauntlet_10 pass@5** (`eval_results/gauntlet_passk/`, fable, temp 1.0): **10/10 across both
languages** — the conjunction hypothesis is dead at 10 sections for the frontier, and AILANG is
at reliability parity with Python on the suite's hardest benchmark (a strong "AILANG is as easy
for frontier AI as Python" data point). **(3) The R6 flagship proposal**:
`design_docs/planned/v0_29_0/m-contracts-as-code-vertical.md` — "Contracts as Code: a verified
deontic engine" (ADT-unrepresentable states, Z3-proved settlement math — verified live:
`✓ VERIFIED addCap [31ms]`, Z3 4.15.4 — replayable event fold, capability gating), porting the
wave-5 reference into a docs-site vertical. Two Design Freeze items await Mark.

### Added — gauntlet_10: conjunction benchmark, and the wave-4 verdict on defeating the frontier (M-EVAL-FRONTIER-TIER, wave 4)

Ten validated hard sections re-parameterized with fresh constants (isomorphic relabelings provably
preserve each section's divergence traps; new "0110" automaton brute-verified; fresh emphasis
vectors re-validated 12/12 against cmark), one program, 67 exact output lines, all-or-nothing.
Probe history is the finding: the FIRST probe defeated fable on both cells — but forensics showed
one failure was an AUTHORING bug (the compact `pattern/string` glob notation was ambiguous for
patterns containing `/`; fable's matcher was correct under the other legal reading, 66/67 lines
right) and the other was ambiguity + a timeout of a correct-but-~20×-slower AILANG program
(S7 was too heavy; ref now 1.1s = 27× headroom per the sizing rule). After fixing the benchmark's
own defects, fable passes both cells 67/67 first-try; sonnet fails both. Wave 1→4 conclusion,
recorded for the ELO era: at temp-0 pass@1 with adaptive thinking, single traps do not defeat the
authoring-class model (computation is delegated, self-reference is routed through quine machinery,
specs are followed, conjunction-at-10 is survived at ~0.99+/section); the discriminating axes that
remain are RELIABILITY (pass@k over trials), sheer conjunction length, and compute-wall search
tasks — plus everything in the suite cleanly separating mid-tier from frontier today.

### Added — quine grading mode + 2 self-reference benchmarks targeting frontier logic errors (M-EVAL-FRONTIER-TIER, wave 3)

Wave 3 answers "what would the strongest model fail with a LOGIC error, one-shot?" — honestly:
things iterative authoring can verify but a single forward pass cannot produce. New harness
capability: `grading: quine` compares stdout against the SUBMITTED SOURCE (normalized both sides) —
the only deterministic grader for self-reproducing programs; plus `min_bytes` (blocks the
degenerate empty quine) and `max_char_run` source constraints. New benchmarks: `quine` (Python
quines are memorized; the AILANG side has zero training priors and demands exact fixed-point
escaping reasoning with no test run — file-read cheats banned by substring scan + no FS
capability) and `emit_exact_bytes_varied` (exactly 512 source bytes with NO 3+ char runs — kills
the uniform-padding trick fable used to pass the 256-byte version; the padding itself must be
varied text counted byte-exactly at generation time). The AILANG reference quine was built by
scripted fixpoint construction (1,455 bytes, verified stdout==source); wave-3 prompts pre-flighted
against fable's refusal classifier — Python cells will read `refused` (consistent with #326), the
AILANG cells (long teaching-prompt context) run and are the measuring cells.

### Added — `refused` error category: API-level model refusals are model behavior, not api_error

The wave-2 probe surfaced that Fable 5's safety layer **deterministically refuses** three of the
four constrained-construction prompts in short contexts (Anthropic `stop_reason: "refusal"`, empty
content, HTTP 200) — while the same tasks pass when preceded by the long AILANG teaching prompt.
The harness recorded these as `api_error` ("empty response from Claude"), conflating "model
declined" with "API broke". Now: the anthropic client surfaces `stop_reason=refusal` as a typed
error, and both eval paths (standard + agent) categorize it as `refused` — visible to capability
scoring and rotation instead of being lumped with infrastructure noise. Wave-2 probe scoreboard
(1 trial/cell): sonnet-4-6 passes only `binary_strings_1e18` (2/8 cells; `emit_exact_bytes` fails
with `constraint_violation` even after repair, both `digitless_constants`/`commonmark_emphasis`
fail on logic in Python); fable-5 passes all 5 measurable cells first-try (4 AILANG + 1 Python)
with 3 Python cells refused. The suite now clearly separates the two tiers.

### Added — source-constraint grading + 4 delegation-proof frontier benchmarks (M-EVAL-FRONTIER-TIER, wave 2)

The sonnet-4-6 probe of wave 1 (13/16 cells passing) exposed a structural ceiling: fully-specified
algorithmic benchmarks test *spec-following*, which mid-tier models are drilled on — the model
delegates all computation to the program it writes. Wave 2 puts difficulty where delegation can't
reach. New harness capability: `source_constraints` in benchmark YAML (exact_bytes / max_bytes /
banned_chars / banned_substrings) grades the program TEXT before execution, with a new
`constraint_violation` error category and precise violation messages routed into self-repair
(`internal/eval_harness/source_constraints.go`). New benchmarks: `emit_exact_bytes` (source must be
EXACTLY 256 bytes — byte-precise self-awareness of the model's own emission), `digitless_constants`
(print 1000000007/823543/1729 with zero digit characters in source), `commonmark_emphasis` (full
delimiter-stack '*' rules incl. rule-of-3 on novel vectors — every expected output cross-validated
against the cmark reference binary; superficial Markdown priors mislead), `binary_strings_1e18`
(count length-10^18 binary strings avoiding "101" mod 1e9+7 — O(n) DP is impossible in the 30s
budget in EITHER language, forcing automaton + O(log n) matrix exponentiation; second exponent
deliberately structureless after a first draft hit the Frobenius fixed point f(p)=f(1)). All 8
reference solutions (2 languages × 4) verified byte-identical to expected_stdout; tier-distribution
tripwires re-centered (stretch 22 → 26).

### Added — 8 frontier-class discriminatory benchmarks (M-EVAL-FRONTIER-TIER, authoring phase)

The v0.25.0 baseline showed the suite no longer discriminates at the top end (41% of benchmarks
saturated; claude-fable-5 passes 36/37 AILANG). Authored the first 8 benchmarks from the
M-EVAL-FRONTIER-TIER candidate list, each targeting a difficulty axis that defeats pattern-matching
priors rather than formatting: `ssa_constant_fold` (5 composed IR passes), `bytecode_vm_trace`
(relative-jump/step-count off-by-ones), `lfu_cache_trace` (LFU+LRU tie-break state), `parse_prec_climb`
(unary-minus-between-*-and-^ precedence), `effect_txn_rollback` (checkpoint pop-on-fail semantics),
`glob_match_spec` (deviates from fnmatch: `*` never crosses `/`), `dep_resolver_backtrack`
(greedy-highest dead-ends; needs real backtracking), `stream_lcg_topk` (exact LCG + top-k + checksum).
Every `expected_stdout` was computed by a Python reference implementation AND reproduced exactly by a
hand-verified AILANG solution (`ailang run`); plausible-wrong implementations were checked to diverge
(e.g. 3 classic LFU bugs, greedy resolver, fnmatch). Tiered `stretch` (the `frontier` tier doesn't
exist in `ValidTiers` yet) so they enter the next core+stretch rotation automatically.
(design_docs/planned/m-eval-frontier-tier.md)

### Fixed — unresolved uppercase identifier in pattern position was a silent catch-all (#323)

Originally filed as "imported `std/list.nth` returns None inside recursive functions" — root cause
was neither `nth` nor recursion. In `elaboratePattern` (`internal/elaborate/patterns.go`), a bare
identifier not found in the elaborator's constructor table silently fell back to a **variable
pattern**, which matches everything. So in any module that didn't import Option's constructors
(e.g. only `import std/list (nth)`), `match nth(xs, i) { None => ..., Some(v) => ... }` bound the
`Some(7)` value to a variable named "None" and always took the first arm — while Some-first arm
order happened to work, making the bug look recursion/nth-shaped. Fix: an uppercase identifier in
pattern position now ALWAYS elaborates as a nullary constructor pattern (constructors are enforced
UpperCamelCase at declaration, so uppercase-as-variable is never legitimate), matching how
constructor patterns *with* arguments already behaved; and a bare KNOWN non-nullary constructor
(e.g. `Some` without arguments) is now a compile error carrying the fix instead of a silent
catch-all. Regression coverage: elaborate unit tests, `examples/option_pattern_import_free.ail`
(run by verify-examples), and `benchmarks/frontier_refs/bytecode_vm_trace.ail` (None-first +
unimported Option + recursion, byte-exact expected output). Tier-distribution drift tests
re-centered for the 8 new stretch benchmarks (stretch 14 → 22).

### Added — consumer regression guardrail in CI (M-RIG-RELIABILITY)

A CI gate (`tools/ci/motoko_smoke.sh`) that `ailang check`s a vendored corpus of real-world
consumer AILANG constructs (escapes incl `\uXXXX`, ADTs, record-payload constructors, match,
effects) on every PR — and the real motoko harness core when the fork is present (rig). The
v0.27.0 `\uXXXX` lexer break stalled the rig ~35h with zero CI signal; this is that missing gate.
Verified: it FAILS on a reintroduced lexer break (corpus + motoko core), PASSES on good code.
(design_docs/planned/m-eval-rig-reliability.md)


### Added — tuple- and function-type aliases (`type Handler = (Req) -> Resp`) (M-PARSER-ALIAS-TARGETS)

`type Pair = (int, string)` and `type Pred = (int) -> bool` previously did not parse
(`PAR_TYPE_BODY_EXPECTED` at the `(`) — the type-decl body parser dispatched on `{`, `[`, and IDENT
but had no case for a target beginning with `(`. Added an `LPAREN → parseType()` branch in
`parseTypeDeclBody` (`internal/parser/parser_type_decl.go`), so tuple and (parenthesized) function types
are accepted as alias targets, mirroring the existing list-alias (`[…]`) case. `(` was previously only an
error path, so nothing valid is shadowed (sum constructors like `Circle(int)` begin with an IDENT). These
targets already lower fine (`astTypeToInternalType` handles `TupleType`/`FuncType`), and — composing with
M-XMOD-ALIAS — they cross module boundaries too. Tests: parser AST-shape unit test + cross-module pipeline
fixture. Design doc: `design_docs/implemented/v0_28_1/m-parser-alias-targets.md`. (M-PARSER-ALIAS-TARGETS)

### Fixed — chained type aliases expand transitively (`type B = A`, `A = Json`) (M-XMOD-ALIAS-CHAIN)

An alias pointing at another alias (`type Ref = Id; type Id = int`) failed to unify with its ultimate
target (`cannot unify type constructors: int vs Id`) — `expandAlias` (`internal/types/unification_core.go`)
did a single lookup and returned `Id` without chasing it to `int`. This affected single-module and
cross-module code alike. Made `expandAlias` iterate to a fixpoint, with a `seen`-set cycle guard so a
self/mutual-referential alias (`type A = A`) stops gracefully instead of spinning the unifier. The record
`TypeName`-preservation path (M-CROSS-MODULE) is unchanged — a record is terminal. Tests: chained alias
(non-record + record-terminated), cyclic-termination, all prior alias tests still green. Design doc:
`design_docs/implemented/v0_28_1/m-xmod-alias-chain.md`. (M-XMOD-ALIAS-CHAIN)

### Fixed — cross-module transparent aliases to non-record types (`type Row = Json`) (M-XMOD-ALIAS)

A transparent alias whose target is **not a record** (`type Row = Json`, `type UserId = int`)
type-checked fine *within* a module but failed across module boundaries with
`cannot unify type constructors: Json vs Row`. Root cause: the interface builder
(`internal/iface/builder.go`) only registered `AddTypeAlias` for `*ast.RecordType` (and single-ctor
record ADTs) — the record half of the cross-module story fixed by M-TYPE-ALIAS (v0.9.11) and
M-TRANSITIVE-ALIAS-ENV-IMPORT (v0.22.0). Non-record aliases (`*ast.TypeAlias`) got no expansion in the
exported interface, so importers saw a nominal `Row` that wouldn't unify with `Json`. This bit real
published packages under toolchain skew: `sunholo/duckdb@0.1.0` (locked at v0.14.2) broke the `eparse`
CLI on v0.27.0. Fix: register non-record aliases too, mirroring the elaborator's existing `*ast.TypeAlias`
handling (`elaborate/file_funcs.go`) — a ~12-line addition. Sum types and nominal newtypes
(`type Gen = Gen(int)`) are `*ast.AlgebraicType`, a disjoint AST node, so they stay nominal (verified by
negative regression tests). **Digest-neutral** — `computeDigest` hashes Exports+Constructors, not
TypeAliases, so no dependent-package cascade rebuilds. Regression pack:
`internal/pipeline/cross_module_nonrecord_alias_test.go` (fail-before/pass-after + nominal-stays-nominal
guards) and `internal/iface/xmod_alias_digest_test.go`. Known out-of-scope follow-ups: chained aliases
(`type B = A`, `A = Json` — pre-existing `expandAlias` gap, fails single-module too) and tuple/function-type
aliases (parser-level). Design doc: `design_docs/planned/m-cross-module-type-alias.md`. (M-XMOD-ALIAS)

### Fixed — `\uXXXX` string escapes (v0.27.0 regression that bricked the motoko harness)

v0.27.0's escape rework (M-TERMINAL-IO) accepted only the braced `\u{H..H}` form and made
the classic 4-hex JS/JSON form **`\uXXXX` a hard parse error**. This silently broke existing
code: the motoko eval harness's `.ail` core (a `\u2014` em-dash) no longer parsed, so
`ailang run` died at module-load and **every motoko agent run produced 0 events for ~35 hours** —
masquerading as flaky infra (the 9h wedge, api_errors, stalled A/B experiments were all downstream
of this one break). The leaderboard's motoko scores went stale (frozen by `--skip-existing`). It
escaped CI because the motoko fork isn't in ailang's test suite. Fix: accept **both** `\u{H..H}`
and `\uXXXX`, matching JS/JSON/Rust (`internal/lexer/lexer_strings.go`). Tests cover both
forms + the exact regression; `ailang check` of the motoko core is green again. Design doc:
`design_docs/implemented/m-lexer-u4-compat.md`. Prevention: add a CI smoke-check of the motoko core
so a language breaking-change fails the release, not the rig 35h later. (M-LEXER-U4-COMPAT)

### Added — `ailang check` warns on toolchain skew (VER001) (docparse feedback)

A std/net breaking change (`httpRequest`'s `Err` moved from `string` to `NetError` in
v0.25.0) surfaced only as `cannot unify type constructors: string vs NetError` at every
`js(err)` site, with no hint the real cause was a *version skew* — a repo whose `ailang.lock`
was cut by an older toolchain, checked with a newer binary. `ailang check` now walks up to the
nearest `ailang.lock` and, when its `ailang_version` release core differs from the running
binary's, prints `WARNING VER001 (toolchain-skew)` naming both versions and the fix
(`ailang lock`, or install the matching toolchain). It's a hint only — never fails the check,
and fires only on a *decidable* release-core mismatch (a dev binary or a suffix-only difference
like `-N-ghash`/`-dirty` stays silent, so no false positives on local builds).
(`cmd/ailang/check_skew.go`, wired in `check.go`; `check_skew_test.go`.)

### Changed — std/net: migration note for `js(e)` → `js(show(e))` (docparse feedback)

Documented the `NetError` stringification path callers need after the v0.25.0 `Err` type
change: `show(e)` renders any `NetError` variant, so error-JSON code like
`encode(jo([kv("error", js(e))]))` becomes `js(show(e))`. No new stdlib helper is needed —
`show : α -> string` already covers it (verified end-to-end). Also fixed the `httpRequest`
doc-comment example, which still used `++` for string concat (list-only since v0.13.0) —
now uses `"${…}"` interpolation. (`std/net.ail`.)

## [v0.28.0] - 2026-07-01

### Fixed — deriving(Eq) non-determinism at runtime (Felix v0.27.0 feedback)

`deriving (Eq)` on user ADTs flakily failed at runtime — build-to-build, `deriving_eq.ail`
was 0/20 in one build and passing in another, erroring `missing dictionary method:
prelude::Eq::<T>::eq` (module-level `let`) or `comparison '==' on unsupported types:
TaggedValue` (inside a polymorphic lambda closure — reported by Felix, ticket `fb_cef305`).
Root cause: the derived-Eq marker was sometimes absent from the runtime dict registry when
the `Eq` dictionary was forced. Two surgical, determinism-restoring fixes that reuse the
existing structural-equality machinery (no duplicate): (1) `evalDictRef` synthesizes
structural Eq on a registry miss for an `Eq` method — the type checker already proved `Eq`
valid and derived Eq is *always* structural, so this removes the dependency on the flaky
registration; (2) the `==`/`!=` runtime fallback uses `valuesStructurallyEqual` instead of
erroring. Now **20/20 deterministic**; `deriving_eq` removed from the `verify-examples-toplevel`
run-skip list; regression test added. (`internal/eval/eval_patterns.go`, `eval_operations.go`.)

### Added — Name-collision hint on a failed function application (Felix v0.27.0 feedback)

`length("hello")` where `length` is imported from `std/list` gave an opaque `cannot unify
list[a] with string` with no hint that `std/string` also exports `length` (ticket `fb_942b7f`).
When an application fails to unify AND the callee is a bare name exported by more than one
stdlib module, the error now carries a note listing the other exporters and an alias example
(`import std/string (length as …)`). The note is attached to the application constraint's
`Path`, so it surfaces **only on failure** — correct use (`length([1,2,3])`) is never
annotated. Kept as a hint, not a rename. (`internal/types/import_hint.go`,
`typechecker_functions.go`.)

### Changed — IMP010 "not exported" errors suggest the closest export

Reaching for a symbol a module doesn't export (e.g. `flushStdout`, when `std/io` exports
`flush`) now yields `did you mean 'flush'?` instead of a bare dead-end. General across the
stdlib via a prefix relationship or small edit distance, and silent for genuinely unknown
symbols. This keeps ONE canonical name per operation (no duplicate aliases) while rescuing a
cold-start miss. (`internal/importhint`, `internal/stdlibindex`.)

## [v0.27.0] - 2026-06-30

### Added — Real-time terminal output: C-style string escapes + `flush()` (M-SNAKE-FEEDBACK)

From external 0→1 user feedback (a self-playing Snake built in AILANG): the two runtime
gaps that made live terminal rendering impossible without a `/dev/tty` hack are fixed.

- **String escapes `\xHH`, `\u{H..H}`, `\e` (ESC), `\0` (NUL)** now work in string and char
  literals — `println("\x1b[92mok\x1b[0m")` emits real ANSI without importing `std/bytes`.
  Hex/unicode escapes decode to Unicode code points (strings stay valid UTF-8). **Malformed
  escapes are now a positioned lexer error instead of a silent passthrough** (No Silent
  Fallbacks) — `\x1`, `\u{}`, `\033` (octal unsupported), `\q` all report a clear message
  via a new `PAR_ILLEGAL_TOKEN` diagnostic. Escape decoding is shared between string and char
  literals (`readEscape` in `internal/lexer/lexer.go`).
- **`flush()` in `std/io`** plus `printErr`/`eprintln` (unbuffered stderr). On a TTY, stdout
  is now **line-buffered** (flushed on each newline) instead of a 64KB full-buffer, so
  per-line/per-frame renders appear in real time; `flush()` handles partial-line `\r` output
  (progress bars, game frames). Piped stdout stays unbuffered (preserves the M-PERF6B
  line-reader contract). New `examples/progress_bar.ail` renders under `--caps IO,Clock` —
  no `FS`, no `/dev/tty`.

### Added — `nth_or` / `head_or` / `last_or` in `std/list`

Total, default-returning versions of `nth`/`head`/`last` that remove the `Some`/`None`
unwrap boilerplate at every indexing call site while staying crash-free.
`nth_or([10], 5, 0) == 0`. New gated example `examples/runnable/list_safe_indexing.ail`.

### Fixed — Rotted examples + a real CI gap (M-SNAKE-FEEDBACK)

- Migrated **8 examples** that used `++` on strings (list-only since v0.13.0, so they never
  ran) to `${…}` interpolation: `bounded_xml_fold`, `datetime_demo`, `deriving_eq`,
  `effect_budget_demo`, `first_non_repeat`, `short_circuit_and`, `short_circuit_or`,
  `string_replace`. Fixed 2 more (`hello_coordinator`, `coordinator_e2e_test`) missing
  `import std/io`.
- **Root cause:** CI's `verify-examples` only checks `examples/runnable/`, never the
  top-level `examples/*.ail` dir — so these rotted undetected. Added `make
