  verify-examples-toplevel` (`tools/verify_examples.sh`): every top-level example must
  type-check, runnable ones must run, AI/network + known-bug examples are skipped **with a
  logged reason**. Wired into `make ci` and `make ci-strict`.
- Two pre-existing runtime bugs surfaced by the new gate and logged for follow-up (not
  silently patched): `deriving_eq` (auto-derived `Eq` dictionary missing at runtime,
  M-DX19) and `effect_budget_demo` (`IO @limit` is counted cumulatively across the call
  chain rather than per-function).

### Changed — Docs truth + clearer `.ail` extension error

- Clearer non-`.ail` warning: now names the file and the fix (`got "foo.ailang" … rename to
  foo.ail — module resolution requires .ail`).
- New docs: capability-per-stdlib-module table ([effects](docs/docs/reference/effects.md)),
  module-level `let` bindings ([language-syntax](docs/docs/reference/language-syntax.md),
  supported since v0.5.0), and precise stdin status — `readLine()` works (blocking, line);
  raw/non-blocking keyboard input does not (#231) ([limitations](docs/docs/reference/limitations.md)).

### Fixed — LSP position fidelity for string interpolation

The synthesized `show` call that `"${expr}"` desugars to was given the inner expression's
exact position, so an interpolated identifier (e.g. `basicReplace` in `${basicReplace()}`)
collided with the phantom `show` at the same line:col — breaking cursor→symbol resolution and
the LSP position-fidelity probe. The synthesized `show` now carries the `${` marker's position
instead. (`internal/parser/parser_literals.go`.) Also split `internal/lexer/lexer.go` (string /
char / escape lexing → `lexer_strings.go`) to stay under the 800-line gate.

## [v0.26.2] - 2026-06-30

### Fixed — Public MCP prod sync (`mcp.ailang.sunholo.com`) was stuck at 0.25.0

The gated release pipeline (`ailang-core-release` → `cloudbuild-release.yaml`: ci-gate → build → deploy test → smoke gate → promote prod) **fired** for v0.26.0 and v0.26.1 but **failed at the very first step, `ci-gate`**, which polls the tagged commit's dev `CI` (`test` job) and requires it green. Dev `CI` was red on `make check-file-sizes` at both tags, so ci-gate aborted the release (prod untouched) — leaving the public MCP one-to-two versions behind (agents got `unknown_version` for 0.26.x). With the file-size gate fixed (below) dev `CI` is green again, so this release's ci-gate passes and prod promotes — the first validated happy-path roll since v0.25.0. No code change for the fix itself; this is a clean release on green CI to re-trigger the pipeline. (The trigger lives in `ailang-multivac-deploy` / region **europe-west3** — querying the default global region misleadingly shows no builds.)

### Changed — Split 3 files back under the 800-line CI gate (dev CI was red)

`make check-file-sizes` is a **hard** gate in `ci.yml` (runs on every push/PR to `main`/`dev`), and dev had been red on it for several pushes — `internal/loader/loader.go` (805), `cmd/ailang/eval_suite.go` (816), and `cmd/ailang/check.go` (846) had crept over 800, which also failed CI on every open Dependabot PR (and, per the fix above, blocked the MCP prod promotion via ci-gate). Split each by moving a cohesive group to a sibling file in the same package (no behavior change, no API change): loader error/diagnostic helpers (`newIMP010Loader`/`newLDR001`/`suggestSimilar`/`min`) → `loader_errors.go`; the check JSON/agent output helpers (types + `outputCheckJSON`/`outputCheckAgent`/`distillAgentLine`/`errorToCheckJSONError`) → `check_output.go`; and the eval-suite model-list resolution + agent-only safety guard → `resolveEvalModelList` in `eval_suite_models.go`. All three now 670/726/768 lines; the file-size gate passes, dev CI goes green, and the Dependabot PRs are unblocked.

## [v0.26.1] - 2026-06-29

### Added — linux/arm64 (aarch64) release binary

The release pipeline had **never** built a Linux ARM64 binary — the build matrix in `.github/workflows/release.yml` only produced `linux/amd64`, `darwin/amd64`, `darwin/arm64`, and `windows/amd64`. But the official installer (`docs/static/install.sh`) already maps `aarch64|arm64 → arm64` and constructs the URL `linux.arm64.ailang.tar.gz`, so every aarch64 Linux user (AWS Graviton, ARM VMs, Raspberry Pi, Apple-Silicon Linux guests) hit a `404` on install — the installer promised an asset the release never produced. Added a `linux/arm64` matrix entry (cross-compiled from `ubuntu-latest`, pure Go / no cgo → statically-linked ELF, version-stamped and cosign-signed identically to every other artifact). The changelog download block now lists both Linux architectures. v0.26.0 could not be backfilled because GitHub immutable releases cryptographically lock a published release's assets (HTTP 422 on upload); this is the first release to ship the binary. Reported by an external tester on aarch64 Linux.

### Fixed — A hung motoko could silently wedge the whole eval rig for hours (M-MOTOKO-RIG-WEDGE-FIX)

The motoko executor's main run (`internal/executor/motoko/motoko.go`) called `cmd.Run()` on the *incoming* ctx, which carries no per-benchmark deadline — unlike claude/codex/opencode/pi, motoko had **no timeout enforcement on its main run** (only the 5s `--version` probe). So a hung motoko subprocess blocked `cmd.Run()` forever, which also hung the eval-suite goroutine waiting on it. Because motoko's env-server binds the **fixed `ENV_PORT=8080`**, the hung run's env-server then squatted 8080, and every *later* motoko spawn — the rotation's own remaining trials and any concurrent eval — crashed with the cryptic "terminated without emitting run_summary" (it couldn't bind its env-server → 0-event JSONL). Observed 2026-06-29: one hung rotation trial wedged the rig for **10 hours, 0 results banked**, and crashed an unrelated gate A/B 5/5; ollama and the model were healthy the whole time. Fix (AILANG-side defense in depth; the proper upstream fix is an ephemeral env-server port): (1) **bound the run** with `timeoutSeconds` + a hard 3h fallback ceiling so `cmd.Run()` can never block forever; (2) **`Setpgid` + a group-kill `Cancel`** (new `procgroup_{unix,windows}.go`, mirroring `eval_harness/process_*.go`) so the timeout reaps the env-server child instead of orphaning it on 8080; (3) **pre-flight `clearStalePort8080()`** kills an orphaned `bun src/tui` still holding 8080 before spawning (at `--parallel 1` any holder is a leftover), turning a silent 5/5 cascade into self-healing. New `procgroup_unix_test.go` proves ctx-timeout → group kill → child reaped + bounded run.

### Fixed — Eval harness graded an isolated re-stubbed file, not the model's workspace (M-EVAL-RELIABLE-GRADING)

Agent-mode grading re-seeded `input_files` into a *fresh* workspace — re-stubbing the very module the agent was told to implement — and ran `benchmark/solution.ail` in isolation. So a correct multi-file solution could only pass by **ignoring the task** and inlining everything into `solution.ail`; the score measured "did the model guess the solution.ail convention," not "did it implement the task." Proven by `docx_reimplement`: a local model implemented `docx_parser.ail`, verified `ailang run --entry main main.ail` produced **byte-for-byte golden output**, and still scored FAIL ("entrypoint 'main' not found" on the discarded `solution.ail`); `markdown_reimplement` passed only by luck (it happened to inline). Fix (opt-in, additive): `BenchmarkSpec.GradeEntrypoint` + `SolutionFiles` — `validateSolution` now grades the agent's **preserved** workspace by re-seeding every input_file *except* `solution_files` (restoring the harness-owned probe + fixed deps, keeping the agent's implementation) and running the probe against it; failures are attributable to the model (compile error or wrong output), never a missing-entrypoint artifact. Benchmarks without `grade_entrypoint` (the 74 single-file suite) take the unchanged legacy path. End-to-end: `docx_reimplement` + `markdown_reimplement` now grade **2/2 PASS** on motoko-local (docx was a measurement-artifact FAIL). New `grade_workspace_test.go` (correct impl PASS / wrong impl FAIL) + benchmark self-check. See [m-eval-reliable-grading](../design_docs/planned/m-eval-reliable-grading.md).

### Added — Multi-file reimplement benchmarks + executor `agent_env` (M-EVAL-REIMPLEMENT-BENCH)

`markdown_reimplement` and `docx_reimplement`: multi-file benchmarks where the agent reimplements a stubbed parser module against seeded dependencies, exercising `MOTOKO_AST_AUTOREAD` (compact dependency interfaces — the M-IFACE-COMPACT-ADT-FIELDS path) on a long, real task. Required `BenchmarkSpec.AgentEnv` (per-benchmark env exported to the agent subprocess, `${WORKSPACE}` expansion) routed through the **executor** path (`executor.Task.ExtraEnv` → `BuildEnvironment`, applying to every executor uniformly). The original injection sat in the dead legacy `RunHeadlessSessionStreaming` path, so `MOTOKO_AST_AUTOREAD` silently never reached executor agents (motoko/opencode/pi) and AST-autoread stayed off — diagnosed via a file-probe (the eval harness swallows agent stderr) and fixed; validated end-to-end (`proc.MOTOKO_AST_AUTOREAD="1"`, dependency reads return the 2138-char compact iface vs 6500-char full source).

## [v0.26.0] - 2026-06-26

### Added — Hex, binary, and octal integer literals

AILANG integer literals now accept radix prefixes — `0xFF`/`0XFF` (hexadecimal), `0b1010`/`0B1010` (binary), `0o17`/`0O17` (octal) — in addition to decimal. The lexer (`readNumber`) recognizes the prefix and reads the radix digits as a single `INT` token; the parser converts via Go's base-0 auto-detection, with **decimal kept on base 10** so a leading-zero decimal (e.g. `0123`) stays `123` and is never silently reinterpreted as octal. Surfaced as a root cause by the motoko docx harness: a local model reimplementing a DOCX parser needed Unicode private-use codepoints (`0xE000`–`0xF8FF`) and wrote `0xE000` (valid in nearly every other language), which previously failed with `PAR020` — the model then fell into a `sed`/`python`-via-BashExec edit loop trying to convert hex to decimal until the step budget exhausted. Supporting what models naturally write removes the wall. New `examples/integer_literals.ail`, lexer test (`TestHexBinOctalLiterals`), and parser test (`TestParseIntLiteralValue`, incl. the leading-zero-decimal regression).

### Fixed — `iface --compact` now carries ADT constructor fields (M-IFACE-COMPACT-ADT-FIELDS)

`ailang iface --compact` — the token-efficient interface motoko's `MOTOKO_AST_AUTOREAD` serves when an agent reads a *dependency* `.ail` module — rendered sum-type constructors as bare names (`type Block = HeadingBlock | TextBlock`) and leaked the Go-internal `<*types.TRecord>` for record return types. An agent implementing a parser whose job is to *construct* ADT variants couldn't learn the constructor shapes from the interface and fell back to `cat`-ing the full source (observed: 11 cat-fallbacks in a docx eval run), defeating the compaction. Now: the iface `ConstructorInfo` carries `FieldTypes` (threaded from the elaborator through `convertToIfaceConstructors`); `formatTypeCanonical` renders records structurally (`{label: type, ...}`, cycle-safe via a depth guard) or by nominal name; and the normalized-JSON constructor entries are full signatures — so the compact interface shows `HeadingBlock({level: int, text: string})` and `simpleCell : (string)->TableCell` instead of bare names and `<*types.TRecord>`. Interface digests for ADT/record-bearing modules change accordingly (constructor field types are part of the public interface). New tests in `internal/iface/compact_adt_fields_test.go`. See [m-iface-compact-adt-fields](../design_docs/planned/m-iface-compact-adt-fields.md).

### Fixed — duplicate approval notifications under Pub/Sub at-least-once (M-SECRET-EFFECT)

The coordinator's `/pubsub/push` approval bridge forwarded *every* delivery to ntfy, so Pub/Sub's at-least-once redelivery (push subscriptions can't use exactly-once) produced duplicate phone notifications for the same approval. The bridge now suppresses duplicates by Pub/Sub message ID (atomic mark-before-forward, un-mark on send failure so genuine retries still push; 1-hour TTL, lazy prune). The request and its confirmation are distinct publishes (distinct IDs) and are unaffected.

### Changed (breaking) — `secret(ref, purpose)`: approval requests now carry a reason (M-SECRET-EFFECT)

`secret()` now takes a required second argument, a human-readable **purpose**, surfaced in the phone approval request so the operator knows *why* a secret is being requested — `secret("op://Prod/stripe/api-key", "charge order #1234")`. Previously `secret(ref)` defaulted the purpose to the reference itself, so the notification showed the `op://` ref twice with no context. Breaking: the stdlib signature is now `secret(ref: string, purpose: string) -> string<secret> ! {Secret}` (and the `_secret_read` builtin is 2-arg); existing single-arg calls must add a purpose. The remote-approval notification was also reworked to a clear multi-line body (requested-by / secret / purpose / task / decision-window). Examples, the builtin-types golden, and tests updated. See [docs/guides/secret-approvals.md](../docs/docs/guides/secret-approvals.md).

### Security — Static IFC enforcement: `<secret>` leaks are now compile errors (M-SECRET-EFFECT M5/M6)

M-TAINT-TYPES (v0.16.0) shipped the information-flow label lattice and parsing but never enforced it — `CheckSinkRefinement`/`CheckDeclassify` had **zero callers**, so a `<secret>` value could reach a `{not secret}` sink with no error (verified empirically: a deliberate leak passed `ailang check` clean). M5 closes that gap with `CheckModuleIFC` (`internal/types/ifc_check.go`), a self-contained post-typecheck analysis over the surface AST that enforces two rules over the label lattice: **(A) sink refinement** — a value labelled `<ℓ>` may not reach a `T{not ℓ}` parameter; **(B) declassification** — a function may not hide body taint behind a cleaner explicit return label without `! {Declassify}`. Sources are explicit `<ℓ>` annotations plus the `secret()` builtin; unannotated functions are label-transparent (join of arg labels), so ordinary code needs no annotations. The pass emits standard `TypeCheckError`s (`SinkRefinementError`, `DeclassifyRequiredError`) and **never injects `TLabelled` types into CoreTI/codegen** — labels stay confined to the type layer, so there is zero runtime cost and no regression surface in the HM core or backend. `secret()`'s stdlib signature now honestly declares `-> string<secret>`. Scope: intraprocedural sink enforcement with source-label propagation within a module (cross-module sink flow remains Z3-contract-backed, deferred to M-TAINT-TYPES Phase 2); `inbox_injection_v2.ail` is unchanged (no `{not ℓ}` typed sinks there). New worked examples `examples/runnable/secrets/{gated_secret,leak_attempt}.ail` (the safe declassify pattern vs. the rejected leak), 14 unit tests (deterministic at `-count=20`), guide updated ([ifc-labels.mdx](../docs/docs/guides/ifc-labels.mdx)). Deferred from M6: the `approved(ref)` Z3 contract predicate (approval is inherently a runtime property; tracked as Phase 2). See [design_docs/planned/v0_26_0/m-secret-effect-remote-approval.md](../design_docs/planned/v0_26_0/m-secret-effect-remote-approval.md).

### Docs — Website Gemini CLI retirement / Managed Agents (Antigravity) sync (M-WEBSITE-ANTIGRAVITY-DOCS-SYNC)

The **code** retired the Gemini CLI executor in v0.22.0 (M-MANAGED-AGENTS) in favour of the Vertex **Managed Agents API** (the Antigravity `antigravity-preview` agent), but ~18 website files still presented "Gemini CLI" as a live coding harness — and one of them, the Getting Started bootstrap, was an actively-broken `gemini extensions install …` instruction after Google shut off Gemini CLI on **2026-06-18**. This docs-only sweep replaces the stale references with the correct successor *per context*: user-install pages (getting-started, microrag, homepage quickstart) now point at Claude Code / Codex; eval/benchmark/coordinator/telemetry pages point at the hosted Managed Agents API (`managed_agents.execute` span, ADC auth, post-hoc cost). Benchmark-dashboard harness labels are fixed **additively** — historical `gemini` rows keep a "Gemini CLI (retired)" label rather than being relabelled "Managed Agents" (those runs really were Gemini CLI), and a `managed_agents` label is added for new data. Deliberately untouched: the unrelated `gemini_live`/`gemini_files` stdlib packages and the design-doc history index. No Go or `models.yml` changes. See [design_docs/planned/v0_25_1/m-website-antigravity-docs-sync.md](../design_docs/planned/v0_25_1/m-website-antigravity-docs-sync.md).

### Changed — Eval grading: boolean-case + numeric-format parity (M-EVAL-OUTPUT-NORMALIZE)

Sibling of M-EVAL-JSON-COMPARE. The v0.25.0 baseline revealed that **32 of 83 Python stdout failures (39%) across all 11 models** were pure `True`/`False` vs expected `true`/`false` casing — Python emits native booleans, benchmarks expect AILANG/JSON-style lowercase — plus a tail of numeric formatting (`7.50` vs `7.5`, `16.0` vs `16`). `CompareOutput` gained a third, **safe-by-construction** branch (after the exact and JSON paths): a **per-line** normalized comparison that case-folds standalone booleans, canonicalizes decimal trailing-zeros/integer-float, and removes whitespace adjacent to structural punctuation (`[1, 2, 3]`==`[1,2,3]`). It can only turn a *fail into a pass* when the normalized forms are exactly equal — a different line count (verbose/extra output) or a wrong value/structure (a Python set `{1,2}` is **not** equated to a list `[1,2]`) still fails, so genuine misses keep failing. Verified by `TestCompareOutput_Normalization` (positives + safety negatives). New `tools/eval-regrade` re-applies the grader to a stored baseline's `stdout`/`expected_stdout` with **monotonic** semantics (recovers false-negatives, never removes a stored pass) — re-grading the v0.25.0 baseline recovered **44** results, moving field Python **85.5%→92.1%** (AILANG ~93%, essentially even), with zero genuine downgrades. The re-grade also surfaced **1** pre-existing runtime-grader leniency (a verbose repair output stored as a pass) flagged for separate investigation. See [design_docs/planned/m-eval-output-normalization.md](../design_docs/planned/m-eval-output-normalization.md).

### Added — ELO ratings: persistence, mode-separation, selective rerun + saturation report (M-EVAL-RATING-EFFICIENCY)

The eval rig now derives benchmark *difficulty* and model *capability* from ELO ratings instead of hand-assigned tiers, and spends compute where it buys information. **ELO engine** (`internal/eval_harness/ratings.go`): `FitFromTrials` fits converged, deterministic ratings over a baseline (a PASS = the model beating the benchmark). **Persistence** (`internal/observatory` v16 migration): `benchmark_ratings`/`model_ratings`/`trial_history` tables, **mode-separated** (`mode` in the primary key) because standard and agent are different difficulty regimes — agent saturates, so a combined fit falsely ranks agent harnesses top. **`eval-suite --benchmarks-by-confidence <db>`** picks the most informative, non-saturated benchmarks for the run mode (drops the Trivial band, ranks the rest by proximity to the field's median rating) — a "did this morning's fix help?" check becomes a handful of benchmarks, not a full rotation. **`eval-trend tier-saturation`** renders a per-mode saturation report with demotion candidates + recommendation. On the regraded v0.25.0 baseline: standard model ELO has **claude-fable-5 #1 (2154)**; standard is 24% saturated (still discriminating) while **agent is 68% saturated** (→ add harder benchmarks, M-EVAL-FRONTIER-TIER). `tools/eval-elo --persist` seeds the DB. Emitting these into `latest.json` for the website is the dashboard-redesign sprint (M-EVAL-DASHBOARD-REDESIGN). See [design_docs/planned/v0_24_0/m-eval-rating-efficiency.md](../design_docs/planned/v0_24_0/m-eval-rating-efficiency.md).

### Added — Dashboard: ELO ratings & difficulty page; v0.25.0 (regraded) published (M-EVAL-DASHBOARD-REDESIGN)

The benchmark site gains an **ELO Ratings & Difficulty** page (`docs/benchmarks/elo`): a per-mode (standard/agent) model-capability leaderboard and a difficulty-banded benchmark list — saturated (Trivial) rows dimmed as demotion candidates, grader-artifact benchmarks (`contract_sorted_merge`, `decision_block_capture`) badged "⚠ artifact" so they aren't read as genuine difficulty. Fed by the new `ratings`/`grading` blocks `eval-report` emits into `latest.json` (phase 1). The published dashboard moves to **v0.25.0** with **regraded** AILANG+Python numbers (cloud eval is AILANG+Python by design — multi-language moves to the OSS on-device rig). Standard leaderboard: claude-fable-5 #1; agent: claude-sonnet-4-6 #1. React `EloLeaderboard` component reads `latest.json` (degrades gracefully when ratings absent). See [design_docs/planned/m-eval-dashboard-redesign.md](../design_docs/planned/m-eval-dashboard-redesign.md).

## [v0.25.0] - 2026-06-12

### Security — Go 1.25.10 → 1.25.11: clear stdlib vulnerability gate (GO-2026-5037, GO-2026-5039)

Bumped the toolchain in `go.mod` and all CI/release workflows (`ci.yml`, `build.yml`, `release.yml`, `docusaurus-deploy.yml`, `eval-weekly.yml`, `test-game-codegen.yml`) from Go 1.25.10 to **1.25.11** to clear two standard-library advisories (**GO-2026-5037, GO-2026-5039**) flagged by the `govulncheck` gate. The released v0.25.0 binaries are built on the patched toolchain.

### Changed — Eval grading: JSON-aware output comparison (M-EVAL-JSON-COMPARE)

`CompareOutput` did an exact string match (after trimming outer whitespace), so **correct JSON output failed on formatting alone**: AILANG's `std/json` encode emits compact `{"a":1}` while benchmarks (and Python's `json.dumps` default) expect spaced `{"a": 1}`. The v0.24.1 analysis found **9 of 10** whitespace-only AILANG "logic_error" failures were correct JSON failing byte-exact match — including all of `ast_patch_roundtrip`, the #1 AILANG-vs-Python gap, which *looked* "genuinely hard" at 38% but was a **grader artifact**. Fix: if **both** expected and actual parse as valid JSON, compare their canonical parsed forms (`reflect.DeepEqual` over `json.Unmarshal`), which also normalizes int-vs-float and key order. **Safe by construction** — it only triggers when both sides are valid JSON, so non-JSON near-misses (`"1 2"` vs `"12"`) and formatted-text benchmarks are unaffected; exact match stays the fast path and genuinely-wrong JSON still fails. Verified against real v0.24.1 data (`ast_patch_roundtrip` 38%→~95%); 13 `CompareOutput` unit tests including 2 safety cases.

### Changed — Nightly eval: build + run from an isolated worktree pinned to committed dev (M-EVAL-NIGHTLY-REPRO)

The nightly (`tools/launchd/nightly-eval.sh`) now builds and runs from a **throwaway git worktree pinned to the committed `dev` HEAD** rather than the live working tree, so an in-progress local edit can never leak into — or be corrupted by — an overnight run. The run is now reproducible from a single commit SHA recorded in the log.

### Changed — Nightly regression gate: alert only on solid→broken transitions (M-EVAL-LOCAL-OLLAMA)

The nightly's regression gate fired a Discord alert whenever a benchmark with *any* historical pass ("has a baseline") failed both trials — so flaky benchmarks (which pass ~50–70% of the time) tripped it by chance every few nights: **5 false alerts on 2026-06-12** (cli_args, contract_roman_numeral, graph_bfs, pipeline, state_machine_vending). The gate now fires only on a genuine **solid→broken** transition: a benchmark that passed **all** its trials in the immediately previous nightly run but fails every trial tonight. Everything else (flaky, already-failing, never-passed) is filed quietly to controlplane — no Discord. Replaying 2026-06-12 against the new gate: **5 alerts → 1** (`pipeline`, a real pass-both→fail-both break; the other 4 were flaky and correctly suppressed). Missing prior run fails **safe to no-alert** (better to miss one than cry wolf). The classifier now compares against the previous run's result files (date-sorted glob) instead of querying `eval_baselines`, so the observatory-DB lookup is dropped from the gate.

### Changed — Nightly eval: thrash-cap to bound runaway agent loops (M-EVAL-OS-LONGITUDINAL)

The nightly had no per-benchmark token ceiling, so a single thrashing trial could burn **6.7M–8.3M tokens** (graph_bfs) and add ~an hour to the run — the "useless thrashing" that bloated 06-11/06-12 to ~6h. Added `--max-tokens-per-bench 4000000` to the nightly's `run_eval`. Because `eval_baselines` is now populated (the v15-migration fix), the eval-suite applies an **adaptive `mean + 2σ`** token threshold per (model, benchmark) once ≥5 passing samples exist, with the 4M value as the fixed ceiling otherwise. 4M sits **above the heaviest legitimate passing trial observed (~3.66M)**, so a slow-but-correct run is never converted into a false thrash-abort, while runaways are bounded. A 1M cap was **A/B-validated 2026-06-11** — it bounded an 8.3M-token runaway and cut `config_file_parser`'s thrash from 5/5 to 2/5 capped trials.

### Added — PAR020: actionable "missing `;` between block statements" error (M-AILANG-ERROR-QUALITY)

The #1 *unactionable* small-model failure in agent mode is the **mirror of PAR017**: a *missing* `;` between block statements. A model writes a `{ }` block body and drops the separator —
```
pure func f() -> int {
  let n = length(s)        // ← no ';'
  if n > 0 then 1 else 0
}
```
— and the parser emitted a bare `PAR_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN: expected }, got if` with **zero recovery signal**; `config_file_parser` burned **66 agent turns** on exactly this. The parser now emits **`PAR020`** — "missing ';' between block statements (found \`X\` where \`;\` or \`}\` was expected)" with the concrete two-line fix and a docs link — when a block body (function-declaration path, `parser_func.go`) or block expression (`parser_expr.go`) is followed by a statement-starting token (`let`/`letrec`/`if`/`match`/identifier) instead of `;`/`}`. PAR017 (extra `;` in `=`-body) + PAR020 (missing `;` in block) now bookend the whole `;`-confusion family — **~32% of local-qwen agent failures**. Guarded by `TestPAR020_MissingBlockSemicolon` (fires on the pattern; no false-positive on valid or single-expression blocks); parser/elaborate/pipeline suites green; `make verify-examples` at baseline. See [design_docs/planned/v0_24_0/m-ailang-error-quality-for-llm-iteration.md](../design_docs/planned/v0_24_0/m-ailang-error-quality-for-llm-iteration.md).

### Changed — Dialect-traps card: sharpen the #1 small-model failure (M-AILANG-ERROR-QUALITY)

A frequency analysis of **334 local-qwen agent trials (44 failures)** found that **~36% of failures are a single family** — expression-body (`= expr`) vs block-body (`{ stmts }`) / statement-separator confusion — **dominated (20.5%) by the `func f() = let x = e; rest` reflex** (`PAR017`: `;` not valid in expression-body functions). The dialect-traps card (`prompts/agent/dialect-traps.md`, M-EVAL-PROMPT-DELIVERY) trap #2 was sharpened to name that exact anti-pattern and give both fixes — brace block (`func f() { let x = e; rest }`) or let-in (`func f() = let x = e in rest`); verified the anti-pattern rejects and both fixes run. Notably `match … with` (`PAR019`) and `++`-for-string-concat — the old *big-model* top failures — are now rare/zero on qwen, confirming the card already works for those; the small-model frequency banners on the sibling `m-prompt-*` docs undercount what's still live for the models we run continuously. Data recorded in [design_docs/planned/v0_24_0/m-ailang-error-quality-for-llm-iteration.md](../design_docs/planned/v0_24_0/m-ailang-error-quality-for-llm-iteration.md), which re-prioritizes it: the parser/card already cover `PAR017`, yet the model violates it and can't recover (`config_file_parser` thrashed **66 turns**) — the remaining lever is making `PAR017` recovery-actionable (suggest the two rewrites inline).

### Changed — Aver peer-language eval: authoritative prompt + richer failure diagnostics (community: #240, #241)

Two improvements to how the eval harness treats the Aver peer language, both reported by Aver's author ([@jasisz](https://github.com/jasisz), [#240](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/issues/240) / [#241](https://github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/issues/241)):

- **`prompts/aver.md` is now the upstream curated guide.** The previous teaching prompt was hand-rolled from `docs/language.md` + `docs/pushback.md` + `examples/core/` and had drifted — it taught integer `/` and treated `Int.mod` as returning `Int`, when Aver makes `/` Float-only and `Int.div`/`Int.mod` return `Result<Int, String>`; it also missed `verify fn trace`/`given` stubs, independent products `(a, b)!`/`(a, b)?!`, the `Tuple<A, B>` type spelling, and `Bool.and`/`Bool.or`/`Bool.not`. Replaced the body with `averlang.dev/llms.txt` (Aver 0.21.x) verbatim, keeping a small benchmark-harness header (single-file `solution.av`, `aver run` is the grader, `Console.print` takes `String`). Synced to the embedded copy at `cmd/ailang/prompts/aver.md`.
- **Compile failures now show `aver check`'s structured diagnostics.** On a compile-class failure, `AverRunner` re-runs the solution through `aver check` and surfaces its named error categories (`error[type-error]`), `repair:` hints, and source-line excerpts as retry feedback — far more useful for the LLM loop than `aver run`'s terse one-line stderr. Crucially, `aver run` stays authoritative for pass/fail: `aver check` additionally requires a `module` declaration that `run` does not, so gating the verdict on it (as the original suggestion proposed) would have **regressed** runnable-but-module-less solutions — we enrich the message only, never change the verdict. Guarded by `TestAverRunner_Success` (module-less program still passes) and `TestAverRunner_CompileErrorEnrichedWithCheck`.

### Fixed — `letrec` recursion broken in block (statement) form (M-LETREC-BLOCK-SCOPE)

A statement-form `letrec` inside a block — `{ letrec f = \n. … f(n-1) …; <rest> }`, **the exact form the teaching prompt documents** — failed type-checking with `undefined variable: f`. The recursive name was never in scope, in its own value or in the rest of the block, so block-form `letrec` was effectively unusable (and the prompt taught a non-working pattern). Root cause: the elaborator's `normalizeBlock` special-cased `*ast.Let` statements (threading the binding into the continuation) but **had no `*ast.LetRec` case**, so a block-level `letrec f = …;` was bound to a discarded `_block_N` wildcard and its name dropped. The expression form `letrec f = … in body` always worked (it carries its own body, and `inferLetRec` already scopes the binding correctly). Fixed by adding the missing `*ast.LetRec` case to `normalizeBlock`, emitting a `core.LetRec` whose body is the threaded continuation — closing single-arg, curried, multi-param, and letrec-after-another-statement forms. Found via the first local-qwen core eval baseline (it sank `config_file_parser`). Guarded by `TestLetRecBlockScope` (passes with fix, fails without) and a rewritten `examples/runnable/letrec_recursion.ail` that now covers **both** forms — the prior example used only the always-working expression form, which is why CI never caught it. Top-level `letrec` (module-scope) remains a separate, known limitation (use a top-level recursive `func`). See [design_docs/implemented/v0_24_0/m-letrec-block-scope.md](../design_docs/implemented/v0_24_0/m-letrec-block-scope.md).

### Fixed — Observatory: `eval_baselines` table missing on all existing DBs (M-EVAL-OS-LONGITUDINAL)

The adaptive token-budget baseline table (`eval_baselines`, v0.23.0) was added only to the base `Migrate()` schema, which `MigrateWithVersion` runs exactly once (`currentVersion < 1`). Every `observatory.db` already past v1 — i.e. every existing DB, now at schema v14 — never received the table, so the nightly eval-suite logged `warning: failed to update eval baseline … no such table: eval_baselines` on every passing trial and silently recorded **zero** baselines (the adaptive thrash-abort thresholds never engaged; the eval fell back to the fixed `--max-tokens-per-bench` flag forever). Fixed by adding a **versioned v15 migration** that backfills the table on existing DBs — mirroring how `trace_summaries` got a v12 migration alongside its base-schema entry — and adding `eval_baselines` to `ValidateSchema` so future drift is caught. The live DB self-heals on the next store open (`OpenStore` → `MigrateWithVersion`). Regression test `TestMigrateWithVersion_V15BackfillsEvalBaselines` reconstructs the exact v14-without-table state and asserts the backfill plus a successful `UpdatePassedTrial`.

### Changed — Nightly eval: smoke→smoke+core with baseline-gated regression alerts (M-EVAL-LOCAL-OLLAMA)

The local-qwen nightly (`tools/launchd/nightly-eval.sh`) now runs **smoke + core** (50 benchmarks, up from smoke's 23) — the regression guard plus the capability frontier. Because core is harder and the local model fails benchmarks it has never passed, the persistent-failure alert is now **gated on the `eval_baselines` table** (the one the v15 migration just repaired): a benchmark with a passing baseline that fails every trial = genuine **regression** → one Discord ping; a never-passed benchmark = known **gap** → filed quietly to controlplane for the gap-finder, no Discord. Unknown baseline state (DB unreadable) fails safe to "regression" so a real one is never silently downgraded. Also: agent mode requires an explicit `--benchmarks` list, so the set is derived as smoke ∪ explicit-core ∪ default-core (the `events`-style no-`tier:` benchmarks a `grep 'tier: core'` would miss); fixed a latent `$(( … 2>/dev/null ))` parse bug in the Monday A/B Delta line; and the post-release skill's stale tier counts were synced to 23/26/11/9. The benchmark-set derivation requires an `id:` field so the dashboard meta-file `benchmarks/events.yml` (a suite-change log, not a benchmark) is excluded — it had slipped into the first smoke+core run as a phantom 0-result "gap"; `discoverBenchmarks`/`LoadSpec` already reject it on the Go side, the gap was only in this script's bash derivation. Real core tier is **26** benchmarks (there are no default-core benchmarks; `events.yml` is not one), so smoke+core = 49.

### Fixed — Type-soundness: class constraints survive instantiation + `let` annotations enforced (M-TYPE-LIST-SOUND)

Closed a type-soundness hole that let invalid programs type-check and fail at runtime — a recurring problem that three prior sprints (DX-17, numeric_coercion, M-TYPECHECK-NO-AUTO-UNWRAP-RESULT) hit and deferred. Root cause: **`Scheme.Instantiate` silently dropped the scheme's class constraints.** A let-bound numeric list `[42]` generalizes to `∀a. Num a ⇒ [a]`, but instantiation re-created only the type `[a']`, never re-emitting `Num a'` — so unifying `[a'] ~ [string]` accepted `needStrs([42])`, which then crashed at runtime in `_str_join` (`expected string, got tagged value/int`).

- **`Scheme.InstantiateWithConstraints`** re-emits the freshened class constraints as fresh wanted constraints at each use site (`inferVar`), so the existing ground-constraint resolver rejects `Num[string]` at compile time — the same path the scalar `needStr(42)` already used. Plain `Instantiate` delegates to it (unchanged for all other callers).
- **Bonus fix:** `let x: T = e` type annotations were dropped entirely during elaboration — `let x: int = "hello"` compiled. Annotations are now recorded (`normalizeLet`/`normalizeBlock` → `letTypeAnnots`) and enforced (`inferLet` emits `valueType ~ annot` before solving).

Errors are AILANG-level and span-anchored (`No instance for Num[string]`, `cannot unify string vs int`), never Go-internal or runtime leaks.

**Round 2 — constructor-pattern binding (closes the `json_parse` shape + a broader bug class):** `checkPattern`'s ConstructorPattern arm bound each pattern variable to an *orphaned* fresh type var (the code literally said *"For now, create fresh type variables for each field"*), never tied to the constructor's field type or the scrutinee's resolved type arg. So *any* destructured value — `Some(b)` from `Option[Box]`, even `Wrap(n)` from a monomorphic local ADT — lost its concrete type and could be misused (e.g. `[b]` into `[string]`), type-checking and then crashing at runtime. Fix: instantiate the constructor's registered factory scheme (`$adt.make_<T>_<C>`, whose field/result type-param vars are shared), constrain `scrutinee ~ factory.Return`, and bind each pattern arg to the real field type. Polymorphic scrutinees keep their param vars (no over-tightening). This closes a whole class of soundness holes, not just lists — a destructured value misused *anywhere* is now caught.

Verified: full `types`/`elaborate`/`pipeline`/`cmd` suites green; `make verify-examples` unchanged at baseline (181 pass / 5 pre-existing fail); **adversarial verification across 140+ programs found zero false positives** (every valid destructure — std/option/result/json, nested, recursive, polymorphic, cross-module — still compiles), A/B-confirmed.

**Round 3 — compound fields (closes the last constructor-pattern gap):** a constructor whose field *embeds* the type param in a compound type (`type Bag[a] = Bag([a])`, `(a,a)`, `Option[a]`) with an annotated scrutinee still leaked — the factory-scheme builder only remapped a field that was a *bare* type var, so a compound field kept the declared param name, disconnected from the result type's renamed var, and a destructured value escaped its concrete type. Fixed by a shared `internal/types.BuildConstructorFactoryScheme` (deduping the pipeline + REPL factory builders) that **substitutes the declared-param → result-var mapping across the entire field type** (identity on bare/concrete fields, free-var safety net). Verified: suites green, `make verify-examples` at baseline, zero false positives (valid compound-field uses + genuine polymorphism still compile).

**Round 4 — let-generalization side-condition (closes the no-constructor wrapper leak):** the residual `join`/`concat`/`++` leak — reproducible with no constructor pattern at all (`func f(b) -> string { concat([b]) }`; `f(5)` crashed in `_str_join`) — turned out **not** to be `_str_join`-specific: a plain user function `needStrs(xs: [string])` leaks identically. Root cause: a non-atomic argument like the list literal `[b]` is ANF-lifted to `let $tmp = [b] in g($tmp)`. `[b]` has element type α — the enclosing param `b`'s type, bound by the surrounding lambda — but `generalizeWithConstraints` generalized **every** free var of the let value (its own comment: *"simplified generalization … would check against environment free vars"*), so `$tmp : [α]` became `∀α. [α]`. Re-instantiating it at the use site minted a fresh element var, severing the link to `b`; `b` was never constrained and `f` over-generalized to `∀α. α -> string`, dropping the `[string]` element obligation. Fix: apply the HM generalization side-condition precisely — withhold the vars introduced by binders **inside the declaration** (`freeVars(currentEnv) \ baseEnvFreeVars`, with `baseEnvFreeVars` snapshotted at the decl's `InferWithConstraints` entry). A top-level binding still generalizes over its own vars (`map`/`zipWith` and friends unchanged); only the enclosing-lambda param vars are held back, so the use-site element constraint flows back to the real parameter. The subtraction is essential — AILANG's shared module env carries spurious "free" rigid type-parameter names (`a`, `k`, `v`, …) from other bindings' schemes, and over-broad env subtraction breaks `map`'s own generalization (occurs-check). Verified: full `types`/`elaborate`/`pipeline`/`repl`/`testing`/`cmd` suites green; `make verify-examples` at baseline (181 pass / 5 pre-existing effect-row fail); the original `concat([b])` / `join(", ", [b])` / `Some`-wrapper repros now reject at compile time, valid string-joining and polymorphic wrappers still compile. See [design_docs/planned/v0_24_0/m-type-list-element-soundness.md](../design_docs/planned/v0_24_0/m-type-list-element-soundness.md).

### Changed — Eval agent prompt delivery: kill MOVE, default the dialect-traps card on (M-EVAL-PROMPT-DELIVERY)

Local qwen3.5 agent evals were failing on **dialect** mistakes (missing imports, backtick
templates, `;` in expression bodies) that the ~22k teaching prompt already covered — a *delivery*
problem, not a content one. A controlled n=2 experiment on 3 reliably-failing benchmarks refuted
the standing hypothesis and reset two defaults in `internal/eval_harness/agent_runner_multi.go`:

- **Full-prompt persistence (`AGENTS.md`, re-injected every turn) now defaults OFF.** Re-injecting
  the full prompt every turn ("MOVE") was the *worst* delivery — **1/6 vs 3/6** for turn-1
  concatenation — because it bloated context (up to 39 turns / 2.4M tokens) and the model lost the
  signal. Re-enable for A/B with `AILANG_EVAL_PERSIST_PROMPT=1`.
- **A 30-line dialect-traps card is now front-loaded into the turn-1 message by default**
  (`prompts/agent/dialect-traps.md`). It fixed the missing-import class outright (`symbolic_diff`
  1/2 → **2/2**) and cut flailing where it didn't flip a result (`state_machine_vending` 3087k →
  1341k tokens). Disable with `AILANG_EVAL_TRAPS_CARD=off`; override the path with
  `AILANG_EVAL_TRAPS_CARD=<file>`.

Residual failures are now language-level, not delivery-level (a known match-in-block parser
interaction; an Ord/type-defaulting gap) and are tracked as follow-ups. Full write-up:
[design_docs/implemented/v0_24_0/m-eval-prompt-delivery.md](../design_docs/implemented/v0_24_0/m-eval-prompt-delivery.md).

## [v0.24.2] - 2026-06-05

### Fixed — Gated release pipeline now deploys end-to-end (M-RELEASE-GATE)

v0.24.1 published CLI binaries but its **gated deploy failed on gate-environment bugs**, so
prod stayed on 0.24.0 (prod jumps 0.24.0 → 0.24.2; 0.24.1 was a CLI-only release). Two fixes
to `cloudbuild-release.yaml`:

- **`$TAG_NAME` in the smoke gate** was eaten by Cloud Build's substitution pass (empty
  version → gate could never pass). Strip the `v` in bash (`$${TAG#v}`).
- **Unit-test gate is now hermetic** — `go test ./internal/...` (no `ailang`-binary / wasm /
  env deps) instead of full `make test`, which shelled out to the `ailang` binary absent in the
  bare gate container. Full `make test` remains in pre-release checks + dev CI.

Both bugs were surfaced by the gate **failing closed** (prod untouched) on the v0.24.1 run —
the safety property working as designed.

## [v0.24.1] - 2026-06-05

### Added — Gated release pipeline + public MCP release sync (M-RELEASE-GATE)

The public MCP docs server (`mcp.ailang.sunholo.com`) is a *separate* deployment from the CLI
release and had silently frozen at 0.19.1 for ~3 weeks (v0.20–v0.24) — agents got
`unknown_version` for new releases (reported by BlackMage). Root cause: the release Cloud Build
trigger ran a stale inline config whose agent build was missing `--build-arg PROJECT`
(`invalid reference format`), aborting every release build before test/prod could update.

- **`cloudbuild-release.yaml`** + `ailang-core-release` trigger — a gated release pipeline that
  runs on every `v*` tag: `unit tests + build → deploy test → SMOKE GATE → (only if both pass)
  crane copy test→prod → deploy prod`. **Two gates protect prod**: (1) `make test` unit tests,
  and (2) a smoke gate that verifies the **test** MCP serves the released version (`std/VERSION`
  must equal the tag), `docs_search` answers it (not `unknown_version`), and coordinator/dashboard
  `/health`. Either failing leaves prod on the last known-good revision.
- **`release.yml`** now ignores pre-release tags (`!v*-*`) so `-rc` tags don't publish CLI releases.
- **release-manager skill** documents the flow (step 7.6) with a break-glass manual prod deploy.
- Stale inline `ailang-core-test-release` trigger retired; `ailang-core-dev`/test/prod now all
  build from the maintained `cloudbuild-dev.yaml`. Design: [m-release-gate.md](../design_docs/planned/m-release-gate.md).

### Fixed — Parser hints for ML-style `match … with` and `let rec`

Targeted, actionable parse-error hints when a model writes OCaml/Haskell/F#-style syntax that
AILANG doesn't use (`match x with …`, `let rec f …`), pointing at the AILANG form. (`internal/parser/parser_expr.go`.)

### Eval harness

- **Deterministic local HTTP mock** for `api_call_json` benchmarks (M-EVAL-NETWORK-MOCK-FIXTURE)
  — removes network flakiness from agent/standard eval runs (`internal/eval_harness/httpmock.go`).
- Refreshed post-release model suites to modern OS flagships (GLM-5.1, MiniMax-M3, DeepSeek V4
  flash+pro); allow native standard mode for dual-path models; agent-harness routing fixes.
- Fixed multi-trial pass-rate denominator in `eval-suite`; nightly-eval failures now route to
  controlplane with a Discord ping.

## [v0.24.0] - 2026-06-04

### Added — Lenient XML parsing for real-world (malformed) input (M-STDLIB-XML-LENIENT)

Go's `encoding/xml` (which `std/xml.parse` wraps) is a strict XML 1.0 parser: a bare/unescaped `&` aborts the **entire** document with zero recovered content. Real-world XML — Office `content.xml` (DOCX/PPTX/XLSX/ODT), HTML5, third-party invoicing tools — routinely emits an unescaped `&` (company names, "R&D", URL query strings). Reported by sunholo/ailang-parse (docparse) on a production ODT invoice.

- **`std/xml.parseLenient(xml) -> Result[XmlNode, string]`** — repairs the input then parses. For real-world XML from non-reference generators.
- **`std/xml.sanitizeXml(xml) -> string`** — the repair step, exposed standalone so streaming parsers (`parseFold`/`parseElements`) can sanitize before their own parse call. Pure, single-pass O(n), idempotent.
- **Conservative by design**: only escapes a `&` that does **not** begin a valid entity reference (`&amp;`, `&#123;`, `&#xAB;`, `&name;` pass through untouched — never double-escaped). Strict `parse` is **unchanged** — leniency is opt-in (no silent fallback). Stray `<` recovery and unknown named-entity resolution are deliberately out of scope.
- New Go builtin `_xml_sanitize` (`internal/builtins/xml_sanitize.go`); example `examples/runnable/xml_lenient.ail`. Design: [m-stdlib-xml-lenient.md](../design_docs/planned/m-stdlib-xml-lenient.md).

### Fixed — Deterministic local HTTP mock for `api_call_json` (M-EVAL-NETWORK-MOCK-FIXTURE)

`api_call_json` was the only core benchmark making a real external network call (HTTP POST to `https://httpbin.org/post`), grading on `expected_stdout: "200"`. httpbin returns `503` intermittently: in the v0.23.0 standard run, 5 of 7 models got `503` and failed while 2 got `200` and passed — **with identical correct code**. This non-deterministic benchmark inflated GLM-5.1's leaderboard rank over the frontier models (it spuriously appeared to "beat Opus" 91% vs 84%; the real gap is ~3 points, with GLM-5.1 and gemini-3-1-pro tied ~90%). It contaminated every eval run and violated AILANG's determinism principle.

The harness now starts a local `httptest` HTTP mock (binds `127.0.0.1:0`, returns a deterministic `200` + httpbin-shaped JSON echo) for any benchmark whose `task_prompt` contains the new `{{MOCK_HTTP_URL}}` token, substitutes the live server URL into the prompt per-run, and tears it down at run end. Benchmarks set `net_allow_localhost: true` so the solution runs with `--net-allow-http --net-allow-localhost` (AILANG's Net effect blocks plain http:// and loopback by default — https-only + SSRF protection). One mock server per run (ephemeral port) keeps `--parallel N` collision-free.

Verified: all 4 previously-`503` frontier models (opus-4-8, sonnet-4-6, gpt5-5, gemini-3-1-pro) now deterministically return `200`, offline, zero httpbin references in generated code. (`internal/eval_harness/httpmock.go` + tests, `cmd/ailang/eval_benchmark.go`, `internal/eval_harness/runner.go`, `internal/eval_harness/spec.go`, `benchmarks/api_call_json.yml`.)

### Added — Per-channel event filters (Step 4 of build order, M-NOTIFY-FANOUT cont.)

Phones don't want every `✅ Task done` event — they want the things that need your attention. The fan-out now stamps each `Notification` with an `EventType` (`pending_approval`/`completed`/`failed`/`public-feedback`/`message`) and channels opt out of types they don't care about via the new `EventFilter` interface (`internal/notify/channel.go`).

- **Default Discord allow-list** (`internal/notify/discord.go`): `pending_approval`, `failed`, `public-feedback` — the "needs human attention now" subset. Routine `completed` events and generic inbox `message` events no longer ping Discord; they still land on macOS as a passive feed.
- **Override**: `DiscordChannel.SetEventTypes([]string{...})` — pass nil/empty to accept everything.
- **Fan-out semantics**: a filtered-out channel is **skipped**, not failed — it doesn't count toward the remote-authoritative ack quota and doesn't trigger a retry. So if all remote channels filter out an event, `SendAll` falls back to local best-effort and acks on macOS success.
- **Dead-lettering**: documented the Pub/Sub-native approach (`gcloud pubsub subscriptions update --dead-letter-topic=ailang-dev-dead-letter --max-delivery-attempts=5`) in [notification-channels.md](../docs/docs/guides/notification-channels.md). Subscription-level DLQ is the right Pub/Sub idiom; no in-daemon publish needed.

### Fixed — `ailang install <version>` returns an actionable error

`ailang install v0.23.0` previously failed with the opaque `invalid package name: "v0.23.0" (must be vendor/name)` — `ailang install` is for registry *packages* (`vendor/name[@version]`), not the toolchain, but nothing said so. It now detects a version-like argument and points at the real toolchain-install path (`go install github.com/sunholo-data/ailang/cmd/ailang@<tag>` or the release page), while still suggesting the package form. Surfaced by an external integrator (motoko_agent PR #33) who hit the confusing error during a v0.22→v0.23 upgrade. (`cmd/ailang/pkg_install.go` + `looksLikeToolchainVersion` unit test.)

### Fixed — Inbox messages persisted without a `MessageID`

`InsertInboxMessage` (Firestore) was not populating the `MessageID` field on the stored record, so downstream delivery/ack lookups had nothing to key on. The field is now set on insert and a daemon delivery log was added for traceability. (`internal/storage/firestore/messaging_inbox.go`, `internal/daemon/daemon.go`.)

### Eval harness — local-Ollama motoko profiles + refreshed model roster

- **Motoko local-Ollama loop** (M-MOTOKO-LOCAL-OLLAMA): `models.yml` can now set a `motoko_profile` field; the motoko executor reads `task.Metadata["motoko_profile"]` as the effective profile, enabling agent-mode eval runs against local Ollama models on the M4 Max rig. (`internal/eval_harness/models.go`, `internal/executor/motoko/motoko.go`, `internal/eval_harness/agent_runner_multi.go`.)
- **Model roster refresh**: added `claude-opus-4-8` and several OpenRouter models (GLM-4.5/Air, Kimi K2.5, DeepSeek V3.2, MiniMax M3, Qwen 3.5 35B-A3B), and removed 4 stale/regressed entries after a version-versus-reality audit. (`internal/eval_harness/models.yml`.)

### Internal — split 20 oversized files (>800 LOC)

Mechanical refactor splitting 20 files that exceeded the 800-line CI gate into focused same-package modules (no behavior change), keeping `make check-file-sizes` green. Largest: Firestore coordinator task/transition storage.

## [v0.23.0] - 2026-05-29

### Changed — Notification daemon fans out to multiple channels (M-NOTIFY-FANOUT, v0.23.0)

Build-order Step 3 — the "join". The existing laptop notification daemon (`internal/daemon`, wired in `cmd/ailang/daemon.go`) already subscribed to the `events`/`messages` Pub/Sub topics, mapped task `pending_approval`/`completed`/`failed` and inbox messages to notifications, deduped, excluded, and **nacked on failure** — but it fired to a single channel (macOS desktop). It now fans out over a `notify.Registry` (macOS + any env-gated remote channels like Discord). **Additive/backward-compatible**: with no `AILANG_DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL` set, the registry holds only macOS and behaviour is identical to before.

- **Local best-effort, remote authoritative** (`internal/notify/registry.go::SendAll` + `LocalChannel` interface, + tests). A native desktop notifier reports success the moment it *queues* a banner — even if the user is away — so letting it gate the ack would silently drop a simultaneously-failed Discord send (the channel that actually reaches the phone off-session). `MacOSChannel` declares `IsLocal()`; the fan-out fires it but never counts it toward the ack and never retries on its failure. Remote channels (Discord) are authoritative: the ack requires every remote channel to succeed, else `SendAll` returns an error and the daemon nacks. With no remote channel registered, it falls back to today's "ack if the local one delivered".
- **Retry actually retries** (`internal/daemon/dedup.go::forget` + handler nack paths, + test). The dedup recorded an event's key *before* firing, so a nack-on-failure was eaten by the dedup window on redelivery. Handlers now `forget` the key on every nack path (delivery failure, fetch error, "message not yet visible"), so Pub/Sub redelivery genuinely re-attempts — also repairing the pre-existing "not yet visible → retry" path that had the same latent bug. Successful deliveries stay deduped (no duplicate banners from Pub/Sub at-least-once).

### Added — Outbound notification channel framework + Discord webhook (M-NOTIFY-CHANNELS, v0.23.0)

Build-order Step 2 of the autonomous-triage work: a pluggable outbound-notification framework in `internal/notify`, ported from the design behind Aitana's v6 Channels Framework (`BaseChannel` + `ChannelRegistry`). It reuses the **existing** `internal/notify.Notification` (the macOS notifier's type) rather than inventing a second one, so the long-standing macOS desktop notifier becomes just one `Channel` alongside the new Discord webhook. Self-contained library this step — the daemon fan-out + per-channel routing is Step 3. See [`m-notify-channels-framework`](../design_docs/planned/v0_24_0/m-notify-channels-framework.md).

- **M1: Framework core** (`internal/notify/channel.go` + `registry.go` + tests, +197 LOC). `Channel{ Name() string; Send(ctx, Notification) error }` and a `Registry` output router (`Register`/`Get`/`Names`; idempotent on the same instance, errors on a *different* instance for the same name). `MacOSChannel` wraps the existing `Notify` path so the desktop notifier is a registered channel.
- **M2: Discord webhook + rune-safe chunker** (`internal/notify/discord.go` + `chunk.go` + tests, +231 LOC). `DiscordChannel.Send` renders a notification (bold title + subtitle/body/URL; macOS-only fields ignored) and POSTs it to the incoming webhook, chunked at Discord's 2000-char limit via `chunkMessage` (port of Aitana `_chunk.py`, never splits a multi-byte rune). Transport errors and non-2xx responses return typed errors for dead-lettering. HTTP is injected (`httpDoer`) so tests cover happy path / chunking / 500 / transport failure with zero network.
- **M3: Fail-closed registration + smoke contract + docs** (`internal/notify/register.go` + `smoke_test.go` + `docs/docs/guides/notification-channels.md`, +~120 LOC). `RegisterChannels` registers Discord only if a webhook URL is configured (logs a skip otherwise) — a fresh host with no secrets boots with zero channels. A cross-channel contract test enrolls every registered channel (non-empty `Name` matching its registry key). The how-to guide documents adding a channel in ~1–2h.
- **M3.1: Keychain-backed secret + live smoke** (`internal/notify/secret_{darwin,other}.go` + `discord_live_test.go`). The webhook URL resolves from `AILANG_DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL` first, then the macOS login Keychain (`security find-generic-password -s ailang-discord-webhook`) — so the daemon (a user LaunchAgent with Keychain access) needs no plaintext secret in its plist. Lookup is an overridable var so registration tests stay hermetic. `TestDiscordLive` (skipped unless the env var is set) performs a real send through the render→chunk→POST path; verified live against a real webhook (reached Discord mobile).
- **Deferred:** daemon fan-out over the registry + per-channel event filters + `approval_pending` mapping (Step 3); a second channel + dead-letter wiring (Step 4); inbound reply-to-approve (future).

### Added — Auto-triage router: inbound bug/feature → design-doc-creator inbox (M-MSG-TRIAGE-ROUTER, v0.23.0)

Build-order Step 1 of [`m-msg-auto-triage-pipeline`](../design_docs/planned/v0_24_0/m-msg-auto-triage-pipeline.md): connects the existing message-triage, forward, and coordinator-agent-runner pieces so a clear inbound bug/feature report is automatically promoted to the `design-doc-creator` inbox — where the existing `ApprovalWatcher` chain (design-approved → sprint-planner → sprint-executor) already takes over. The human's first touch stays at the `design-approved` label. **Opt-in**: nothing changes until `coordinator.triage.enabled: true`.

- **M1: Reusable clustering** (`internal/messaging/triage.go` + test, +138/-76 LOC). Moved the greedy envelope-slot clustering out of the private CLI helper (`cmd/ailang/messages_triage.go`) into the exported `ClusterMessages([]InboxMessage, slot string, threshold float64) []Cluster`, reusing the existing `CosineSimilarity`. The CLI `ailang messages triage` now calls the library with identical JSON output; the router (and future cluster-batching) reuse it.
- **M2: Deterministic promotion classifier** (`internal/coordinator/triage_router.go` + table tests, +212 LOC). `classify(msg, cfg) → promote/hold/drop`: a categorized bug/feature that is not a duplicate promotes regardless of sender; uncategorized messages from noise agents (e.g. `eval-suite`) drop; everything else holds for a human. `TriageConfig` (the `coordinator.triage` block) carries `Enabled`, `IntakeInboxes`, `PromoteInbox`, `PromoteCategories`, `NoiseAgents`, `ClusterSlot`, `SimilarityThreshold`, `PollIntervalSecs`, all with normalized defaults.
- **M3: TriageRouter daemon tick** (`internal/coordinator/triage_router.go` + `agent_config.go` + `daemon.go` + test, +217 LOC). Lifecycle mirrors `ApprovalWatcher` (`Start`/`Stop` + ticker `pollLoop` with an initial run). Each `tickOnce` lists unread intake-inbox messages (duplicates collapsed), classifies, and forwards promotions to the promote inbox; idempotent (a forwarded message leaves the intake listing). Wired into the daemon's local-mode startup gated by `Triage.Enabled` (off by default → zero behaviour change). Depends on a narrow `triageStore` interface for testability.
- **M4: Integration test** (`internal/coordinator/triage_router_integration_test.go`, +80 LOC). Against a real SQLite temp store: a seeded `bug` lands in `design-doc-creator`, a `general` note stays held, a `DupOf`-set message is never forwarded.
- **Deferred (noted in the design doc):** "drop = archive" and "hold = surface in dashboard" are no-ops for now; cluster-based batching of related promotions is reserved (the library exists, the tick classifies per-message). Notification fan-out so a human is *paged* off-session is build-order Steps 2–3 (the channel framework + dispatcher).

### Added / Fixed — docparse resilience: `callJsonSimpleResult` + MCP missing-param rejection (M-DOCPARSE-RESILIENCE-FIXES, v0.23.0)

Two independent robustness fixes surfaced by the docparse external project (source messages `623811a0`, `b08617cb`). Both harden the boundary between a host-supplied request and AILANG execution so a malformed/partial input returns a typed error instead of crashing deep in stdlib. Design docs: [`m-ai-call-json-simple-result`](../design_docs/implemented/v0_23_0/m-ai-call-json-simple-result.md) (M1), [`m-mcp-unit-param-binding`](../design_docs/implemented/v0_23_0/m-mcp-unit-param-binding.md) (M2).

- **M1: `std/ai.callJsonSimpleResult`** (`internal/effects/ai_step.go` + `internal/builtins/ai_step.go` + `internal/builtins/ai.go` + `std/ai.ail` + example + golden, +90 LOC). A `Result`-returning variant of `callJsonSimple` — `callJsonSimpleResult(input: string) -> Result[string, AIError] ! {AI}`. It calls `CallJson(input, "")` (empty schema, same as `callJsonSimple`) but routes provider/transport failures through the shared `classifyOpError` mapper, so a transient 5xx surfaces as `Err(AIError{retryable:true})` for the caller to back off on, rather than aborting the host. This is the retry-vs-surface pattern that bare `callJsonSimple` (which crashes on provider error) cannot express. A nil AI handler returns `Err(AIError{code:"ProviderNotFound"})`. New builtin `_ai_call_json_simple_result` added to `builtin_types.golden`; worked example at `examples/runnable/ai_call_json_simple_result.ail` verified on both the `--ai-stub` Ok path and the no-handler Err path. (Prompt teaching-list mention deferred — the active teaching prompt `v0.16.1` is hash-locked for eval reproducibility; the new variant lands in the next deliberate prompt-version bump.)

- **M2: MCP `tools/call` rejects omitted/null params before the engine** (`internal/apiserver/mcp.go::makeToolHandler`, +22 LOC). A declared tool parameter the client omitted (absent key) or sent as JSON `null` was bound to `nil`, which the engine converted to Unit; the AILANG function then crashed deep in stdlib (e.g. `_str_len: expected String, got Unit`) with no actionable message. The handler now presence-checks each declared param and returns a structured `missing required parameter(s): <names>` error (`isError: true`, names in declaration order) **before** `CallPreserveFloats`. Type-agnostic — an omitted `int` rejects identically to a `string`. The legacy positional `{"args":[...]}` form opts out of named binding and is unaffected.
  - **`@route` audit (systemic-fix rule):** confirmed NOT the same bug. The HTTP `@route` path zero-pads omitted *typed* params via `zeroValueForType` (`""`/`0`/`false`/`[]`/`{}`), never `nil`→Unit for known types — the deliberate v0.21.0 `m-serveapi-get-query-shadow` design that lets handlers self-validate partial GET/POST input. MCP rejects (RPC contract) while `@route` substitutes a typed zero (lenient HTTP) — different points on the strictness spectrum, both crash-safe. Added `TestRouteParamOmission_ZeroPadsNotNil` as a regression guard and documented both behaviors + why they diverge in [`docs/docs/guides/serve-api.md`](../docs/docs/guides/serve-api.md).

### Added — Tag-routed `messages send --requires` (M-COORD-TAG-ROUTING-LASTMILE, v0.23.0)

Closes the v0.22.0 acknowledged gap that left `ailang messages send` unable to participate in M-COORD-MULTI-HOST-WORKERS routing. Three decoupled milestones land in this entry:

- **M1: Local daemon HTTP listener on by default** (`tools/launchd/dev.ailang.coordinator.plist.template` + `tools/launchd/install_coordinator.sh` + `cmd/ailang/coordinator_lifecycle.go`, +86 LOC). The plist now sets `PORT=8765` (default; override via `make coord-install --port N` or the `AILANG_COORD_HTTP_PORT` env var, validated as an unprivileged port 1024-65535). `ailang coordinator status` gained an `HTTP: ✓ http://127.0.0.1:8765` line that probes `/health` with a 500ms timeout — surfaces "daemon up but HTTP not configured" immediately instead of users discovering it via `--requires` failing later. Pre-v0.23.0 installs need a one-time `make coord-install` to pick up the new default.

- **M2: `ailang messages send --requires` flag** (`cmd/ailang/messages_send.go` + new `messages_send_test.go`, +274 LOC). The flag accepts comma-separated worker tags (`--requires agent:motoko,ollama:gemma4`) and routes via the daemon's HTTP `/api/messages` endpoint, which carries the tags to Pub/Sub attributes for tag-subset filtering by workers (the v0.22.0 mechanism). Without `--requires`, behavior is unchanged from v0.22.0 — the SQLite-only path stays the default for inbox-addressed fire-and-forget. New `sendViaHTTP` helper reuses M1's `discoverCoordinatorHTTPPort` (env → plist) for port discovery; clear actionable error when the listener isn't reachable (suggests `make coord-install`). 4 deterministic tests at `count=20`: splitAndTrim parser (8 cases), POST body shape, API key Bearer header, no-PORT failure mode.

- **M3: In-repo half of the cloud-fallback Job** (`cloudbuild-dev.yaml` + `docker/agent-motoko-multivac-prs.md`, +135/-6 LOC). Adds the `build-agent-motoko` step to `cloudbuild-dev.yaml` mirroring `build-agent-go` (registry-cached buildx, FROMs agent-base via the existing `Dockerfile.agent-motoko`). The cross-repo work in `ailang-multivac` (Terraform Job definition, mounted coordinator config update with `worker_tags: [agent:motoko]`, prod `ailang-openrouter-api-key` secret) is documented as PRs #0-#3 in `docker/agent-motoko-multivac-prs.md` — the doc was originally drafted during M-MOTOKO-EXECUTOR-ADAPTER (v0.18.0) but never executed; v0.23.0 refreshes it with the M-COORD-MULTI-HOST-WORKERS integration points and a NEW PR #0 (operational, pre-flight) noting that the cloud `ailang-coordinator` Cloud Run service is on a 2026-04-28 image (pre-v0.21.0) and MUST be redeployed before any E2E test of the v0.22.0 `requires` field can succeed.

- **Docs**: [`docs/docs/guides/coordinator-workers.md`](../docs/docs/guides/coordinator-workers.md) gained a "HTTP endpoint configuration" subsection (default port, override paths, /health probe, route catalog with auth requirements). [`docs/docs/guides/agent-messaging.md`](../docs/docs/guides/agent-messaging.md) gained a "Tag-routed sends (v0.23.0+)" subsection with concrete `--requires` examples + prerequisites callout.

**End-to-end verification matrix** (M4 of the sprint):

| # | Scenario | Status | Gating |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Studio → Studio (local tag match): `ailang messages send eval-rig "x" --requires agent:motoko` from Studio shell lands locally | ⚠️ Partial — HTTP send path verified (M2 live smoke); end-to-end claim-and-dispatch requires the local dispatcher to honor `requires` for executor selection (currently it claims tag-routed messages but dispatches via the agent's `provider` field, not the message's `requires`). The CLI + transport are working; the dispatcher-side `requires →executor` mapping is a follow-up. | Follow-up sprint |
| 2 | Laptop → cloud coordinator → Studio (Pub/Sub routing): same message from a non-Studio host hitting the cloud coordinator URL | ⏸️ Deferred — gated on PR #0 (cloud coordinator redeploy from 2026-04-28 image to v0.22.0+) | PR #0 in `docker/agent-motoko-multivac-prs.md` |
| 3 | Cloud-fallback (Studio offline): with `launchctl bootout` first, message claimed by `ailang-agent-executor-motoko` Cloud Run Job | ⏸️ Deferred — gated on PRs #1 (cloudbuild image build), #2 (Terraform Job + coordinator config addendum) in `ailang-multivac` repo | PRs #1+#2 in `docker/agent-motoko-multivac-prs.md` |

The local-side surface (M1+M2) is feature-complete and ships in v0.23.0. The cloud-side dispatch (M3.x in ailang-multivac) is documented but lives in a separate repo and is deferred until that PR set is opened + merged.


## [v0.22.0] - 2026-05-26

### Changed — Retired Gemini CLI, adopted Vertex Managed Agents API (M-MANAGED-AGENTS, v0.22.0)

Google announced Gemini CLI deprecation at I/O 2026 (forced-retirement deadline 2026-06-18). Antigravity CLI was evaluated as the successor but was unsuitable for our eval harness (OAuth-only, no `--model` flag, no streaming JSON output, heavy gRPC server boot per invocation, pollutes working directories with `.antigravitycli/` symlinks). **Vertex AI Managed Agents API** is the chosen replacement: ADC-native, server-side multi-turn state, native SSE streaming.

- **Deleted `internal/executor/gemini/`** — Gemini CLI executor removed in full. Config-load now produces a clear next-step error if any existing `~/.ailang/config.yaml` still has `agent_cli: "gemini"`, pointing at `managed_agents` for `gemini-3-5-flash` agent-mode and noting that older Gemini models are now standard-mode-only via `internal/ai/gemini/` (unchanged).
- **Added `internal/executor/managed_agents/`** (~1255 LOC across client, SSE parser, types, tests): HTTP client targeting `aiplatform.googleapis.com/v1beta1/projects/<p>/locations/global/interactions` with ADC auth via `golang.org/x/oauth2/google` (same path `internal/ai/gemini/client.go` uses). SSE parser uses a forward-compat unknown-event map (mirroring motoko's pattern) so new event types from the API are captured into `ProviderData` without code changes. Multi-turn via server-side `interaction.id` + `environment_id` — no client-side history accumulation, big win for token cost. New `executor.CapRemoteSandbox` capability flag signals isolated-sandbox executors to the eval harness, which uses the existing `internal/eval_harness/managed_agents_bridge.go` to bridge solution files via the agent's text response.
- **`models.yml` rewired**: 8 Gemini entries lost their `agent_cli: "gemini"`. `gemini-3-5-flash` gained `agent_cli: "managed_agents"` with `agent_model_name: "antigravity-preview-05-2026"` (model is implicit in the API; this names the agent identity). Other Gemini models stay standard-mode-only.
- **`ailang doctor managed_agents` subcommand** (followup) wires the new executor into the existing doctor flow — checks ADC, prints next-step setup commands, probes a no-op interaction to confirm provisioning.
- **Post-hoc cost-budget reporting** (followup, `baa9ce0f`): cost-budget enforcement for managed_agents flags overruns in the run report rather than aborting mid-stream (the API only returns total cost in the terminal `interaction.completed` event — there's no per-token check point to gate against during the stream).
- **Lesson encoded for future**: when Google ships a new GCP service, **probe `v1beta1` first** — that's the Vertex AI canonical version prefix for new APIs, not `v1` or `v1beta`. Saved as `feedback_managed_agents_v1beta1_path.md`. Reference snapshot of the API surface in `reference_managed_agents_api.md`.
- **Docs**: [docs/docs/guides/evaluation/harness-setup.md](../docs/docs/guides/evaluation/harness-setup.md) — Gemini CLI section removed, Managed Agents API setup added (`gcloud auth application-default login`, project/location, one-time provisioning note). `.claude/rules/coordinator.md` executor table refreshed.
- **Design doc**: [m-antigravity-cli-migration.md](../design_docs/implemented/v0_22_0/m-antigravity-cli-migration.md) (filename is historical from the initial Antigravity-CLI direction; the implemented work is the Managed Agents API pivot documented inside).

### Operational — motoko_agent restored on AILANG v0.21+ (M-MOTOKO-AILANG-RECONCILE + M-MOTOKO-V021-EFFECT-ROW-MIGRATION, v0.22.0)

Two-sprint reconciliation to make `agent:motoko` a verified-working worker tag (M-COORD-MULTI-HOST-WORKERS, v0.22.0) rather than aspirational. All code work landed in `arniwesth/motoko_agent` (via `sunholo-voight-kampff` fork) and `sunholo-data/ailang-packages`; this repo gets the iface fix (above) plus this docs update.

- **M-MOTOKO-AILANG-RECONCILE** (2026-05-26): migrated `motoko_agent/src/core/ai_compat.ail` from the v0.20-era `std/ai/streaming.callStream` (a synchronous-final accumulator) to v0.21's `std/ai.stepWithStream` (callback-based real streaming). Public `callStreamResult` signature and `AIStreamResult` return shape unchanged — the change is internal. Bumped all 12 `motoko_ext_*` packages in `ailang.toml` to AILANG v0.21+ compatible versions. Republished `motoko-ext-ai-compat@0.2.0` (effect ceiling widened to `[AI IO Process FS Env Net SharedMem Clock Stream]` matching std/ai's post-v0.21 surface) and `motoko-ext-compose@0.2.3`.
- **M-MOTOKO-V021-EFFECT-ROW-MIGRATION** (2026-05-26): surfaced the AILANG iface nested-effect-rows regression above. Shipped a motoko-side workaround (extract on_chunk's match block to a top-level `handle_stream_chunk` function) plus defensive type annotations on `agent_loop_v2.ail`'s on_chunk closure and `stub_step.ail`'s Scripted branch. `make check_core` now reports 23/23 passing; `make build` exits 0.
- **Pinned motoko revision updated** ([`internal/executor/motoko/README.md`](../internal/executor/motoko/README.md)): now tracks commit `f7b26c8` on `feature/v021-effect-row-migration`; declares AILANG version floor of **v0.22.0+** (the iface fix is required).
- **Published**: `motoko-ext-ai-compat@0.2.1` and `motoko-ext-compose@0.2.4` (repins ai_compat path-dep against 0.2.1). 0.2.1 ships the `handle_stream_chunk` workaround in the registry source. The live registry validator picked up the iface fix automatically when dev was pushed (validator reports `v0.21.0-102-g9e405913` — auto-rebuild on dev push, not via the `release.yml` tag-trigger path).
- **Filed for follow-up**: a second AILANG type-checker regression where a `match` expression inside a lambda with at least one pure branch (`Usage(_) => ()`) erases the lambda's inferred effect row to `{}`. Top-level function decls correctly honor `! {IO}` annotations; this is specific to inferred lambdas. Workaround is the same `handle_stream_chunk` extraction pattern shipped in this sprint. Not blocking, but should be fixed before the next AILANG release closes the gap with cleaner ai_compat source.

### Fixed — Iface dropped nested function-type effect rows (M-IFACE-NESTED-EFFECTS, v0.22.0)

When a top-level function declared a parameter that was itself a function type with an effect annotation (e.g. `on_chunk: (StreamChunk) -> () ! {IO}`), the typechecker erased the nested effect row during unification. The iface builder did not restore it, so importing modules saw `(StreamChunk) -> ()` (closed empty effect). Any caller passing a lambda that actually used effects then failed to unify with `"incompatible closed rows: r1 has extra labels [], r2 has extra labels [IO]"`.

The fix extends `applyLabelsFromAST` in `internal/iface/builder.go` with a new helper `restoreNestedEffectRow` that walks the AST and re-applies effect rows to nested function-typed positions whenever the AST has an explicit `! {...}` annotation. Typechecker-inferred rows (where the AST has no annotation) are preserved unchanged, keeping row-polymorphic callbacks intact.

Discovered during M-MOTOKO-V021-EFFECT-ROW-MIGRATION while migrating `motoko_agent` to AILANG v0.21+. The bug had been masked in the v0.21.0 era because a SECOND latent bug (lambda+match-with-pure-branch effect inference) cancelled it out — both bugs together produced `{} = {}` which trivially unified. Fixing only the iface bug surfaces the lambda-effect bug as a separate failure mode; the lambda-effect bug is filed for follow-up. Regression tests in `internal/iface/nested_effects_test.go`.

### Added — Multi-host worker routing (M-COORD-MULTI-HOST-WORKERS, v0.22.0)

The coordinator + Pub/Sub system can now route messages to specific hardware (e.g. "this task needs `ollama:gemma4-26b-ailang`, route to the Mac Studio rig") without adding any new transport. Five small additions on top of the existing M-PUBSUB-MESSAGING + M-CLOUD-DISPATCH foundation:

- **Worker identity + tags** (`internal/coordinator/agent_registry.go` + new `tag_matcher.go`, ~245 LOC): `AgentConfig` gains `WorkerTags []string` and `WorkerHostID string`. Defaults preserve all existing behavior (empty tags = match-all, empty host_id = `os.Hostname()`). Pure `TagMatches(required, advertised)` with set-inclusion + prefix-glob (`ollama:*`) semantics. Named "worker_tags" deliberately to avoid collision with AILANG's `--caps` effect-system capabilities flag.
- **Pub/Sub subscription tag filter** (`internal/coordinator/pubsub_adapter.go` + `internal/pubsub/topics.go`, ~165 LOC): `PubSubInboxAdapter.SetWorkerTags()` enables tag-subset filtering of incoming messages. Mismatches return a non-nil error from `HandleNotification` (Pub/Sub treats as NACK → redelivers to next worker) BEFORE the Firestore fetch — efficiency: don't pay the round-trip for messages we'll reject. `pubsub.MessageAttributes.Requires` field encodes as comma-separated `requires` attribute. `POST /api/messages` accepts a `requires` array and threads it through.
- **Heartbeat + unified `workers` CLI** (`internal/coordinator/heartbeat.go`, `cmd/ailang/coordinator_workers.go`, ~445 LOC): `WorkerHeartbeat` struct + `HeartbeatStore` interface + `MemoryHeartbeatStore` + goroutine writer with configurable interval (60s default). `ailang coordinator workers list` shows BOTH bare-metal hosts (live heartbeats) AND Cloud Run history (aggregated from the existing coordinator task store by `AgentID`). `--type bare-metal|cloud-run`, `--since`, `--max-age`, `--json` filters. `workers ping <host>` lookup with a clear placeholder for the full Pub/Sub round-trip.
- **launchd + onboarding** (`tools/launchd/dev.ailang.coordinator.plist.template` + `install_coordinator.sh` + `make/coord.mk`, ~360 LOC): `make coord-install TAGS="..." HOST_ID="..."` installs an idempotent LaunchAgent that survives reboots. `--dry-run` shows planned changes including the rendered plist. `--uninstall` cleanly removes. Backs up existing plist to `.bak` on replace. Does NOT auto-edit `~/.ailang/config.yaml` — prints the exact YAML block to add so the user keeps full control.
- **Docs** (`docs/docs/guides/coordinator-workers.md`, ~210 lines): concept guide with architecture diagram + 3 worked examples (Studio onboarding, tag-routed send, health visibility).

Test surface: 75 new sub-tests across tag matcher (26), Pub/Sub adapter filter (10), Pub/Sub attribute encoding (10), agent config YAML round-trip (2), heartbeat machinery (7). All existing tests still pass.

The work was motivated by the 2026-05-22 Tailscale outage (38 hours of Studio unreachability went undetected). With heartbeats, `workers list --json | jq '.[] | select(.alive == false)'` is a one-liner monitoring hook.

Cumulative commits: `2f51868c` (M1), `d1b71321` (M2), `de710a65` (M3), `765b83ef` (M4), `<this commit>` (M5).

### Added — 24/7 eval-rig infra reliability hardening (M-EVAL-OS-LONGITUDINAL M0)

Three pieces shipped together to make the local-Ollama overnight rotation actually run for 12+ hours unattended without rotting. Background: a 2h 15m overnight run on 2026-05-23 produced zero useful data — observatory.db-wal had ballooned to 5.4 GB and all 17 benchmarks TTFT-timed out at 0 tokens. Root cause + fixes:

- **Opencode noise filter** (`internal/observatory/otlp_receiver.go`): extended `DefaultSpanFilterConfig.DenyPatterns` with service-scoped patterns for opencode internals (`Plugin.trigger`, `Config.get`, `SyncEvent.run`, `FileSystem.readJson`, `Auth.all`, `Session.updatePart`, `SessionProcessor.readToolCall`, etc.) — opencode-via-`@effect/opentelemetry` was emitting ~33 internal spans per `LLM.run` span, drowning the useful signal. Useful spans (`LLM.run`, `opencode.execute`, `opencode.step`) deliberately kept. Service-scoped so non-opencode spans of the same name (e.g. ailang-server's legitimate `FileSystem.*` ops) are NOT filtered. Regression coverage in `internal/observatory/otlp_receiver_filter_test.go` (8 new sub-cases covering deny + explicit "kept" + non-opencode "not filtered").
- **WAL auto-checkpoint** (`internal/observatory/wal_maintenance.go`, new ~95 LOC): `MaybeCheckpointWAL()` inspects the `.db-wal` size and runs `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` when above the configured threshold (default 1024 MB, override via `AILANG_OBSERVATORY_WAL_CHECKPOINT_MB`). Non-destructive — merges committed WAL pages into the main DB.
